### Chapter 7. On God the Holy Spirit — De Spiritu Sancto¹⁰⁸

*(pp. 233–258 of the imprint.)*

#### Refutation of the Argument that the Son and Holy Spirit are "Less than the Father"

**Certain objectors set forth an argument** by which they wish to prove that the ***Holy Spirit is less than the Father and the Son*** — namely thus:

> ***That which proceeds from another, is less than the one from whom it proceeds.***
> ***But the Holy Spirit proceeds from the Father and the Son.***
> ***Therefore [the Spirit] is less than the Father and the Son.***

And the same argument is also transferred to the ***Son:***

> ***The begotten is less than the begetter.***
> ***The Son is begotten.***
> ***Therefore [the Son] is less than the Father.***

**We answer**: The ***works ad intra*** do not make inequality of essence, power, and glory, but discern the Persons who are in one essence. And nevertheless it is and remains true that ***the Father is the principle and cause*** — that is, that ***the Father is from no one, the Son is from the Father, and the Holy Spirit is from both*** — ***from the beginning always, and without end,*** as pious antiquity has spoken. For just as ***from the Sun proceeds light, from the fountain the river is derived, from fire heat emanates,*** and these in an inseparable conjunction remain conjoined one to another: so (if it is permitted to compare with divine things those which can be comprehended by human senses) ***the Son is produced from the Father, from whom He is begotten, and the Holy Spirit proceeds from the Father and the Son*** — the one essence of Divinity all the while remaining one.¹⁰⁸ᵃ

#### That the Son is ὁμοούσιος with the Father

**The Son is ὁμοούσιος (consubstantial) with the Father and the Holy Spirit,** according to that of **John 10:** ***"I and the Father are one."*** And **John 14:** ***"Believe me, that I am in the Father, and the Father in me."*** And Paul in **Philippians 2** says: ***"He did not deem it robbery to be equal with God"*** (*ἁρπαγμὸν οὐχ ἡγήσατο τὸ εἶναι ἴσα Θεῷ* — as Basil most rightly interprets these voices apt for deceiving heretics, and adds: for this the Pauline infinitive-sentence intends, *τὸ εἶναι ἴσα, non ἴσος*). And this let us hold ***against the Arians, Sabellians, Metagimonites*** or ***transaquationists*** (who feigning ***metagismos,*** that is, ***transaquation,*** said that the Son is in the Father as a vessel is in a vessel), and against others — especially in our petulant age, in many ways contumelious both against God and against men.

#### That the Holy Spirit is ὁμοούσιος with the Father and Son

**The Holy Spirit likewise is ὁμοούσιος with the Father and the Son,** because thus the Divinity has manifested itself in creation and in the baptism of Christ, and this is the command of the Son of God: that we should render the same honour of worship, veneration, and invocation to the Holy Spirit which we render to the Father and to the Son — as the manifest and unmistakable ***form of Baptism*** delivered and prescribed by the Son of God Himself attests. For we all — one and all — have been baptised to this end: that we should acknowledge, celebrate, and invoke ***God the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit.*** He who does not do this, or pertinaciously disputes whether he ought to do it, ***does not yet know the true God,*** and has not yet considered the end of his own Baptism, nor does he understand what is the sentence of the command: ***"Baptise all in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit."*** With Basil we rightly think and say: ***"It behoves us to be baptised, as we have received; to believe, however, as we are baptised; and to glorify, as we believe — the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit."***¹⁰⁸ᵇ

Some say they have nowhere read that the Holy Spirit is [***of the same substance***] with the Father and the Son. But what ignorance can there be, if not this? **Paul says: *"The Spirit is Jehovah."*** The Son of God commands that the Holy Spirit be invoked in the same way as the Father and the Son. In the Symbol we say: ***"I believe in the Holy Spirit, LORD"*** — that is, ***Jehovah.*** We have the manifestation of the whole Divinity in Christ's Baptism, and at Pentecost. We feel consolation, faith, and other good motions in us — excited by the Holy Spirit. We have been baptised to the acknowledgement, veneration, and invocation of the Holy Spirit as much as of the Father and the Son. There is also this known and true rule: ***Whatever is outside the Person of the Father, and outside the Person of the Son, in which is the divine nature — this must necessarily be a peculiar Person, and must be true God.*** The Holy Spirit is outside the Person of the Father and the Son (as the manifestation in Christ's Baptism and the form of Baptism attests), and in Him is the divine nature, as it is written [***"The Spirit is the Lord"***]. Therefore it is necessary that the Holy Spirit be God. We know moreover that blasphemers are divinely punished, and that the sin committed against the Holy Spirit is not remitted. Yet with all these things (as it were) consigned to oblivion, there are found many perjurers, rash, audacious, petulant, pertinacious, and blasphemous men, who prefer to obey their own proud and foolish reason (although every proud man is truly a fool, and becomes at length utterly stupid) than to subject themselves to the Word and manifestation of God. Let these depart, and receive their wages — which shall be the fire of eternal damnation — and let them dismiss us who acknowledge, confess, preach, and invoke the ***Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit.***¹⁰⁸ᶜ

#### The Response to the Argument on Procession

**Nevertheless the solution of this argument also is to be retained.**

> ***Procession is attributed both to the Son and to the Holy Spirit,*** since the Son says of Himself in **John 8:** ***"I proceeded and came forth from God."*** And **Micah 5** it is written: ***"Out of Bethlehem shall come forth a Ruler in Israel, whose goings-forth are from the days of the world."***
> ***Therefore to proceed is not proper to the Holy Spirit.***

**I answer to the antecedent.** ***Only the Holy Spirit [is called ἐκπορευόμενον = proceeding by spiration]*** — for *ναχ* (*nachah*) is agitation and motion. From ***Naphah,*** ***by breathing out,*** He proceeded. And the word ***Naphah*** is also used in the Syrian Testament of the Holy Spirit — ***the Holy Spirit proceeds as agitator and gladdener***: therefore, since His going-forth or procession is said to happen (for we must speak as babes and sucklings) ***from a willing power.*** But the Son of God [proceeds] not in that way in which the Holy Spirit [proceeds]. Therefore He says (John 8): ***"I proceeded from God"*** — ***lo za,*** *yatsati.* And the terms *γεννηθῆναι* (to be born) and *ἐκπορεύεσθαι* (to proceed) differ: this latter is ***from the will;*** that is ***from the thought-power.*** And the word ***going-forth*** — Hebrew ***yatsa*** — is more general than ***procession.*** And when it is used of the Son, it must be declared through the words ***to be born, to be begotten, to be sent.*** The Son goes forth ***as begotten.*** His goings-forth are ***from the days of the world.*** Here is understood the ***eternal generation.*** ***"He shall go forth from Bethlehem."*** ***"I went forth from God."*** Here is understood the ***mission for teaching***, and for accomplishing all things which pertain to the redemption and salvation of the human race. ***"I,"*** says the Son of God, ***"by the authority and command of the Father do what I do — by teaching, by doing miracles, by obeying, by suffering, by satisfying,"*** etc. The same is expressed in the accustomed form: ***"The Son acts through Himself, but not from Himself."***

#### The Filioque Established from John 16

**That the Holy Spirit proceeds from the Son equally as from the Father,** testimonies clearer than the light of noonday convince — **John 16:** manifestly the Son says: ***"When I shall have gone, I will send Him to you."*** Since therefore the Holy Spirit is sent equally by the Son as by the Father, it is necessary that He proceed from both — as also there the Son of God manifestly attests, saying: ***"He shall receive of mine."*** And: ***"All things which the Father hath, are mine."*** And: ***"He shall not speak of Himself."*** These words most evidently show: **(1)** ***the Son is [ὁμοούσιος] with the Father;*** **(2)** ***the Holy Spirit is a Person distinct from the Father and the Son;*** **(3)** ***and [He] proceeds from both.***¹⁰⁸ᵈ

#### The Grammatical Argument from ἐκεῖνος

To the younger [students] I also wish that the ***Syntax*** be considered: [***ἐκεῖνος***], John 16. For this Syntax against the ***Arians and Pneumatomachi*** (both ancient and recent), even by the grammatical judgement of children, attests that the Holy Spirit is a ***Person,*** and is ***not the Father Himself agitating, nor an agitation created by the Father***: for otherwise the Apostle would have used the neuter gender, [***ἐκεῖνο***]. The same is done also at chapter 14 of John: [***ὁ Παράκλητος... ἐκεῖνος...***]. And Paul at Ephesians 1: ***"You have been sealed [by the Holy Spirit of promise, who — ὅς — is the earnest of our inheritance]."***¹⁰⁸ᵉ

Diligently also is this to be observed: that ***the Holy Spirit is called now the Spirit of the Father, now the Spirit of the Son.*** Rightly therefore **Augustine,** in his 99th tract on John, speaks to this sense: ***"Since the Holy Spirit is both of the Father and of the Son, He proceeds also from both."*** For **Matthew 10** it is written: ***"The Spirit of the Father speaketh in you."*** And **Galatians 4:** ***"God hath sent forth the Spirit of His Son into your hearts, crying Abba, Father."*** And **Romans 8:** ***"If any man have not the Spirit of Christ, he is none of His."*** In **John 20** the Son breathes the Holy Spirit upon the Apostles. **"If, however"** (says Augustine) ***"He did not proceed from Him"*** (namely the Holy Spirit from the Son), He would not, after His resurrection, when representing Himself to His disciples, have said, breathing upon them, ***"Receive the Holy Spirit."*** For what else did that inbreathing declare, but that the Holy Spirit proceeds also from Him? And the word ***"He breathed" (ἐνεφύσησεν)*** is exceedingly emphatic. For it shows that Christ is truly and by nature God, because Christ by His own breath gives the Holy Spirit from Himself — which is proper to the work of God. It shows moreover that the Holy Spirit is the ***third Person of the Divinity,*** and proceeds ***as much from the Son as from the Father.*** Afterward it teaches what sort the Holy Spirit is — namely, ***the breath, or the ardour of love between the Father and the Son,*** by which the Father and the Son are mutually complete, and by which they love the Angels also, and men.¹⁰⁸ᶠ

**These things must be held against certain Greeks and Armenians,** who have contended that the Holy Spirit does not proceed from the Son, but only from the Father — which without doubt was first stirred up and agitated by the ***Arians,*** who, affirming that the Son is not [***ὁμοούσιος***] with the Father, [affirmed] also that the Holy Spirit does not equally proceed from the Son as from the Father. And ***Raphael Volaterranus*** reports in the lives of Eugenius and Gregory X that the Greeks thirteen times came to agreement with the Latins concerning controverted dogmas — especially concerning the procession of the Holy Spirit — and as often defected again: which is horrible to read and hear. They brought forth these words of Christ, **John 15:** ***"The Spirit proceedeth from the Father."*** Therefore, they said, He does not proceed from the Son. This is certainly frivolous and plainly impious — especially since those things which are the Father's are also the Son's, and the Son in His humiliation speaks humbly, and does not seize equality with God, but ascribes all things to the Father, and yet does not thereby exclude Himself, especially when in that very place He says: ***"I will send to you the Paraclete from the Father."*** Therefore the Holy Spirit proceeds also ***from the Son,*** as is manifest from what we have said.¹⁰⁸ᵍ

#### Patristic Testimonies for the Filioque

**Let there also be ready to hand certain testimonies of the ancient Church** — of which we shall recite a few:

**Basil the Great,** *De Spiritu Sancto,* chapter 18: ***"Nay, He is called Christ's Spirit, as being by nature joined to Him: for if any man have not Christ's Spirit, he is none of His."*** — Basil has (that I may say this in passing) many splendid things on the Holy Spirit, as in **Book 3 *Against Eunomius,*** who had feigned that the Holy Spirit is ***third from the Father and the Son*** in nature, or has a ***third and alien nature,*** and is not ***of the same nature or essence with the Father and the Son*** — although the form of Baptism delivered by Christ [***ὁμοτίμως***] affirms that the Holy Spirit is [equal in honour]. And in **Isaiah 6** the Seraphim three times cry ***Holy,*** in order that the natural Sanctity in the three ***hypostases*** may be honoured. And the voice of Basil is worthy of consideration: ***"Baptism is the seal of faith, and faith is the confession of Deity, according to the form of Baptism: In the name of the Father, of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit."*** And to Amphilochius against the Eunomians Basil writes most gravely of the Holy Spirit, showing that the Holy Spirit is Creator, life-giver of all things together with the Father and the Son, that He remits sins, that He has the same power with the Father and the Son, that He is Renewer and Perfecter of all things which through the Son have been made by God, that He is glorified with the Father and the Son, that He dwells in the hearts of the pious, that He is [***ὁμότιμος***] and inseparable from the Father and the Son — both in creation, and in the conservation and dispensation of things, and also in the future judgement and in all eternity — as ***eternal God*** and ***Jehovah the Lord.*** For thus says Basil in *De Spiritu Sancto,* chapter 21: ***"We find in the Apostle, 2 Thessalonians 3: 'May the Lord direct your hearts unto the love of God, and unto the patience of Christ.' Who is the Lord who leads into the love of God, and into the endurance of the afflictions of Christ? For if it be said of the Father, it were needful to speak thus: 'May the Lord direct you into the love of Himself'; if of the Son: 'into His own endurance.' Let us therefore inquire what other Person it is, whom it is fitting to honour with the appellation of Lord."*** Item, ***2 Corinthians 3:*** ***"The Spirit is the Lord."*** And: ***"as by the Lord Spirit,"*** etc.¹⁰⁸ʰ

Basil alleges also the testimonies of many whose authority was pre-eminent in the Church before his age — which it is useful to remember. For the pious are confirmed when they hear that the doctrine has been delivered to the true and purer Church by certain testimonies — as concerning hearing the Church it is written **Judges 14:** ***"If ye had not ploughed with my heifer, ye would not have found out."*** And splendidly says Basil: [***"Venerable are the ancient dogmas, which by antiquity as a certain hoariness, hold something to be reverenced."***] Basil cites moreover **Clement of Rome,** third Bishop in the year of Christ 94, saying: ***"God liveth, and the Lord Jesus Christ, and the Holy Spirit."*** And **Irenaeus,** near to the times of the Apostles; and **Dionysius of Alexandria,** disciple of Origen; and **Eusebius of Palestine,** whose words are these: [***"θεοπρόσδεκτος, ὁμότιμος τῷ Πατρὶ καὶ τῷ Υἱῷ"***]; and **Origen;** and **Gregory the Great, of Neocaesarea** [Thaumaturgus, wonder-worker], and ***the other Moses.***¹⁰⁸ⁱ

But of Basil's judgement and piety enough. Let us now hear the testimonies of others on the procession of the Holy Spirit from the Father and the Son.

**Athanasius,** who is by Theodoret named the ***bulwark of truth,*** in the Symbol issued by him — which is thought to have been written in the year of Christ 372 — says: ***"The Holy Spirit is from the Father and the Son, not made, not created, not begotten, but proceeding."***

**Didymus of Alexandria,** *De Spiritu Sancto,* book 2, on **John 16:** ***"He shall not speak of Himself"*** — that is, ***not without me and the Father's will,*** since He is inseparable from my will and the Father's. Because He is not from Himself, but is from the Father and from me. ***"I speak truth"*** — that is, I inspire what He speaks. Since indeed He is the Spirit of truth, etc. — This Didymus was blind from earliest age, and named the ***wonder of the world*** for his erudition; and he wrote much against the Arians and on the Holy Spirit in the year of Christ 395. He was called by Jerome ***the seeing Prophet.***¹⁰⁸ʲ

**Cyril,** on John, book 10, chapter 41: ***"When the Lord had predicted that the Paraclete was to come to them, He named Him the Spirit of Truth (for He Himself is Truth), that the disciples might know that they were not to receive some alien power. Therefore in a certain manner He promises Himself to them — the Spirit of Truth, that is, His own — being about to send. For the Spirit is not alien to the substance of the Son, but proceeds naturally from that nature, altogether the same in nature, but truly subsisting in His own proper Person."*** To the same effect, in the letter in the name of the Council of Ephesus written under Theodosius through Cyril, these words are read: ***"Although the Holy Spirit be in His own substance, and is understood in the propriety of Person, according to that which He is Spirit and not Son, nevertheless He is not alien from Him. For the Spirit is called of Truth: and Truth is Christ. Whence also from Him He similarly proceeds, as from God the Father."*** 

In the *Thesaurus,* the same Cyril, book 13, chapter 1, says: ***"Since the Son is Life according to nature, and the Holy Spirit also, whom He Himself bestows, gives life: it must altogether be confessed that the Holy Spirit is of the substance of the Father and the Son, and has His whole power and operation from them."***

**Augustine,** *De Trinitate,* book 5, chapter 11: ***"The Spirit is both of the Father and of the Son. He is the Gift of the Father and the Son, because He proceeds from the Father and from the Son."***

**Hilary,** *De Trinitate,* book 8: ***"Since by the nature of God nature itself dwells in us, [our] indistinct nature will be believed to be from the Father, since the Holy Spirit, who is both the Spirit of Christ and the Spirit of God, is demonstrated to be a thing of one nature."***

**The Confession of the Lateran Council, in the year of Christ 1204** [i.e., IV Lateran 1215]: ***"Firmly we believe and simply confess, that there is one and only one true God, eternal, immense, unchangeable, omnipotent, incomprehensible and ineffable — Father, and Son, and Holy Spirit. Three Persons indeed, but one essence or nature, altogether simple: the Father from no one, the Son from the Father alone, and the Holy Spirit equally from both, from the beginning, always, and without end. The Father begetting, the Son being born, and the Holy Spirit proceeding — consubstantial, co-omnipotent, and co-eternal."***¹⁰⁸ᵏ

With these few testimonies we would now be content — beseeching God to govern us in simplicity and truth, to defend and conserve us, that we may truly invoke, worship, and behold Him propitious in all eternity — ***God the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit — one, only, true, living, omnipotent, just, immortal, most-clement God, whose is all praise, honour, victory, power, and universal and sempiternal reign.***

#### Was the Holy Spirit Before the Incarnation?

**With these things indicated,** it is now no longer needful to move that customary question — namely, whether the Holy Spirit ***was before the Son of God took on flesh.*** For since the Holy Spirit is ***true and eternal God together with the Father and the Son,*** as up to this point we have shown, there is no need of further inquiry. The Spirit of Christ was ***in the Prophets*** (1 Peter 1; Isaiah 59: ***"My Spirit, which is in thee"***); ***Zechariah 7;*** the Holy Spirit led Moses through the Red Sea (Isaiah 64). And the Son of God Himself was ***in the flesh, of Mary the Virgin, conceived by the Holy Spirit.*** Before the incarnation of the Son, therefore, it is necessary that the Holy Spirit have been.¹⁰⁸ˡ

#### On John 7: "The Holy Spirit was not yet given"

**What is inquired of the saying of John 7** — [***"the Spirit was not yet given"***] — it is certain that this must be received of the ***manifest and visible display*** of the Holy Spirit, whom the Son of God after His assumption into heaven and glorification had promised to send — as the preceding words manifestly show: ***"Rivers of living water shall flow from his belly who believeth in me": this He said of the Spirit, whom they that believed on Him should receive. For the Holy Spirit was not yet [given]*** — namely, in such fullness, and with a visible sign, because Christ was not yet glorified. For the Evangelist does not mean that the Person of the Holy Spirit did not exist — whom we know to be ***eternal*** — or that the Holy Spirit was not efficacious in the Church before the glorification of Christ; for the Patriarchs and Prophets, the pious, all in the Old Testament ***received the grace of the Holy Spirit,*** and the Apostles also — and believers even before Christ's ascension into heaven — were not without the Holy Spirit. For whence is faith, save from the Spirit? The Evangelist therefore speaks ***comparatively,*** as when the New Testament is opposed to the Old — and says that the ***manifest and full exhibition of the Spirit*** is deferred to the time of glorification, that is, to Christ risen and reigning in glory. And indeed it appears that in the primitive Church in the time of the Apostles the appellation of the Holy Spirit was used simply of the ***manifest showing and visible exhibition of the Holy Spirit,*** or of the visible gifts, in which — as in mirrors — the Spirit appeared, who at Christ's coming had been promised. Item: of the fullness of grace and gift, and of the virtue of the Spirit, by which we are reborn in Christ, and made new creatures — according to that of **Joel 2:** ***"I will pour out"*** (Titus 3: [***πλουσίως***] ***abundantly and copiously***) ***"of My Spirit upon all flesh,"*** without distinction of persons. **Jews and Gentiles, small and great — as many as shall believe in me — shall be endowed with the Holy Spirit and with spiritual goods.**

Such is the passage of **Acts 19** concerning the twelve Ephesians, whom Paul interrogates — as disciples of Christ, baptised with the ministry of John: ***"Did ye receive the Holy Spirit when ye believed?"*** (For Paul speaks of the ***manifest revelation of the Holy Spirit conjoined with the testimonies of miracles*** — which the word ***"whether"*** [***εἰ***] evidently proves. For whence is faith, save from the Holy Spirit?) But they said: ***"We have not so much as heard whether there be any Holy Spirit"*** — that is, ***we do not know that the Holy Spirit is otherwise given than through faith, and of His manifest exhibition with testimonies of miracles conjoined we have not yet heard anything, nor has the Holy Spirit been in that manner poured out upon us hitherto.***¹⁰⁸ᵐ

Let us therefore understand in that place: [***"He was not yet manifested"***]. And since what follows also seems to have some scruple, it seems good briefly to add those words too. Paul said to them: ***"Into what then have ye been baptised?"*** And they said: ***"Into John's baptism."*** But Paul said: (***"John indeed baptised with the baptism of penitence, saying to the people that they should believe on Him who was to come after him, that is, on Christ Jesus:"***) **but they also** (the people or the hearers of John) ***were baptised into the name of the Lord Jesus. And when Paul had laid his hands upon them, the Holy Spirit came upon them, and they spake with tongues, and prophesied,*** etc. This grammatical syntax removes many labyrinths concerning the Ephesians as ***rebaptised.*** For the particles [***μὲν... δὲ***] mutually corresponding, attest that the sentence is a whole not to be torn asunder, and the accustomed [***μὲν οὖν*** + ***δὲ...***] synthesis has place also in these — since all these things thus far Paul is manifestly recounting, and they are not yet to be referred to the narrative-context begun by Luke, which is first resumed from the following period: [***"and having laid his hand upon them..."***], etc.¹⁰⁸ⁿ

#### The Two Processions — Eternal and Temporal

**Out of these things which have thus far been said,** a **twofold procession** of the Holy Spirit is gathered — ***eternal*** and ***temporal.*** Of the eternal and ineffable procession it has already been said. The temporal procession is named ***the mission and donation of the Holy Spirit,*** whom the whole Divinity — the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit ***Himself*** — sends and gives to creatures — and this in two manners, ***visible*** and ***invisible.*** ***Visibly,*** as at the Baptism of Christ and on the Day of Pentecost. ***Invisibly,*** He is given daily to believers. **Romans 5:** ***"The love of God is shed abroad in our hearts through the Holy Spirit, who is given to us."***¹⁰⁸ᵒ

Of the ***visible*** mission of the Holy Spirit, let the words of **Augustine,** *De Trinitate* book 11, chapter 5, be observed: ***"There was made a certain species of creature in time, in which the Holy Spirit might be visibly shown forth. And this operation visibly expressed, and offered to mortal eyes, has been called the mission of the Holy Spirit — not that His own substance should appear to them, which is invisible and unchangeable as the Father and the Son — but that by exterior visions the hearts of men, moved by the temporal manifestation of Him who comes, might be converted to the hidden eternity of Him who is ever present."***

**Synecdochical, therefore, is this phrase said to be.** ***"The dove is the Holy Spirit"; "the flame in the mouth of the Apostles is the Holy Spirit"*** — that is, ***with that visible thing the Holy Spirit is truly present, and shows Himself visibly, and testifies that He is truly there, and inwardly sanctifies the mind, heart, and the whole man.***

Of this visible manifestation of the Holy Spirit are the words of **John 7** to be understood: ***"The Holy Spirit was not yet, because Jesus was not yet glorified."*** For these words speak of the ***visible and miraculous outpouring*** of the Holy Spirit, which after the ascension took place at Pentecost — and of the fullness of grace and gifts, which as regards the increase and joy of faith and consolation followed the ascension of Christ with greater increment and abundance than had ever previously happened in the Old Testament.

#### The Eight Causes for the Visible Mission of the Holy Spirit

**This visible mission and appearance of the Holy Spirit chiefly took place for these causes.**

**The First:** ***that the promises*** both of the Old Testament and of Christ Himself ***might be fulfilled.*** For Peter, together with the other Apostles in his first sermon (Acts 2) from Joel 2, proves that God promised the mission of the Spirit, His coming, and His outpouring upon all flesh — and that this mission has now been fulfilled. For Christ exalted at the right hand of God, having received the promise from the Father, ***poured forth the Holy Spirit.*** The promise of the Holy Spirit which Christ received from the Father is that supreme power which the Father gave to the Son in heaven and on earth. Such a promise of the Holy Spirit stands in **Zechariah 12:** ***"I will pour out upon the house of David and upon the inhabitants of Jerusalem"*** — that is, upon the Church and her true members — ***the Spirit of grace and of prayers.*** And **Luke in Acts 1** attests that Christ spoke thus of the Holy Spirit: ***"At Jerusalem await the promise of the Father, of which ye have heard from me: for John indeed baptised with water, but ye shall be baptised with the Holy Spirit."*** Item: ***"Ye shall receive power after that the Holy Spirit is come upon you, and ye shall be witnesses to me."*** Here belong the words of Christ, **John 14:** ***"I will pray the Father, and He shall give you another Paraclete."*** And: ***"The Paraclete, the Holy Spirit, whom the Father will send in my name, shall teach you all things."*** **John 15:** ***"The Paraclete I will send to you from the Father."*** **John 16:** ***"If I go, I will send the Paraclete to you."***

**The Second cause is,** that ***Christ, now exalted at the right hand of God, and having all power in heaven and on earth, might prove Himself to be Lord and God,*** from whom the Holy Spirit proceeds, and is sent, that He may be [***the Head***] of the Church of Christ, ***Leader, Ruler, Guardian.***

**The Third:** that the Holy Spirit might show forth His ***divine and omnipotent Majesty and virtue,*** and might liberally discharge the office of His mission and legation — and by this very execution of His office might show Himself to be the ***mover of hearts, sanctifier, and comforter,*** which is proper to the divine nature and power. For to make fiery tongues, to move the heart and sense of men, is the work of God, not of creatures. Hence the saying of **Gregory** is celebrated: ***"The Holy Spirit appeared in fiery tongues, because He makes hearts ardent and mouths speaking."***

**The Fourth cause is,** that the ***abundance of grace and gift*** might manifestly be beheld, and thence the ***certainty of doctrine,*** and ***trust, faith, and consolation*** be confirmed — because God communicates Himself wholly to us and is present with us and in us. For although before the Messiah's exhibition the Holy Spirit was also present in the Church of God, and governed the Prophets, and kindled them with the light and knowledge of the true Messiah and with knowledge of things to come, nevertheless, after Christ had completed His office and function, He was solemnly outpoured upon the Apostles and upon those believing in Christ, and showed that the ***horn of plenty of gifts*** is now exhibited to all believers in the Son of God — as it is written to **Titus 3:** ***"He hath saved us through the laver of regeneration and renewal of the Holy Spirit, whom He hath poured richly upon us through Jesus Christ our Saviour."*** And here is to be referred the whole Prophecy of **Joel 2:** ***"It shall come to pass after this, that I will pour out of My Spirit upon all flesh, and your sons and your daughters shall prophesy,"*** etc.

This abundance of the Holy Spirit was reserved in the Old Testament until the times of the Messiah. For visibly, while Christ was conversant with men, and now glorified, ***the Holy Spirit also showed Himself visibly as a Person distinct from Him*** — so that for the manifestation and vision of the whole Trinity nothing more is now lacking, save that ***face to face*** the Father also, who is the Person sending, might be manifested to us, and with the Son and the Holy Spirit ***be all in all*** — which shall happen in eternal life.

**The Fifth cause is,** that ***the highest testimony of God's love toward us might stand — who imparts Himself to us, and, having given the Holy Spirit, makes us partakers of His own nature,*** even as by having sent the Son He had made Himself partaker of our nature: and that on this account He might be glorified and celebrated as good, merciful, and [***φιλάνθρωπος***]. **1 John 4:** ***"By this we know that we abide in Him, and He in us, because He hath given us of His Spirit."***

**The Sixth cause is,** that the Holy Spirit might ***create, illumine, teach, corroborate, confirm, and animate*** suitable ministers of the New Testament against all onslaughts, machinations, threats, hatreds, and plagues — especially in the primitive Church, full of persecutions.

**The Seventh cause is,** that many, being warned and moved by this visible mission of the Holy Spirit and by the miracle so stupendous and plainly divine, ***might be converted,*** and in this way the true Church of God might be gathered — as on the day of Pentecost Peter with one sermon converted three thousand men.

**The Eighth cause is,** that this very sensible mission might be a ***testimony*** that God through the ministry of the Gospel wills to give the Holy Spirit to all those obedient to the Word of God — even if this happen in an insensible and invisible manner, namely through the Word and through the use of the Sacraments.¹⁰⁸ᵖ

#### Invisibly through the Ministry of the Word

**For invisibly the Holy Spirit is sent into the hearts of the pious through the ministry of the Gospel** — whether through the Word or through the Sacraments. For ***faith is from hearing*** (Rom. 10). And ***the ministry of the New Testament is not the ministry of the letter, but of the Spirit*** (2 Cor. 3). Hence Clement of Alexandria names the Spirit ***the soul of the Word.***

**On the question whether the Holy Spirit is substantially present in the hearts of the reborn, or virtually only in that He kindles certain gifts in the mind and heart** — the true response is taken from Christ's words (***"We will come to him, and make our abode with him,"*** John 14): ***the Holy Spirit is in believers not only in respect of gifts, but really and substantially — not that He is the same Person, but that He is in the believing man as in a dwelling, palace, and temple, inseparably.*** Whence expressly it is written **Gal. 4:** ***"God hath sent the Spirit of His Son into your hearts, crying, Abba, Father."*** And **Augustine** on the words of **Romans 5** says thus: ***"'The love of God is shed abroad in our hearts through the Holy Spirit, who is given to us.' By whom is He given? By Him who gives to men. What gift? The Holy Spirit. For He gives such a gift as He Himself is. Great is His mercy: He gives a gift equal to Himself, because His gift is the Holy Spirit."***¹⁰⁸ᑫ

Let us now use the prayer of **David,** that we may have a ***clean heart*** — namely, by imputation and by inchoation — and a Spirit ***certain, holy, and free,*** **Ps. 51.** ***Certain,*** who firmly assents to the Word and promise, does not vacillate nor doubt of God's will. ***Holy,*** because He sanctifies and governs us in actions, in vocation, and in the whole life. ***Free,*** who willingly submits himself to the will of God, has no resistance, no contumacy, no hostility, but obeys God, implores divine help, is patient in the cross, and constant in confession.

#### The Four Modes of God's Presence

Here also the customary and useful doctrine **of the four modes of God's presence** is to be repeated. **The First mode** is the ***universal presence*** of the whole Divinity — the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit. **The Second** is that by which God is present with Angels and blessed men inseparably in eternity. **The Third grade** pertains to the ministry and all its parts, and to the reborn in this life, in whom God is and dwells through faith and the Holy Spirit ***separably*** — that is, in such wise that if the saints rush into crimes, He deserts them and departs from them. This presence in the reborn is otherwise called ***renewal, inchoation, sanctification, quickening, indwelling, newness of life, first-fruits of the Spirit,*** and ***gift.*** For this grade must be distinguished from the second, and out of zeal for novelty and sophistical [inclination] the confusion which some today make is not to be admitted. **The Fourth grade is [***καθ' ὑπόστασιν***]** — as Justin speaks — the ***hypostatic union of the divine and human nature in Christ the Son of God and [of Mary]***, who also as God and man sends and gives to us the Holy Spirit, as Peter says in **Acts 2:** ***"Jesus of Nazareth, crucified, exalted to the right hand of God, hath poured forth the Holy Spirit."*** There is also added ***a fifth grade,*** namely the ***sacramental*** — as when the corporeal presence of Christ in the Supper is spoken of. But of these enough for now.¹⁰⁸ʳ

#### On the Offices of the Holy Spirit — De Officiis Spiritus Sancti

**The offices and effects of the Holy Spirit are comprehended in this little verse:**

> ***"He is our pledge; He teaches, renews, aids, seals unto salvation."***

For the Holy Spirit is the ***earnest and pledge*** of our heavenly inheritance, and gives assurance of life and eternal salvation (Eph. 1).

**He teaches truth** — that is, **first,** He shows that men cannot by their own ingenuity attain to the true doctrine of the essence and will of God; **then,** He shows that the truth of the doctrine of the redemption, righteousness, and salvation of the human race is not of the Law, but of the Gospel; **thirdly,** He is efficacious in teachers, that they may understand the truth; and in learners, that they may receive it. Of these things it is said in **John 16:** ***"He shall convince the world of sin, of righteousness, and of judgement"*** — that is, ***by teaching He shall convince the world to be guilty of sin, to lack righteousness, and to be subject to the tyranny of the Devil and to eternal death.*** **Eph. 3:** ***"In other ages [this mystery] was not made known to the sons of men, as it is now revealed to His holy Apostles and Prophets by the Spirit, that the Gentiles should be fellow-heirs and of the same body, and consorts of the promise in Christ through the Gospel."*** Item, **John 14 and 16:** ***"The Spirit of truth shall teach you all things."*** And **1 John 2:** ***"The anointing teacheth you all things, and is true and is not a lie: and as it hath taught you, remain in it."*** By the ***anointing*** is named the Holy Spirit Himself, anointing us into ***kings and priests,*** that we may offer to God ***spiritual sacrifices,*** and may be victors of the Devil, the world, and death, through faith in Christ.¹⁰⁸ˢ

**Doctrine is followed by renewal,** which is effected by the efficacy of the Holy Spirit through the Word, and through the use of Baptism and the Lord's Supper.

**Renewal is followed by the help of the Holy Spirit,** who aids the reborn ***in four modes:***

**1. By testifying:** as it is written, ***"When the Paraclete shall have come, He shall bear witness of me"*** — namely, of my Person and my benefits.

**2. By giving to the reborn true confession of the [***ὁμοούσιος***] Son of God and the confession of Christ, and many other spiritual gifts** — as **1 Cor. 12** it is written: ***"No man can call Jesus Lord"*** — namely, not verbally, but with true [***καρδίᾳ***] confession — ***but by the Holy Spirit.*** [Verbal confession] is that which consists in words only, and directs neither mind nor heart to the glory of God — such a confession the Devils also, and the impious, and secure men can use. But [heart-confession] is that in which the mind of the confessor is converted to God, and looks upon a congruent end, and gives true glory to God, and rests upon true faith in Christ, and is confirmed — as it is written **Ps. 116:** ***"I believed, therefore I have spoken."*** Of other gifts Paul speaks in the same place — such as ***speech of divine things, science by which others are instructed, trust, consolation, knowledge of tongues,*** etc.

**3. The Holy Spirit aids by consoling,** whence He is called ***Paraclete,*** that is, advocate, patron of the Church and of all the pious — who is present to us miserable ones, and receives our cause as His own, and instructs, warns, teaches us wandering, lest we lose the cause — and at last consoles us languishing, and raises us up, certifying us that we have God propitious, and that we are sons of God, as it is written **Rom. 5:** ***"The love of God is shed abroad in our hearts through the Holy Spirit, who is given to us."*** And **Rom. 14:** ***"The kingdom of God is righteousness, and peace, and joy in the Holy Spirit."*** Hence the Church piously sings: ***"Best Consoler, sweet guest of the soul, sweet refreshment; in labour, rest; in the heat, temperance; in weeping, solace."***

**4. He aids by praying,** according to that of **Rom. 8:** ***"Ye have received the Spirit of adoption, through whom we cry Abba, Father."*** And **Gal. 4:** ***"That very thing which we are to pray for, as we ought, we know not; but the Spirit Himself intercedeth for us with groanings unutterable."***

#### The Sealing of the Spirit

**Whoever has this help of the Holy Spirit is already sealed** in the certainty of God's promises — that is, he truly believes and obeys God, is the temple of the Holy Spirit, heir of God, and co-heir of Christ, according to that of **Eph. 1:** ***"Ye have been sealed with the Holy Spirit of promise, who is the earnest of our inheritance for the redemption of the acquired possession, unto the praise of His glory."*** **Eph. 4;** **2 Cor. 1** says the same: ***"God hath sealed us, and given the earnest of the Spirit in our hearts."***

From this sealing of the Holy Spirit we can gather and establish that ***it is the work and benefit of the Holy Spirit alone that we have faith, retain faith, and constantly persevere in it, and retain a good conscience,*** as **1 Peter 1** it is written: ***"By faith ye are kept in the power and virtue of God unto salvation."*** And the Son of God Himself says **John 6:** ***"This is the work of God, that ye believe on Him whom the Father hath sent."***

**From the same sealing it is established** how it may be recognised who indeed have the Holy Spirit, and who not. For the sealing is not idle, but brings forth fruits of faith and of the Spirit — of which it is written **Gal. 3:** ***"The fruits of the Spirit are love, joy, peace."*** And **Rom. 8:** ***"If by the Spirit ye mortify the deeds of the body, ye shall live."***

**Wherefore they who in sins against conscience live and persevere, have not the Holy Spirit.** For ***"He that committeth sin is of the Devil"*** (1 John 3). And they who rush against conscience into errors, and into crimes — these ***shake off the Holy Spirit,*** and afflict Him with ignominy and even with our own sorrow.

Considering these things, let us deplore our own depravity, and beseech that we may be governed by the Spirit of God — who is the Spirit of ***sanctification*** (Rom. 1), of ***love, and of soberness*** (2 Tim. 1) — and let us beware of falling against conscience, and let us always have in view that most grave and most severe judgement of **1 Thess. 4:** ***"God hath not called us unto uncleanness, but unto sanctification: therefore he that rejecteth, rejecteth not man, but God, who hath given His Holy Spirit unto us."*** And Paul in that place uses the word which signifies ***to spurn, and out of contempt (as it were) to set aside the Holy Spirit, to irritate, reject, and cast forth the Holy Spirit Himself*** — which happens by sins against conscience: covetousness, lusts, hatred, adulteries, thefts, drunkenness, curses, etc. Here certainly true is that which is wont to be said: ***"Guests are cast out more foully than they are refused admittance."*** And of these Peter says (2 Peter 2): ***"The dog is turned to his own vomit again, and the washed sow to her wallowing in the mire."***¹⁰⁸ᵗ

#### How and Through What the Holy Spirit is Given — Quomodo et Per Quid Detur Spiritus Sanctus

**The Holy Spirit is given through the vocal Word and preaching of the Gospel** — which has itself to the Holy Spirit as the body to the soul. For the Spirit is the soul of the Word. Under the Word must also be comprehended the ***use of the Sacraments,*** of Baptism and the Supper, and ***true invocation,*** of which the Son of God says (Luke 11): ***"If ye, being evil, know how to give good gifts to your children, how much more shall your Father in heaven give the Holy Spirit to those who ask Him?"*** And in **Acts 4** it is written: ***"When they had prayed, the place was moved, and they were all filled with the Holy Spirit, and spake the word of God with confidence."*** Of the vocal Word Paul in **Eph. 1** speaks most gravely: ***"Ye have heard the word of truth — even the Gospel of your salvation — in which believing ye were sealed with the Holy Spirit of promise, who is the earnest of our inheritance."*** And **Acts 10** it is written: ***"While Peter yet spake, the Holy Spirit fell on all them that heard the word."*** Of the Sacraments also Paul says (**Titus 3**): ***"God saved us according to His mercy through the laver of regeneration, and renewal of the Holy Spirit, whom He poured richly upon us through Jesus Christ our Saviour."*** And the Son of God (**John 3**) joins to Nicodemus water and Spirit: ***"Unless one be reborn of water and Spirit, he cannot enter into the kingdom of heaven."*** Diligently therefore let us read, hear, and meditate on the Word of God, let us venerate the ministry, and magnify the use of the Sacraments — for through these things, joined with invocation and piety of life, the Holy Spirit is given and remains in us — and not otherwise. **Enthusiasms and raptures of Anabaptists are not to be feigned, apart from the hearing of the Word;** a new and secure rebellion, contumacy, and repugnance must not be introduced; but the Son of God must be heard, who ***promises the Holy Spirit and the increase of the Holy Spirit to those hearing, learning, and asking.*** Whoever does not do this, it is necessary that there befall him what has been expressed in certain little verses of a demonic sort, as it were:¹⁰⁸ᵘ

> ***"A vestal virgin, frequenting frequent fastings,***
> ***Thinks herself carried away by the divine breath.***
> ***She neither reads nor prays; she only rolls sublime thoughts,***
> ***And, doing this sweetly, catches sleep.***
> ***Presently the illustrious vision of the divine deity***
> ***Comes upon her; she beholds Angelic choirs on high.***
> ***The Spirit of God Himself (so she dreams) placing a crown,***
> ***Author of holy office, is present.***
> ***But waking, touching her head, she finds the betraying crown***
> ***To be — this virgin — a cow-flop of dung.***
>
> ***Say, ye who feign these ravishings for the empty mind,***
> ***That your hope has been placed in the sacred Word.***
> ***We live in the Word, as the embryo lives in the womb;***
> ***Without the Word of God, hope, faith, falls away.***
> ***The Spirit is the virtue of the Word, and the life of what sounds:***
> ***And equally with wings He fosters vows and prayers.***
> ***O God, who reformest all things moved by life-giving warmth,***
> ***From the light of the Father and the Son, send Thyself to me;***
> ***Embrace us also from heaven with wings spread abroad;***
> ***Quicken our hearts by Thy Word.***¹⁰⁸ᵛ

#### For What the Holy Spirit is Given — Ad Quid Detur Spiritus Sanctus

**For what the Holy Spirit is given to us, and what He works in us,** there are two things which make this plain. **The first cause is the ministry of the Gospel. The other cause is the highest necessity of all believers — faith, consolation, invocation, salvation.** What pertains to the ministry and keys of the Church — the Holy Spirit is given, that these may be rightly, legitimately, and orderly performed and administered — namely: **1.** ***Preaching of the uncorrupt Word;*** **2.** ***Dispensation of the Sacraments;*** **3.** ***Absolution or remission of sins, both publicly and privately;*** **4.** ***Ordination of suitable persons for the ministry;*** **5.** ***Excommunication of the contumacious,*** who caught in manifest errors and crimes are unwilling, even when admonished, to desist from them; **6.** ***Absolution of the same, when they have done penance.*** — What pertains to individual believers, the Holy Spirit is given, that He may illumine, rule, govern, console, confirm, and conserve them in the whole life, in all fears, both spiritual and corporeal — as it is written **Rom. 8:** ***"The Spirit Himself giveth witness with our spirit"*** — that is, He speaks consolation in our fears, mingling Himself with our spirits, vital and animal, in the hearts and brains of pious men, bending and moderating the pious to hope, patience, invocation, consolation, and to those things which are pleasing to God, and salutary both to themselves and to others.

**Concerning the efficacy of the Holy Spirit let these testimonies be at hand:** **Isaiah 51:** ***"I have put my words in thy mouth"*** (of all pious teachers, namely) ***"and with the shadow of my hand have I protected thee, that thou mayest plant the heavens, and stablish the earth, and mayest say to Zion, Thou art my people."*** And chapter 59: ***"This is my covenant, saith the Lord: My Spirit, which is in thee, and my words, which I have put in thy mouth, shall not depart out of thy mouth, nor out of the mouth of thy seed henceforth even for ever, saith the Lord."***

**John 8:** ***"He that heareth the word of God is of God."*** And: ***"Verily, verily I say unto you, He that keepeth my saying, shall not see death for ever."*** **John 10:** ***"No man shall pluck my sheep out of my hand."*** **John 17:** ***"The love wherewith thou, Father, hast loved me, may be in them, and I in them."*** **Mark last chapter:** ***"He that believeth and is baptised, shall be saved."*** **Rom. 1:** ***"The Gospel is the power of God unto salvation to every one that believeth."***

**In Acts 2 two similitudes are set forth, by which the efficacy of the Holy Spirit is depicted** — one taken from wind, the other from fire. In wind there concur ***sound*** and ***breath.*** In the ministry there concur ***Word*** and ***Spirit.*** For faith is from hearing. And as wind chiefly blows upon, breaks, and destroys lofty things, and fosters lowly things, so the Holy Spirit casts down, breaks, dissipates the things which before the world are lofty, proud, and great — and delights in the lowly, subject, prostrate, acknowledging and confessing their sins, and fleeing to the grace of God alone offered, given, and shown to us in the Son. And as fire burns and consumes excrements and dross, and purges gold, so the Holy Spirit by the voice of the Law uncovers and shows sins, and by the voice of the Gospel heals and purges them — and thus through the ministry of the Law damns every one [***without excuse***], and through the ministry of the Gospel heals every one [***who believes***], and kindles in us true faith and true consolation, which rests upon the precious blood of the Son of God.¹⁰⁸ʷ

#### On Grieving the Holy Spirit — Quid Sit Contristare Spiritum Sanctum

**Eph. 4:** ***"Grieve not the Holy Spirit of God, in whom ye are sealed for the day of redemption."*** All those grieve and cast out the Holy Spirit from themselves who rush into sins, errors, and crimes against conscience. In the pious retaining the Holy Spirit, however, the Same is grieved when the hearts of the pious are perturbed — as today false teachers, ambitious, contentious men, and the security, ingratitude, manifold errors, and horrible crimes of men grieve the Holy Spirit in many pious — as of **Lot** it is written (2 Peter 2): ***"Evil men by their unjust works vexed the just soul from day to day."*** And this grief is a certain indication of ***imminent punishments*** in Church, Republic, and every kind of life. May our God clemently have mercy on us, and deliver us.

#### The Distinction between Spirit and Letter — Quod Sit Discrimen inter Spiritum et Literam

**By Paul the ***Letter*** is named the whole doctrine, whether of the Law or the Gospel, and every work of man ***without the efficacy of the Holy Spirit inwardly renewing hearts.*** The ***Spirit*** on the other hand is named the doctrine of Law and Gospel efficacious in the heart, and every work of the reborn man. This ***metonymy*** or ***metalepsis*** is illustrious in Paul — as **Rom. 7:** ***"Let us serve in newness of the Spirit, and not in the oldness of the letter."*** And **Rom. 2:** ***"Circumcision in the Spirit, not in the letter."*** And **2 Cor. 3:** Paul says: ***"The letter killeth, but the Spirit giveth life"*** — that is, he who hears the Law without acknowledgement of Christ, incurs death; and he who does not believe the doctrine of the Gospel, or who hears the Gospel without faith, is also damned. But he who hears the Law as a ***schoolmaster to Christ,*** and hearing the doctrine of the Gospel believes in Christ — he has the Holy Spirit, and eternal life. **Chrysostom** adds also this interpretation on the collation of Law and Gospel instituted in chapter 6 to the **Romans:** [***"For the Law of the Spirit of life in Christ Jesus, it says, hath made me free."***] The Law indeed convicts of sin, but grace remits sin. The Law is spiritual, but does not exhibit the Holy Spirit. For Moses did not bring the Spirit, but letters. Of these things we have discussed more copiously in the ***Paedagogia.***

**Now, imposing an end to the doctrine of the Holy Spirit,** let us beseech that here [***the Comforter***] be sent into our hearts, that He may rule and lead us in such perils of life by which on every side we are surrounded — as He led Moses through the Red Sea, as Isaiah says at chapter 64:¹⁰⁸ˣ

> ***"O nourishing Spirit, most pious Breath of Father and Son,***
> ***Irradiate our minds also with living light,***
> ***And defend the greatest mysteries of Thy Word,***
> ***And restrain Satan, lying dogmas, quarrels,***
> ***Heretics, false brethren, dire tyrants.***
> ***Bend our wills, and give us peace.***
> ***Guardian of all Thine own, and Consoler of sorrows.***
> ***Without Thee we are nothing, and without Thee, our Guardian, we perish.***
> ***And if we are anything, we are miseries and terrors.***
> ***Through Thee we all live, blessed by Thy fortune;***
> ***Living and dying, with Thee as our Guide we ascend the stars."***
