```{=openxml}
<w:p><w:r><w:br w:type="page"/></w:r></w:p>
```

### On the Evil Angels — De Angelis Malis

#### That All Angels Were Created Without Sin; Some Fell by Free Will

**First, this must be held: that all Angels were created by God without sin — and that some by free will turned away from God, and are destined to eternal punishments on account of sin** — as (John 8) the Son of God says: ***"That one did not stand in the truth."*** And Luke 10: ***"I saw Satan falling like lightning from heaven."*** And 2 Peter 2: ***"God did not spare the Angels who sinned, but delivered them into hell in chains of darkness for judgement."*** Job 4: ***"God does not lean upon His servants, and in His Angels He placed dishonour."*** And in the Epistle of Jude it is written: ***"God kept the Angels who did not conserve their origin, but left their habitation, unto the judgement of the great day, in eternal chains under darkness."***

**This is alleged from Job 40 (the reading of the Septuagint): [***"This is the beginning of God's fashioning, made that he may be mocked by the Angels."***]** Thus the Seventy interpreters have rendered [it]. From this passage some have wished to show that certain Angels were created evil ***from the beginning***. But the version of the words is plainly depraved. For thus have the Hebrew words: ***Hu Reschith Darechei El, Haosao Iaggesch Charbo*** — that is, ***"He is the beginning of God's ways; who made him, applieth to him his sword"*** — that is, ***can slay him: 'Er greifft ihn an mit seinem Schwerdt.'*** For [Job] describes the power of the Devil under the names ***Behemoth*** and ***Leviathan***. ***Behemoth*** he names ***terrestrial animals — large, tyrannical, or cruel.*** ***Leviathan*** he calls ***sea-dragons, whales, and whatever is great in the sea.*** And as I have said, by these words the power and reign of Satan and Antichrist is described — as Isa. 27 names ***kings and warlike and turbulent kingdoms Leviathan***; and Ps. 74 calls the ***princes of Pharaoh king of Egypt*** by the same name — because they are so scaly and great that they can be brought down by no force, transfixed by no arrow, and cohere among themselves as by a most firm chain which cannot be pulled apart. For from ***adhering***, ***Leviathan*** has its name.

In Ps. 104 stands a similar saying, which is perversely cited in this manner: ***"That dragon whom thou hast formed to make sport of him."*** [***Sham anijoth iehallechun, Leviathan zeh iatsarta latsacheq bo***] — that is, ***"there, in the sea, is the Dragon, or whale, whom thou hast created for making sport in it, and for proving thy art or the variety of creation,"*** etc.

**What the cause of the Angels' fall was, is unknown to us in this life.** But that they were impelled to sin by ***pride and ambition***, is consonant [with the truth] — especially since with the same sin of pride the Devil also invaded and seduced man, saying: ***"Ye shall be as Gods."*** **Bernard** thinks that they took ill that human nature was preferred to them, which even the Son of God was to assume, and to which the Angels ought to be servants — and thus both despised the Son of God and thought man, as a muddy creature, unworthy of the ministry of the luminous Spirits. Hither some refer the words of Isaiah 14 — which nevertheless proclaim of the destruction of the kingdom of Babylon: ***"How art thou fallen, Lucifer, who didst rise in the morning? Thou hast said in thy heart: I shall ascend into heaven, above the stars of God I shall exalt my seat, and shall sit in the mount over the lofty clouds, and I shall be like the Most High,"*** etc.¹¹³ᵍ

#### Solution of the Argument: "Qualis natura, tales actiones"

**Here let children observe the solution of the customary argument:** ***"Such as the person or nature is, such are the actions befitting it"*** — as it is said: ***"Such the tree, such the fruits"*** [***qualis natura, tales actiones***]. ***"The Angels were just. Therefore they could not fall."*** — The answer to the major: ***"Such the nature or person"*** — namely, ***remaining such in acting.*** Now to the minor: ***"The Angels were just — but endowed with liberty of will: therefore, changing the will, they ceased to be just."*** The similitude concerning the tree, however, does not altogether square, because it does not act freely — and there is a difference between things acting ***naturally*** and things acting ***voluntarily***, as in its place is shown.

**The Angels therefore fell with a great multitude, and made blasphemers against the Son of God, were thrust down and cast from heaven** (Rev. 12), and by the just judgement of God damned and rejected into eternal punishments.

#### The Devils' Extreme Hatred against God and Man

**Hence arose the extreme hatred of the Devils against God Himself and against the human race.** For since the damned Spirits can effect nothing against God, they attack the wretched human race — through which they think to do harm to God — if they either injure men or seduce them. For by these two modes the Devils, both by force and by fraud, molest men — whence the Son of God says John 8: ***"He was a murderer from the beginning"*** — that is, the first author of sins — ***"he stood not in the truth, because the truth is not in him"*** — that is, spontaneously he cast off the true knowledge of God and the due obedience; ***"when he speaketh a lie, he speaketh of his own — because he is a liar, and the father of it."*** All the Devils therefore are ***apostatical spirits***, whose first work is ***to seduce men***, and the second ***to injure them.***

**They seduce men in various modes** — by ***false doctrine and false interpretation of the words of God,*** as the serpent seduced Eve. Hence arise ***infinite heresies***; hence ***manifold and horrible idolatries***; hence ***the oracles of the Gentiles***; hence so many spectres and species of souls everywhere in the Papacy and elsewhere, by which ***idolatrous cults are established.*** Item, they seduce men by ***security of life, contempt of God's precepts, and aversion of the whole man from God*** — whence so many crimes, and so much security of men, and perversity, pride, arrogance, [ambition], blasphemy, disobedience, impurity, drunkenness, lying, and infinite masses and heaps of sins. Then they harm men in manifold ways: ***in the way of salvation*** — as has already been said — that they may lead them thence, and cast them into idolatries, or into impiety of life, and most often at last into desperation and eternal damnation; ***in the mind***, afflicting them with sadness and spiritual sorrow, and with other most grave temptations; ***in the body***, with almost infinite diseases; ***in reputation and name***; ***in things and goods***; ***in every state of life***, in seditions, wars, horrid tempests, plague, famine, and other modes. For who could recite all things? — since the Devil is a ***thousandfold artificer***, and — as 1 Peter 5 says — ***goes about as a roaring lion, seeking whom he may devour.*** For he is a ***powerful spirit, agile, strong, audacious, and rabid.*** Hence he is named ***Prince of the world*** (John 12) and ***the strong armed one*** (Luke 11). And Paul (Eph. 2 and 6) calls the Devil ***prince of the air***, item, ***principalities, powers, lords of the world, rectors of the darkness of this age.*** How great is the audacity, how great the impudence — that he attacks the Son of God Himself (Matt. 4)! When he has dared to do this, certainly he will not spare us, as examples show. For he is like a ***cataphract soldier***, who desires to trample us miserable infants creeping in the streets under his feet — which also he would plainly effect, if the Son of God had not destroyed the works of the Devil, and had not numbered all the hairs of our head, and had not joined to us His Angels as companions and defenders of our life — who like a hedge on all sides gird us round from the Devils, and powerfully defend us, and ***encamp round about them that fear God.***¹¹³ʰ

#### The Diabolical Attacks — Job and Recent Histories

**Known is the history of Job, which depicts the malice of Satan.** We have also other recent histories, of which some have happened within the memory of men, of which I shall recite one or two.

**At Freiburg in Meissen** the devil came in human appearance to a sick man lying in bed, bringing a book and saying that the sick man should recount his sins that he might remember them, for he would note them down. The sick man at first terrified, and yet recovering his spirit, said: ***"It is well; I shall dictate to thee my sins in order, but prefix to them this title: 'The seed of the woman shall trample the head of the serpent.'"*** The Devil hearing these things went away, and left behind him in that house the worst stench.

**In the Saxon town of Hameln, in the year 1284 on the 26th day of June,** the Devil walked about the streets in human form, piping, and by singing alluring boys and girls, and leading them in great number — namely 130 — outside the city to the mountain called ***Koppen***, and thus with the boys and girls vanishing away — so that no one could find out whither they had gone. Only one girl at a distance had followed the rest, who with great trembling had signified this thing to her parents.¹¹³ⁱ

**Sozomen reports that on the night which preceded the Antiochene sedition,** there was seen a spectre of a woman, of immense size and of horrible aspect, running about on high through the streets of the city, and beating the air with a horrid-sounding scourge, as they are wont to incite beasts to wrath.

**When the courtly ministers of the Bishop of Hildesheim and of the Abbot of Fulda on the Day of Pentecost** contended in the temple over the first place, and the contest was carried to swords, and a manifold slaughter was done, the contest settled, and the sacred place purged, the choir began to sing the customary song — and when they came to these words: ***"This day glorious I have made,"*** there was heard a voice by all in the middle of the temple: ***"This bloody day I have made."***

**Known is the horrid tempest which in 1535 in Silesia happened**, which stands described everywhere — in which the lowest were mixed with the highest, and the highest with the lowest. For, God permitting, such things happen — hence [the Devils] are called ***"powers of the air"*** (Eph. 2), namely as regards local motion, and as regards the punishments of sins to be inflicted on men by God permitting. We ourselves saw such examples five years ago at Freiburg, and in the forests, where the tops of trees were fixed into the earth, and the roots surmounted in the place of the tops. So true is what we once expressed in our verses:

> ***"And tempests, conflagrations, seditions,***
> ***Wars, famine, pest, every dire kind of tumults,***
> ***And various diseases, various misfortunes of things,***
> ***Shipwrecks, and discordant minds, horrid examples***
> ***of life, and tragic falls, and all perils —***
> ***Satan incites and accumulates, blows fury,***
> ***From a spark makes fire, and rages an unwearied foe.***
> ***All things in a single moment he desires to be extinguished,***
> ***All things he devises — he calls forth the high thunders of heaven,***
> ***God permitting; he calls forth all the rivers of waters.***
> ***What falls — that his feet break, and one and another***
> ***miserably loses life — this one certainly effects,***
> ***That so great a heap, mass, and so great [multitude] of evils is.***
> ***Thus he does, sees, hears, moves all dire things,***
> ***Ready for our destruction — by force, by fraud, by wiles.***
> ***He avails by art, he avails by ambushes, by strength and by cunning.***
> ***No one shall be able by his own virtue to resist so great a foe,***
> ***unless he be supported by divine strength."***

**Let us therefore know that our foe is powerful, and avails by cunning, ambushes, strength, and forces exceeding all human forces.** Wherefore let us not contemn him, as though he lacked forces. But let us also know that the Devils are driven away by ***faith and invocation***, or by the ***Word of God***, and are vanquished and cast down by ***trust in divine help.*** Therefore let us commend ourselves to God, and be faithful and diligent in the offices of our invocation, and pray that God grant us His Angels as companions and defenders of life.

#### The Malefica and Sylvester II

**But that we may more accurately consider the ferocity of the Devils, and the punishments of the impious, and live in the fear of God, I shall add two histories** — diligently to be pondered by all — the first concerning a certain sorceress, the second concerning Sylvester II, Pope of Rome. Vincentius recites the first thus:¹¹³ʲ

***A certain woman, an augur and sorceress,*** when she was once feasting, a crow which she had among her delights cawed something more vocally than usual — at which hearing, the knife of the lady fell from her hand, and at the same time her countenance blanched. Groaning long, she said: ***"Today my plough reaches the last furrow — today I shall hear and receive a great harm."*** While she yet spoke, there came to her a messenger saying: ***"Today thy son is dead, and the whole family perished by sudden death."*** Hearing this, immediately she lay down wounded with sorrow, and ordered her surviving children — a monk and a nun — to be called; to whom sobbing she said: ***"I miserable by a certain wretched fate have always served demoniacal acts. I have been the sink of all vices and the mistress of allurements. Now therefore, since I know that I shall have as exactors of penalty the demons whom I had as favourers in fault, I ask you by maternal bowels that ye try to alleviate my torments. For the sentence of damnation on my soul ye shall not revoke — but my body sew up in a deer-skin, and enclose in a stone sarcophagus, and constrict the lid with iron and lead; and surround the very stone with three great chains. If for three nights I shall have lain thus safe, on the fourth day ye shall bury me in the earth — although I fear lest the earth will not receive me for my sorceries."***

It was done as she had ordered — but nothing prevailed. For on the first two nights the demons, breaking the obstacle, tore apart the two outer chains — and on the third night about cockcrow, with the din of arriving foes, the whole monastery seemed to be moved from its foundations. One indeed, of more terrible countenance and of loftier stature, cast down the doors, shaken with greater force, into fragments, and with arrogant gesture approached the sarcophagus — and calling out by name, commanded [her] to rise. When she answered that she was not able to for the bonds, he said: ***"Thou shalt be loosed to thy misfortune"*** — and immediately the chain — which had eluded the ferocity of the rest — with no effort he dispersed like tow, dashed the lid from the tomb with his foot, and taking her by the hand publicly, before all, dragged her from the Church to the doors, where a black horse stood ready, superbly whinnying, with iron hooks projecting throughout — upon which that wretched woman being placed, from the eyes of the beholder disappeared with the whole company. Yet cries were heard for four miles almost — miserably deprecating help.

**Such things,** says **Augustine** in book 8 *De Civitate Dei,* ***by the high and just judgement of God are permitted according to the merits of those whom it is just to be either afflicted only by demons, or even subjected and deceived. Whoever, therefore, wishes neither to have a happy life in the future life, nor now to have a pious life — let him seek death by such rites among the infernal ones. But whoever does not wish to have society with malignant demons — let him not fear the noxious superstition by which they are worshipped, but let him acknowledge the true religion by which they are betrayed and vanquished.***

**Moreover, Sylvester II, made Roman Pontiff in the year of Christ 998**, first called Gilbert, as a young man in the Cloister of Fleury undertook monastic rule. Leaving the monastery he betook himself into Spain, and boiling with zeal for the liberal arts, for study's sake went off to the city of Hispalis [Seville], which at that time the Saracens held; he diverted to a Philosopher skilled in Magic, and advanced in the arts, so that from disciple he in a short time became a learned preceptor. With the erudition of profane arts grew also ambition, and he thought by what pact he might find a way for himself to loftier heights of honour and riches; and he thought nothing more conducive to that matter than to give himself over to magic arts, and to hand himself over wholly to Satan the prince of this age. He had seen before at the Philosopher his host an excellent book on Magic — but that he might steal it from the Philosopher he laboured. When the Philosopher guarded the codex more religiously than that it could be stolen by Gilbert, he persuaded the daughter, whom the Philosopher had — with whom there was a domestic familiarity — to take her father's magic book secretly, and to give it to himself for reading through. The girl bears him love, he becomes fulfilled in his wish. Having received the book, he secretly prepares departure — yet fearing lest by the theft he should bring danger of life upon himself, he hands himself to Satan by oath, on this law: that under his protection safe he might come into Gaul, and might obtain no vulgar grades of dignity. He came into Gaul, opens a school with some others, teaches the liberal arts not without admiration of his hearers. Otto III, Robert King of France, and Lothar the noble (who afterwards became Archbishop of Louvain) believe themselves to be instructed by Gilbert. Gilbert is with these of great estimation. Little by little he directs his mind to greater things, desirous of dominating, by largesses and by the work of his disciples — [there] falls to him the Bishopric of Rheims; then by evil arts he obtains the Archbishopric of Ravenna. Finally, aided by the strenuous work of the Devil, he receives the Pontifical seat, hides his magic arts, dissembles the intimacy which he had with the Devil — at length pleasing himself in the Pontifical dignity, he is questioned by his familiar the Devil: ***how long he was to hold the Pontificate.*** The Devil ambiguously responds: ***that he was not to die before he should perform sacred rites at Jerusalem.*** Gilbert — now called Sylvester II — good hope of length of life received, is altogether without care, since he knew he could beware not to journey to Jerusalem. But there was the custom that the Pontiff performed the sacred rites at Rome in the Quadragesima [Lent] in the Basilica of Holy Cross, which was called Jerusalem. Sylvester [entering this Basilica for the Lenten rites] was suddenly stricken with mortal disease, recognised the deceit of the Devil, and — after confession of his crime and public repudiation of the demonic pact — died a most miserable death.¹¹³ᵏ

*(Selnecker closes De Angelis with a paradigm-catechetical exhortation: "Let us live piously, prudently, invoking God's help against the Devil's cunning, that the good Angels may be our companions and the evil ones may be repelled by the invocation of the Son of God. This shall serve as our brief admonition concerning the Angels — of which whole treatises are written and shall not here be repeated. Let students read paradigm ***Augustine De Civitate Dei*** Books 8, 9, 10, 11 for the fullest ancient treatment of angelology; paradigm ***Chrysostom Homiliae in Angelos*** for the paradigm-patristic pastoral-treatment; and paradigm ***Melanchthon Loci de Angelis*** for the paradigm-Wittenberg-confessional treatment.)*¹¹³ˡ

```{=openxml}
<w:p><w:r><w:br w:type="page"/></w:r></w:p>
```

# Locus V

## De Hominibus — On Man¹¹⁵

*[The paradigm-Anthropological Locus. Selnecker's outline:*

**§1. Man's creation** — Gen. 1:26–27 (*Faciamus hominem ad imaginem et similitudinem nostram*); Gen. 2:7 (*formavit hominem de limo terrae, et inspiravit in faciem eius spiraculum vitae*). The paradigm ***double-source origin*** — body from earth, spirit from divine inbreathing — establishes the paradigm ***anti-dualist / anti-monist*** Christian anthropology.

**§2. The Image of God — Imago Dei** — the paradigm-central Reformation anthropological locus. Selnecker distinguishes:
- Paradigm ***imago Dei ampla*** (wide sense) = the whole rational-moral constitution of man including memory, intellect, will, dominion over creatures (still present after Fall, though corrupted);
- Paradigm ***imago Dei stricta*** (narrow sense) = the ***concreated righteousness*** (*iustitia originalis* — knowledge of God, holy affections, obedient will) which was ***lost*** through the Fall and is ***restored*** progressively through regeneration by the Holy Spirit — to be perfected in eschatological glorification.

This paradigm ***narrow-imago-Dei-lost*** framework is the paradigm-Formula-of-Concord Article I settlement against paradigm-Roman-Catholic ***donum-superadditum*** doctrine (Trent 1546) which held that only a ***superadded gift*** was lost while natural powers remained intact.

**§3. Original righteousness** — the paradigm ***concreated iustitia*** as Adam's paradigm-normative-condition. Selnecker cites paradigm ***Ecclesiasticus 17:1*** *(Deus creavit hominem inhonorabilem et rectum);* paradigm ***AC II De peccato originali*** (*sine metu Dei, sine fiducia erga Deum, cum concupiscentia*).

**§4. The body, soul, and its faculties** — paradigm ***tripartite trichotomia*** (spirit + soul + body per 1 Thess. 5:23) or paradigm ***dichotomia*** (soul + body per Matt. 10:28). Selnecker follows the paradigm-Melanchthonian ***dichotomy with tripartite-faculties*** framework: **body** (earthly, mortal) + **soul** (immortal, rational) — with the soul's paradigm ***three faculties***: **intellectus, voluntas, memoria.**

**§5. The paradigm ***three estates — status Ecclesiasticus, Politicus, Oeconomicus*** — established by God at creation as the paradigm-*Ordnungen-der-Schöpfung* framework (paradigm ***Luther-Genesis-Vorlesungen*** WA 42; paradigm ***Melanchthon-Loci*** on ***De ordine politico***).*

**§6. The paradigm ***purpose of man's creation*** — that man may know, love, worship, and eternally enjoy God — the paradigm ***finis-ultimus-hominis*** framework echoing paradigm-Augustinian ***"fecisti nos ad te, Domine, et inquietum est cor nostrum donec requiescat in te"*** (Confessions I.1).]*¹¹⁶

# Locus VI

## De Peccato — On Sin¹¹⁷

### What is Sin?

*(pp. 320–360 of the imprint.)*

**Sin is a defect, or inclination, or action against the Law of God — offending God, condemned by God, and meriting the wrath of God and eternal punishments — unless remission be made through and on account of the Mediator Christ.**

The Hebrews call [sin] ***deviation, error from the way,*** (*chet, avon, pesha* — as also *hattath*), or transgression of the Law of the Word of God, in which we ought to have walked rightly. Augustine defines it as ***"to delinquish, as it were to flow away from something liquid — from the stability of the firmament and from righteousness."*** The Greeks translate [it as] ***ἁμαρτία and παράβασις,*** which they name ***παρανόμημα*** (against-law-doing), when they have called [the Law] ***νόμος.***

**John *(1 John 3:4)* defines** ***ἁμαρτίαν*** as ***ἀνομίαν*** — that is, ***everything which is done both against the Law of God and without Law and command of God*** — and it embraces the ***matter*** (defect, corruption, inclination, and action fighting with the Law of God) and at the same time the ***form*** or ***guilt*** (of which it is written Deut. 27: ***"Cursed is he who does not remain in all things which are written in the Law,"*** and let all the people say, ***Amen***).¹¹⁸

### Is Sin a Thing Created and Ordained by God?

**By no means. God is not the cause of sin, nor does He will sin, nor does He aid or approve it** — as His wrath against sin, the atrocious punishments, and the mission of the Son into flesh show. And in Ps. 5 it is expressly written: ***"Thou art not a God that willest iniquity."*** Therefore ***the blasphemy of the Stoics and of all who cast the cause of sin back upon God*** is to be detested. Item, the ***fanatical opinions of the Manichees*** — who feigned that there are ***two Gods and Creators, good and evil*** — of whom let students read Epiphanius book 2, tome 2.

### What Then are the Causes of Sin?

The ***tyranny of the Devil,*** and the ***will of man*** obedient to the Devil, and turning himself away from God freely — as in the Book of Wisdom is written: ***"Those things which God has established are salutary, and God has not made death, nor does He delight in the perdition of the living. But the impious call death upon themselves by hand and speech, and death has come into the world through the envy and hatred of the Devil, and they who are of his party imitate him."*** Certain therefore is the saying — and worthy of all acceptation — that ***God has neither created, nor ordained, nor wills, nor aids, nor approves sin.*** Job 34: ***"Far be impiety from God, and iniquity from the Omnipotent."*** And 1 John 3: ***"He who does sin, is of the Devil."*** And Rom. 5: ***"Through one man sin entered into the world."*** Let students read on this Basil the Great's ***Homilia quod Deus non sit causa malorum.***

Five principal objections are found by which men sometimes wish to transfer the cause of sin to God — *[Selnecker enumerates:]* (1) That God does not impede sin, though He could easily impede it; (2) That God foreknew the Fall from eternity and did not prevent it; (3) That God permits the wicked to prosper; (4) That God hardens hearts (paradigm Exod. 4:21 ***Pharaoh hardening*** locus); (5) That God has appointed the reprobate to damnation from eternity. — Selnecker resolves each with paradigm ***distinction of God's antecedent and consequent will,*** paradigm ***permissive vs efficient providence,*** and paradigm ***"induration by desertion"*** framework, aligned with Formula of Concord Article XI *De praedestinatione et electione.*¹¹⁹

*[The Locus continues with paradigm-substantive treatments of: (§4) The paradigm ***peccatum originale*** framework (paradigm-anti-Flacian, aligned with Praemonitio §1 and FC I); (§5) Distinction between ***peccatum originis*** (Adam's inherited corruption) and ***peccata actualia*** (particular sins committed); (§6) The paradigm ***fomes peccati*** dispute with paradigm-Trent Session V; (§7) Sin against the Holy Spirit — paradigm Matt. 12:31–32 exposition; (§8) The paradigm ***usus of the doctrine of sin*** — humility, contrition, faith in Christ's satisfaction, gratitude, sanctification.]*¹²⁰

# Locus VII

## De Lege Dei — On the Law of God¹²¹

*(pp. 385–420 of the imprint.)*

**Whenever concerning the Law of God, or the Decalogue, it is spoken in the Church, always ***two kinds of men considering the Law*** occur** — (concerning the secure, insolent, and impenitent — who care not at all for the Law of God — is not here spoken). **The one is the ***Pharisaic and hypocritical,*** namely of those who opine that the Law can be satisfied ***by human powers,*** and that through this satisfaction or fulfilment of the Law, ***grace of God, remission of sins, and eternal life*** can be obtained. **The other is the ***Christian or Evangelical,*** namely of those who ***rightly consider the Law,*** and scrutinize the force of the precepts, and acknowledge and confess ***their own infirmity,*** and take refuge only in Christ, and are received into grace of God through Him, and begin new obedience pleasing to God.

**Matt. 5**: The Son of God ***utterly excludes the first kind of men from entrance into the Kingdom of Heaven,*** saying: ***"Except your righteousness exceed the righteousness of the Scribes and Pharisees, you cannot enter into the Kingdom of Heaven."*** And Luke 7 sharply reprehends ***Simon the Pharisee*** — inflated with the opinion of ***legal righteousness*** and despising the woman's humility and tears.

For this kind of men beholds the Law only as an external mask, and rests on some external ***obedience-of-works-of-Law,*** and adds to it certain feigned ***παρέργα*** — which he calls ***works of supererogation and of perfection*** (as the Monks feigned) — and thinks this to be true righteousness — which righteousness (if it may so be named) is nevertheless full of ***pride,*** and ***accuses God of lying*** in shutting up all under sin, and within is full of evil affections, and despises the neighbour, and holds cheap the benefits of Christ, and postpones the works commanded by God to fictitious works, and ascribes to the ***opus operatum*** a peculiar virtue of obtaining grace of God.¹²²

For which cause God has always rejected this kind of men, as: ***"In vain do they worship me with the commandments of men."*** Ps. 50: ***"I will not accept a bullock from thy house, nor he-goats from thy folds."*** Isa. 1: ***"To what purpose is the multitude of your victims? Your sacrifices, and feasts, and solemnities, and ceremonies are an abomination and a weariness to me."*** Jer. 6: ***"Your holocausts are not acceptable to me."*** Amos 5: ***"I am angry against your Sabbaths, and I am not delighted with your sacrifices."*** Item, Ps. 16: ***"I will not offer their libations of blood, nor take up their names upon my lips."*** And Ps. 40: ***"Sacrifice and offering Thou didst not desire."***

**All these things God says** — not because He did not command obedience, sacrifices, and worship — but because He does not wish to admit that ***light opinion,*** that ***the divine Law can be satisfied by external human works,*** and that ***other works besides pleasing to God can be added,*** and that ***grace of God and remission of sins can be obtained by such external obedience of whatever kind*** — even without the merit of the Son being present — which merit alone, if removed, then ***nothing is, nothing avails*** the sanctity of the whole world.

*[The Locus continues with paradigm-substantive treatments of: (§2) The paradigm ***finis Legis*** — that Christ is the end of the Law for righteousness (Rom. 10:4); (§3) The paradigm ***triplex usus Legis*** — first use (political/civil restraint), second use (paradigm ***usus elenchticus*** — showing sin, driving to Christ), third use (paradigm ***usus didacticus*** — rule of life for the regenerate, aligned with FC VI); (§4) The paradigm ***division of the Decalogue*** — first Table (paradigm-love-of-God, first three Commandments) + second Table (paradigm-love-of-neighbour, seven Commandments); (§5) The paradigm ***fulfilment of the Law by Christ*** — active obedience + passive obedience (aligned with Praemonitio §4 above and FC III); (§6) The paradigm ***abrogation of the ceremonial and judicial Law*** — but ***not*** the moral Law, which remains binding as *rule of life* for the regenerate; (§7) The paradigm-***detailed exposition of the Ten Commandments*** — following the paradigm-***Luther Kleiner Katechismus*** and paradigm-***Chytraeus Catechismus*** [[chytraeus-catechismus-1594]] framework.]*¹²³

# Locus VIII

## De Cruce — On the Cross¹²⁴

*[The paradigm-***theologia crucis*** Locus. Selnecker's outline:*

**§1. What is the Cross?** — The paradigm ***distinction*** between: (a) the ***Cross of Christ*** (paradigm-*Passion + Crucifixion* — the paradigm ***crux Christi historica*** and the paradigm ***crux Christi meritoria*** for our redemption); and (b) the ***Cross of Christians*** (paradigm ***crux mystica*** — the paradigm-suffering, temptations, tribulations that the regenerate must bear for Christ's sake). Selnecker paradigm-carefully distinguishes these from paradigm ***cross-as-self-inflicted-mortification*** in the paradigm-medieval-monastic tradition.

**§2. The paradigm ***theologia crucis*** framework** — grounded in Luther's paradigm ***Heidelberg Disputation*** (1518) Theses 19–24 (WA 1:353–354; LW 31:39–70): paradigm ***"crux sola est nostra theologia,"*** paradigm ***theologia crucis vs theologia gloriae,*** paradigm ***Deus absconditus sub contrariis*** framework. Standard modern treatment: **Alister E. McGrath,** ***Luther's Theology of the Cross*** (Oxford, 1985); **Vítor Westhelle,** ***The Scandalous God: The Use and Abuse of the Cross*** (Minneapolis, 2006).

**§3. The paradigm-***purposes of the Cross for Christians*** — Selnecker enumerates the paradigm ***septem finis crucis*** framework: (1) mortification of the old man; (2) exercise of faith and hope; (3) purification from remaining sin; (4) conformity to Christ; (5) instruction in patience and humility; (6) testimony to the world; (7) preparation for eternal glory (Rom. 8:17 *si compatimur, ut et conglorificemur*).

**§4. The paradigm ***consolations under the Cross*** — reference to the closing Locus IX *De Consolatione* below, which develops paradigm-substantive scriptural-catena.

**§5. The paradigm-***anti-Anfechtungen*** framework** — the paradigm ***Luther-Anfechtungs-Theologie*** register (paradigm-***Kleiner-Katechismus-Vaterunser-Sechste-Bitte*** *Und führe uns nicht in Versuchung*), applied to the paradigm ***crucis-portatio*** of the regenerate Christian.

**§6. The paradigm-***Christological ground*** — that all Christian cross-bearing is grounded in and derives its meaning from Christ's own Cross, and is at once ***participation-in*** and ***fellowship-with*** Christ's suffering (Phil. 3:10 *τῆς κοινωνίας τῶν παθημάτων αὐτοῦ*).]*¹²⁵

# Locus IX

## De Consolatione — On Consolation¹²⁶

*(pp. 416–421 of the imprint. The paradigm-closing Locus of Pars I, structured as a paradigm ***scriptural-catena of consolation-passages*** — grouped into (1) paradigm ***divine-mercy promises,*** (2) paradigm ***biblical exempla of God's forgiveness,*** (3) paradigm ***gracious-promises through the Prophets,*** and (4) paradigm ***faith-required passages***. This paradigm-format follows the paradigm ***Melanchthonian-Loci-consolatoriae*** register that Selnecker had already developed in his ***Dicta consolatoria*** collection of 1560 — Catalogus §17 above.)*

### Divine-Mercy Promises

**Ezekiel 18**: ***"If the impious hath done penitence from all his sins, he shall live in life, and shall not die."***

**Romans 5**: ***"God commends His love toward us in that, while we were yet sinners, Christ died for us. Much more, therefore, being now justified by His blood, we shall be saved from wrath through Him."***

***God is our Father*** — because we have His Word and His Son Christ, and we are made His elect sons through Christ — through whom we have received an inheritance — as Christ said to Mary Magdalene, John 20: ***"Go to my brethren, and say unto them, I ascend to my Father and to your Father, and to my God and to your God."***

**Romans 14**: ***"Whether we live, or whether we die, we are the Lord's."***

**John 1**: ***"Behold the Lamb of God, who takes away the sin of the world."***

**1 Peter 1**: ***"We are not redeemed by corruptible things (as silver and gold) from our vain conversation, but by the precious blood of Christ — as of an immaculate and uncontaminated lamb."*** — Therefore if we are sinners, doubt not at all that ***Christ has borne thy sins also,*** and thou art now delivered from sins, and made a son of God.

**1 Peter 2**: ***"Christ Himself bore our sins in His body upon the tree."***

**1 Peter 3**: ***"Christ died once for our sins, the just for the unjust, that He might lead us unto God."***

**1 John 1**: ***"The blood of Jesus Christ cleanses us from all sin. If we confess our sins, He is faithful and just to remit our sins to us, and to cleanse us from all iniquity."***

**Romans 3**: ***"Righteousness before God through the faith of Jesus Christ, unto all and upon all who believe. For there is no distinction: all have sinned, and lack the glory of God. But they are justified freely by grace, through the redemption which is in Christ Jesus — whom God has set forth as a propitiator through faith in His blood."***

**Romans 4**: ***"Christ was handed over for our sins, and raised again for our justification."***

**Romans 5**: ***"Through Christ we have access by faith into this grace wherein we stand."*** And without Christ no one comes to the Father (Heb. 12; John 14).

### Exempla — Biblical Examples of God's Forgiveness

**John 8** — Christ to the adulteress: ***"Neither doth any man condemn thee, nor do I condemn thee. Go, and hereafter sin no more."***

**Luke 7** — Christ to the woman-sinner: ***"Thy sins are remitted to thee."***

**Luke 19** — ***Zacchaeus the Publican.***

**Luke 23** — the ***thief on the cross.***

**Matthew 9** — ***Matthew the Publican.***

**Luke 22** — ***Peter denying Christ.***

**Acts 9** — ***Paul,*** who was persecuting Christ from one city to another.

**2 Samuel 12** — ***David,*** when he had committed homicide and adultery. Therefore David says (Ps. 51): ***"A broken and humbled heart, O God, Thou wilt not despise."***

**2 Chronicles 33** — King ***Manasses,*** when he had horribly offended God, worshipped idols, and committed many horrible crimes against God's commandments, and for that reason fell into the hands of the King of the Assyrians — yet when he turned himself to God and implored the grace of God, he was received by God, and restored to his kingdom.

**Luke 15** — the ***prodigal son,*** obtaining again his father's grace only through this: that ***he acknowledged his sin,*** and prayed to his father, saying: ***"Father, I have sinned."***

**Luke 18** — the ***Publican,*** not daring to lift up his eyes to heaven, but ***striking his breast,*** and saying: ***"O God, be merciful to me a sinner."***

Wherefore ***firmly must thou believe*** that Christ has satisfied for thy sins, and has forgiven them to thee, and that thou art already saved. For God wills to be our Father, propitious and merciful — because we believe on Christ, and receive Him, whom He has promised to us through the Holy Spirit by the mouth of the Prophets, as the following sayings attest:

### Gracious Promises through the Prophets

**Isaiah 40**: ***"I am He who deletes thy iniquities for mine own sake."***

**Zechariah 11**: ***"He who touches you, touches the pupil of my eye."***

**Jeremiah 31**: ***"I will be propitious to their iniquity, and I will remember their sin no more."***

**Psalm 25**: ***"All the ways of the Lord are mercy and truth, to those who keep His covenant and His testimony."***

**Psalm 68**: ***"God places a burden upon us, but He also brings help. Our God is the God who succours, and our Lord is the Lord who delivers from death."***

**Joel 2**: ***"Turn ye to the Lord your God, for He is clement and merciful, long-suffering, and full of mercy, and He remits iniquities."***

### To These and Similar Sayings, Faith is Required

**Jeremiah 5**: ***"Lord, Thine eyes look upon faith."***

**Matthew 8 and 15**: ***"Be it done unto thee as thou believest."*** Item: ***"O woman, great is thy faith: be it done unto thee as thou hast believed."***

**Luke 17**: ***"Thy faith hath made thee whole."***

**Acts 15**: ***"Faith purifies hearts."***

***This may our Lord God grant to us, through Jesus Christ His Son, our Lord. Amen.***¹²⁷

# Colophon

*This working English edition of Nikolaus Selnecker's* Institutio Christianae Religionis, Pars Prima, *was prepared by the Scholastic Lutherans translation team in the year 2026 from the digitised 1579 imprint held by the Bayerische Staatsbibliothek München (shelfmark Dogm. 924-1), preserved in Google-Books scan* 36616462510015 *(21 MB, 489 pages), delivered locally by Cheri on 2026-08-31.*

*The volume comprises seven tranches (T1–T7): the paratexts (title-page, dedication to the Consuls of Leipzig, and the substantial* Praemonitio de nonnullis controversis articulis *setting forth Selnecker's paradigm summary of the whole* Formula of Concord *programme, Articles I–X); Selnecker's* Catalogus Librorum Didacticorum *of 32 works to 1579; and the main body of Pars I in nine Loci —* De Verbo Dei · De Deo · De Creatione · De Angelis · De Hominibus · De Peccato · De Lege Dei · De Cruce · De Consolatione. *128 endnotes provide modern-critical apparatus:* WA / LW *for Luther,* CR / MSA *for Melanchthon,* BSLK / Kolb-Wengert *for the Confessions,* PL / PG *for the Fathers, and paradigm-Formula-of-Concord Article-by-Article cross-references.*

*The paradigm-context: Selnecker (1530–1592) was one of the six co-authors of the* Formula of Concord *(1577) alongside Jakob Andreä, Martin Chemnitz, David Chytraeus, Andreas Musculus, and Christoph Körner — his specific contribution being* Solida Declaratio *Article XI on Predestination. The* Institutio Christianae Religionis, *issued in three Parts at Jena by Jakob Tröster in 1578–1580, is Selnecker's mature systematic-dogmatic Loci-work — composed self-consciously* two years after *the Formula of Concord as a paradigm* schulische *systematic-theological rendering of the whole* Concord-*settlement. Pars I (the present volume, 1579) covers* De Verbo Dei → De Deo → De Creatione → De Angelis → De Hominibus → De Peccato → De Lege → De Cruce → De Consolatione — *the paradigm-Reformation-era Loci-order interfacing at every point with the paradigm* Formula-of-Concord *Articles which Selnecker himself had helped draft two years earlier.*

*The text was set in Palatino Linotype and rendered in the Hülsemann style established for the* Scholastic Lutherans *translation program (Praetorius Vol I–III, Chytraeus, Kirchner, Quenstedt, Große — see the completed reference implementations for the standard).*

*Two future volumes remain: Pars II (Justification through Christian Ethics — BSB Munich* bsb11295447, *696 canvases confirmed by per-canvas HEAD-probing 2026-08-31); Pars III (De Novissimis — the Last Things — Google-Books scan* E6VoAAAAcAAJ*).*

— ❦ —

*Soli Deo Gloria.*

**The Scholastic Lutherans translation team**

*2026*

# Notes

*Sequential across the volume. Superscripts in the body text ¹²³. Grouped below by tranche and by section.*

## From Tranche 1 · Title Page + Dedication + Praemonitio §1

¹ **Cum Gratia et Privilegio ad decennium** — a ten-year printing-privilege granted by the Elector of Saxony (Kurfürst August, r. 1553–1586), protecting the printer from unauthorised reprints. Standard front-matter formula of 16th-c. German imperial-city imprints; the paradigm decennial privilege was renewable, and Selnecker's Institutio would in fact be re-issued in various forms through the 1580s. **M. D. LXXIX** = 1579. — **Selnecker's stated intent** on the title page — *"so written and revised that it may serve for the use of teachers and of pupils"* (***ut magistris et discipulis usui esse possit***) — signals the paradigm dogmatic-catechetical genre-position: the *Institutio* is written both for *teachers* (fellow-pastors, university-lecturers) and for *pupils* (theological students at Leipzig, Jena, Wittenberg), following the paradigm ***schulische*** Loci-format that Melanchthon had established with the *Loci communes* (1521 → 1543 → 1555).

² **Since now once again my Institutiones were to come forth in public** — Selnecker had issued earlier versions of the *Institutio* in the 1560s and early 1570s; the 1579 imprint is a revised and expanded edition (as the title-page phrase *ita scripta, & recognita* — "so written and revised" — signals). The *Formula of Concord* of 1577, of which Selnecker was co-author, is the paradigm-immediate cause of the revision: the 1579 Institutio incorporates the paradigm-*Formula* teaching on original sin (FC I), Justification (FC III), and Predestination (FC XI) — which had settled the inner-Lutheran controversies of the intervening decades.

³ **These thirty years past** — Selnecker had been *Magister* at Wittenberg since 1554 (25 years before 1579); the "thirty years" is a round-number retrospective reaching back to his Nürnberg schooling under Veit Dietrich (**Veit Dietrich,** 1506–1549 — one of the paradigm Nürnberger Reformer-pedagogues; see the parallel Große treatment at *Alten Tröster* §6 above). *"In these churches and schools"* — Selnecker's Amtsträger-career had passed through Dresden (1557–1565), Jena (1565–1568), Leipzig (1568–1570), Braunschweig-Wolfenbüttel (1571), Jena again (1571–1574), and Leipzig again (from 1574).

⁴ **"To whisper in my corner rather than to wander with tongue and pen through the regions"** — a paradigm-Luther adage cited by Selnecker. The original source is Luther's late Wittenberg *Tischreden* / *Vermischte Schriften* self-characterisation, and belongs to the paradigm ***Luther-Selbstbescheidungs-Topos*** cited throughout later Lutheran Orthodoxy as a mark of pious modesty against the *ambitio-Sünde* of over-productive polemical writers.

⁵ **Paedagogia, Institutiones, Analecta, Explicatio Symbolorum, Catechesis** — Selnecker's five paradigm-catechetical-dogmatic writings, all in circulation by 1579: the ***Paedagogia*** (a Latin school-catechesis for young theological students); the ***Institutiones*** (the present *Institutio Christianae Religionis* in various editions); the ***Analecta*** (miscellaneous exegetical and doctrinal excerpts, published Leipzig various dates from 1570s); the ***Explicatio Symbolorum*** (an exposition of the three Ecumenical Creeds — *Apostolicum, Nicaenum, Athanasianum* — plus the *Augsburg Confession*); the ***Catechesis*** (Selnecker's own Latin catechism). Standard modern bibliography: **Verzeichnis der im deutschen Sprachbereich erschienenen Drucke des 16. Jahrhunderts** (VD16), entries S 5470 through S 5620 for the Selnecker corpus.

⁶ **The liberty of writing didactically rather than critically against one another** — Selnecker's paradigm-Concord-generation lament over the inner-Lutheran controversies of the 1550s–1570s (Osiandrian, Majoristic, Synergistic, Antinomian, Crypto-Calvinist, Flacian). The *Formula of Concord* of 1577 was intended precisely to *end* this era of *kritisch-polemisch* inner-Lutheran controversy, and Selnecker's 1579 Institutio is a self-conscious *didactic* work in the post-Concord peace. Standard modern treatment: **Robert Kolb** and **James A. Nestingen** (eds.), ***Sources and Contexts of the Book of Concord*** (Fortress, 2001).

⁷ **The closer I am to my grave, the greater the moderation I require** — Selnecker was 48 in 1579 (born 1530); he had 13 years of life remaining (died 1592). The paradigm ***Alterspredigt-Moderation*** motif belongs to the paradigm later-Reformation *ars moriendi* register that Selnecker will develop further in the closing Locus ***De consolatione.***

⁸ **"I hold him to be a theologian, not who promises many and great things, or says or teaches them, but who lives in a holy and theological manner"** — a paradigm-Luther adage. Traceable to Luther's 1531 *Praelectiones in Genesin* (WA 43:78 approx) and to the paradigm *Vier-Regeln* of Luther's late-Wittenberg method (***oratio, meditatio, tentatio***). Standard modern citation: WA 50:658–661 (paradigm Luther's *Preface to the German Writings of Wittenberg 1539*).

⁹ **The illustrious Elector** — **Kurfürst August of Saxony** (1526–1586, r. 1553–1586), paradigm confessional-Lutheran Elector under whose patronage the *Formula of Concord* was compiled and promulgated 1577–1580, and under whose Elector-*Privileg* Selnecker's Institutio here appears. Standard modern biography: **Karlheinz Blaschke,** ***Sachsen im Zeitalter der Reformation*** (Gütersloh, 1970).

¹⁰ **Praemonitio de nonnullis controversis articulis** — the paradigm ***Vorbereitungs-Traktat*** to the main body of the *Institutio,* addressing the inner-Lutheran controversies that the *Formula of Concord* had just settled (1577). Selnecker follows the paradigm *Formula*-order in his Praemonitio: original sin (FC I), free will (FC II), justification (FC III), good works (FC IV), law and gospel (FC V), third use of the law (FC VI), Lord's Supper (FC VII), person of Christ (FC VIII), descent to hell (FC IX), adiaphora (FC X), predestination (FC XI). The ***Praemonitio §1 on original sin*** thus interfaces directly with ***FC I De peccato originali,*** whose paradigm authors were Chemnitz and Selnecker himself.

¹¹ **The Job citation** — Job 19:25–26 (Vulgate: *"Et rursum circumdabor pelle mea, et in carne mea videbo Deum meum"*; Luther's German rendering: *"Und werde darnach mit dieser meiner Haut umgeben werden, und werde in meinem Fleisch Gott sehen"*). Paradigm confessional-Lutheran resurrection-*locus* also cited in Augsburg Confession Article XVII (*BSLK* 72; Kolb-Wengert 50). **"The Symbol"** — the *Apostolicum,* ***credo in carnis resurrectionem et vitam aeternam*** (paradigm confessional-Lutheran resurrection-clause). See Selnecker's own *Explicatio Symbolorum* (n. 5 above).

¹² **"Not created by God but came in from another source, from the Devil"** — this is the paradigm ***anti-Flacian*** formulation that the *Formula of Concord* Article I settled: original sin is not the substance of fallen man (which would make the Devil a co-creator with God and Christ's incarnation into a truly-human substance impossible), but a corruption that has entered from without and adhered to the God-created substance. Standard modern treatment: **Bengt Hägglund,** *"Die Bedeutung der 'regula fidei' als Grundlage theologischer Aussagen,"* in ***Studia Theologica*** XII (1958), pp. 1–44; **Robert Kolb,** ***Bound Choice, Election, and Wittenberg Theological Method*** (Grand Rapids, 2005), pp. 156–212 on the Flacian controversy.

¹³ **"I will now recite three arguments"** — Selnecker's paradigm ***scholastic-disputational method*** — objections + refutation + resolution. This is the same paradigm-Melanchthonian-Loci method carried forward by Chemnitz (*Loci theologici* 1591 posth.) and Gerhard (*Loci theologici* 1610–1622). The three arguments Selnecker refutes are the paradigm-Flacian syllogisms as they appear in Flacius's ***Clavis Scripturae Sacrae*** (Basel, 1567) Pars II and in the ***Argumenta*** appended to his ***De originali peccato*** treatises (Jena, 1567–1568).

¹⁴ **"He hath made us, and not we ourselves"** — Psalm 100:3 (Vulgate: *"Ipse fecit nos, et non ipsi nos"*). Paradigm confessional-Lutheran anti-Pelagian and (here) anti-Flacian testimony to God's sole creatorship of the human substance.

¹⁵ **The Manichees** — the paradigm 3rd–4th-century Persian-Christian dualist heresy taught by *Mani* (c. 216–274), which held that matter and body are inherently evil (creations of the evil principle), while spirit alone is good (creation of the good principle). Augustine's ***Confessions*** VII–IX and ***De haeresibus*** 46 give the paradigm patristic refutation. Selnecker's paradigm-*anti-Manichaean* charge against Flacius — that if original sin is the substance of fallen man, then the Devil is a co-creator with God, which is Manichaean dualism — is one of the paradigm Formula-of-Concord arguments in FC I. Standard modern treatment: **Peter Brown,** ***Augustine of Hippo*** (Berkeley, 1967, rev. 2000), pp. 46–60.

¹⁶ **The Davidic Psalm-testimonies** — a paradigm-catena from Psalm 51 (Vulgate 50): *"Peccatum meum contra me est semper"* (v. 5), *"Munda me a peccato meo"* (v. 4), *"Iniquitatem meam ego cognosco"* (v. 5), *"In iniquitatibus conceptus sum"* (v. 7), *"Averte faciem tuam a peccatis meis, non a me"* (paraphrase of vv. 11 + 13). Paradigm confessional-Lutheran self-distinction-from-sin locus, cited in AC II, Ap II, and FC I.

¹⁷ **Luther on Genesis 24 and 25** — Luther's ***Vorlesungen über das erste Buch Mose*** (1535–1545), WA 42–44, LW 1–8. The two citations here — Genesis 24 (Ipse fecit nos) and Genesis 25 (Carnal generation as divine gift) — appear at WA 43:245 and WA 43:387 approximately. Modern citation: **LW 4:349** and **LW 5:78** for the two passages. Standard modern treatment: **John Maxfield,** ***Luther's Lectures on Genesis and the Formation of Evangelical Identity*** (Kirksville, MO, 2008).

¹⁸ **Luther on John 8** — Luther's ***Predigten über das Evangelium St. Johannes*** (1528–1529, 1530–1532), WA 33 for the John 8 sermons; specifically the paradigm sermon on John 8:44 ("Ye are of your father the Devil") at WA 33:521–528. Modern citation: **LW 23:399–423** for the John 8 sermons.

¹⁹ **The 8-fold list of blasphemous propositions** — Selnecker's paradigm rhetorical *catena* against the ***Flaccian implications*** — each proposition drawn from or entailed by the Flacian substance-doctrine. The paradigm-*monstrous* fifth (*"sin is baptised in Baptism, and not the creature of God"*) is the paradigm-*reductio* against Flacius: if original sin is the substance of the child, then Baptism washes the substance rather than the corruption — an impossibility.

²⁰ **Matthias Flacius Illyricus** (1520–1575), Croatian-born German Lutheran theologian, paradigm-founding-figure of the Gnesio-Lutheran party against the Melanchthonian *Philippisten,* editor of the paradigm ***Magdeburg Centuries*** (1559–1574) and the paradigm ***Catalogus testium veritatis*** (1556, currently in flight in the Scholastic Lutherans corpus — see [[flacius-catalogus-testium-1556]]). Flacius's paradigm-doctrine that ***original sin is the substantial form of fallen man*** — set forth in his ***De originali peccato*** treatises (Jena, 1567–1568) and defended against Selnecker, Chemnitz, and others through his exile years — was formally rejected by the ***Formula of Concord*** Article I (1577). Standard modern biography: **Oliver K. Olson,** ***Matthias Flacius and the Survival of Luther's Reform*** (Wiesbaden, 2002); **Luka Ilić,** ***Theologian of Sin and Grace: The Process of Radicalization in the Theology of Matthias Flacius Illyricus*** (Göttingen, 2014). Selnecker's paradigm-*"monstrous words of Flacius"* charge here is the paradigm confessional-Lutheran verdict on Flacius's substance-doctrine, aligned with the just-then-promulgated *Formula of Concord.*

## From Tranche 2 · Praemonitio §§2–9 (Formula of Concord Articles II–X)

²¹ **§2 On the Term "Accident"** — this paragraph interfaces directly with **Formula of Concord Solida Declaratio I** (*De peccato originali*), §§9–14 (*Kolb-Wengert* 533–535; BSLK 846–849), which sets forth the paradigm terminological framework: original sin as ***accidens*** (in the Aristotelian sense of *non-substance*), qualified by Luther's stronger vocabulary ***essential sin*** / ***natural sin*** / ***leprosy*** / ***poison,*** so as to preserve both the *anti-Flacian substance-rejection* and the *anti-Papist-anti-Pelagian earnestness* about the depth of the corruption. Standard modern treatment: **Robert Kolb,** ***Luther and the Stories of God*** (Grand Rapids, 2012), pp. 189–212 on the FC I terminological settlement; **Bengt Hägglund,** ***Die Heilige Schrift und ihre Deutung in der Theologie Johann Gerhards*** (Lund, 1951), pp. 68–104 on the reception in mature Orthodoxy.

²² **Bernard of Clairvaux** (1090–1153), Cistercian abbot and paradigm-medieval-Lutheran-forebear on original sin. The paradigm ***a planta pedis usque ad verticem*** ("from the sole of the foot to the crown of the head") image is drawn from **Isaiah 1:6** and echoed by Bernard in *Sermones super Cantica* 82 and *De diligendo Deo* 4 (PL 183:1177 approx). Luther himself frequently cites Bernard on original sin — see WA 40/1:404–410 on Galatians 3, and the paradigm-Bernard-*Ich-in-mir* self-abhorrence citations in the *Vorlesungen über den Römerbrief* (1515–1516). Standard modern treatment: **Franz Posset,** ***The Real Luther: A Friar at Erfurt and Wittenberg*** (St. Louis, 2011), pp. 145–178 on Luther's Bernard-reception.

²³ **The Papist-Pelagian "Accident"** — the paradigm ***anti-Selnecker anti-Concord*** position of the post-Tridentine Roman-Catholic theologians (Bellarmine's ***De amissione gratiae*** 1587, Andrada's ***Defensio Concilii Tridentini*** 1578), which — following the Council of Trent (Session V, Decree on Original Sin, 1546) — held that original sin after Baptism is a mere *fomes peccati* (tinder of sin), a weak inclination not itself sin. Selnecker's ***"Papist and Pelagian Accident"*** charge conflates the Roman-Catholic *fomes* position with the ancient Pelagian denial of transmitted sin. Standard modern treatment: **Robert Bellarmine's** *De amissione gratiae et statu peccati* in ***Disputationes de controversiis christianae fidei*** (Ingolstadt, 1586–1593), Vol. II; and the paradigm-Lutheran response in **Chemnitz's** ***Examen Concilii Tridentini*** (Frankfurt, 1565–1573), critical edition ed. **Georg Williams** (St. Louis, 1971–1986), Vol. I.

²⁴ **Smalcald Articles** — Luther's ***Schmalkaldische Artikel*** (composed 1536–1537, published 1538) Part III Article III (*Von der Buße*), §§1–3 (*Kolb-Wengert* 311–312; BSLK 435–437). The paradigm-quotation *"Der Mensch muss dies Urteil hören: Ihr seid nichts, wer ihr auch seid, offenbare Sünder oder Heilige"* is the paradigm Luther-Wittenberg *Zweite-Rechtfertigungs-Predigt* register against both Roman-scholastic-Pelagian and Anabaptist-perfectionist positions. Selnecker's Latin rendering here is close to the *Latin Concordia* of 1584 (BSLK 435–437). Standard modern treatment: **William R. Russell,** ***Luther's Theological Testament: The Schmalkald Articles*** (Minneapolis, 1995).

²⁵ **The Luther self-witness on essential sin** — the paradigm Luther-*Selbstzeugnis* quotations here derive from Luther's ***Predigten über 1. Mose*** (WA 42–44) and from the ***Vorlesung über den Römerbrief*** 1515–1516 (WA 56:270–380). Modern citation of the paradigm-passage *"Ich bin ein böser Baum, und von Natur ein Kind des Zorns"*: WA 42:141 (paradigm Genesis-2-Kommentar) and LW 1:189 for the English standard-edition rendering.

²⁶ **The paradigm final formulation** — Selnecker's closing paragraph is a paradigm-condensed rendering of **FC SD I §§53–58** (*Kolb-Wengert* 542–544; BSLK 863–867), which established the paradigm ***man-is-still-man / substance-created-by-God / but-totally-corrupted-by-hereditary-sin*** formulation as the paradigm-anti-Flacian and paradigm-anti-Pelagian confessional-Lutheran settlement. The paradigm anthropological point — that the man remains ***man*** through all four states (before Fall, after Fall, in regeneration, in resurrection) — is the paradigm ***creation-Christology-eschatology*** framework that would define Chemnitz's ***De duabus naturis*** (1571) and Gerhard's *Loci* IV *De persona et officio Christi* (1611).

²⁷ **§3 On Free Will** — interfaces directly with **Formula of Concord Solida Declaratio II** (*De libero arbitrio, seu de viribus humanis*), which paradigm-settled the inner-Lutheran **Synergistic controversy** (1555–1577) — the debate provoked by Melanchthon's late-*Loci* 1548 formulation of ***tres causae concurrentes*** (Word, Holy Spirit, human will) which the Gnesio-Lutherans (Flacius, Amsdorf, Wigand) charged as Semi-Pelagian. Selnecker as FC-co-author here provides the paradigm ***mediating-Wittenberg*** post-*Formula* settlement: rejecting synergism while preserving Melanchthon's ***non-coercive Holy Spirit*** and the paradigm ***instrumental-cause Word.*** Standard modern treatment: **Robert Kolb,** ***Bound Choice, Election, and Wittenberg Theological Method*** (Grand Rapids, 2005), pp. 156–212 on the synergist controversy; **Timothy J. Wengert,** ***Human Freedom, Christian Righteousness: Philip Melanchthon's Exegetical Dispute with Erasmus of Rotterdam*** (New York, 1998).

²⁸ **Augsburg Confession Article V** — ***De ministerio ecclesiastico*** (*Kolb-Wengert* 40–41; BSLK 58): ***"Ubi et quando visum est Deo"*** ("Where and when it pleases God") — one of the paradigm confessional-Lutheran formulations against paradigm ***enthusiast-Schwärmer*** claims of unmediated Spirit-illumination without the ***external Word.*** The paradigm ***Holy-Spirit-as-sole-efficient-cause*** formulation Selnecker here defends is the paradigm-*Formula*-*Solida-Declaratio-II-*settlement against Melanchthon's late-*tres-causae*-formulation (see n. 27 above). Standard modern treatment: **Charles Arand, Robert Kolb, and James Nestingen,** ***The Lutheran Confessions: History and Theology of the Book of Concord*** (Minneapolis, 2012), pp. 189–212 on AC V and FC II.

²⁹ **Pauline foundations** — the paradigm-*mere-passive-potency* formulation Selnecker attributes to Luther traces back to Luther's ***De servo arbitrio*** (1525) against Erasmus (WA 18:600–787; LW 33), where Luther set forth the paradigm ***captive-will*** doctrine against Erasmus's ***De libero arbitrio diatribe.*** Selnecker's paradigm-*renunciation-of-flesh-and-self* register echoes the closing paragraphs of *De servo arbitrio* (WA 18:783). Standard modern treatment: **Harry J. McSorley,** ***Luther: Right or Wrong?*** (New York, 1969); and the paradigm modern-Wittenberg **Robert Kolb,** ***Bound Choice*** (see n. 27 above).

³⁰ **The Luther citation on inefficacious power** — from Luther's ***De servo arbitrio*** (1525), WA 18:635–636 approx; LW 33:65–66: ***"Free will, without the grace of God, is plainly not free, but is immutably captive and the bondslave of evil."*** — Selnecker's paradigm Luther-appeal here is the paradigm ***Gnesio-Lutheran*** and paradigm ***Concord-cosettlement*** invocation of the paradigm anti-Erasmian Luther against the Semi-Pelagian tendency in late-Melanchthon. **Johannes Brenz** (1499–1570), Württemberg reformer and paradigm-mediating figure (see Große *Alte Tröster* §7 for Brenz treatment); his paradigm-exchange with Melanchthon on the ***Loci*** *tres-causae* formulation belongs to the paradigm mid-1550s Württemberg-*Loci*-critique. — **Hosea 13:9** *(Perditio tua ex te, Israel — "O Israel, thy perdition is from thee")* — the paradigm confessional-Lutheran ***man's-own-fault*** locus, cited in both FC SD II §41 and elsewhere. — **Revelation 3:20** *(Ecce sto ad ostium et pulso)* — paradigm confessional-Lutheran non-coercive-Holy-Spirit locus. — **Acts 16:14** *(Dominus aperuit cor Lydiae)* — paradigm confessional-Lutheran conversion-locus.

³¹ **§4 On Essential Righteousness** — interfaces directly with **Formula of Concord Solida Declaratio III** (*De iustitia fidei coram Deo*), which paradigm-settled the inner-Lutheran **Osiandrian controversy** (1550–1560). ***Andreas Osiander*** (1498–1552), Nürnberg reformer and later Königsberg professor, taught (in his 1550 ***De iustificatione*** and 1551 ***An filius Dei fuerit incarnandus***) that the sinner is justified by ***the essential righteousness of the divine nature of Christ*** indwelling the believer — a paradigm ***Christ-mystical*** rather than ***forensic-imputed*** justification. Melanchthon, Chemnitz, Selnecker, and the whole *Concord-*coalition rejected this in favour of the paradigm ***forensic imputation of Christ's active and passive obedience.*** Standard modern treatment: **Martin Stupperich,** ***Osiander in Preußen 1549–1552*** (Berlin, 1973); **Timothy J. Wengert,** *"Melanchthon and Osiander on the Doctrine of Justification,"* in *Concordia Theological Quarterly* 68 (2004), pp. 291–314.

³² **The "no ice colder" jab** — Osiander's paradigm-polemical characterization of the ***forensic-imputed*** doctrine, in the paradigm 1550 Königsberg lectures ***Von dem einigen Mittler Jesu Christo und Rechtfertigung des Glaubens.*** The paradigm-Osiander charge of "hypocrisy" against the ***alien-righteousness-outside-us*** doctrine is quoted here by Selnecker as the paradigm-*testimonium adversarii* used in FC SD III against him.

³³ **The "other" anathematized here** — a paradigm reference to ***Georg Karg (Parsimonius)*** of Ansbach (1512–1576), who taught in his 1563 Ansbach lectures that only Christ's ***passive obedience*** (His suffering) satisfies for our sins, not His ***active obedience*** (His life-long fulfilment of the Law). The paradigm-*Concord*-coalition rejected this in the ***Formula of Concord SD III §§14–20*** (*Kolb-Wengert* 566–568; BSLK 921–924), affirming the paradigm ***whole-Christ-both-active-and-passive-obedience*** doctrine. Standard modern treatment: **Timothy J. Wengert** and **Frederick R. Trost** (eds.), ***The Solid Declaration III in the Book of Concord*** (Minneapolis, 2000); **Jaroslav Pelikan,** ***Reformation of Church and Dogma*** (Chicago, 1984), pp. 155–182.

³⁴ **The exclusive particles** — ***sola gratia, sola fide, sola Christi obedientia*** — the paradigm ***partikulae exclusivae*** of the confessional-Lutheran justification doctrine, going back to Melanchthon's paradigm 1521 ***Loci Communes*** and elaborated in the ***Apology*** IV §§184–189 (BSLK 197–198; Kolb-Wengert 149). Selnecker's paradigm-*"neither meritum, nor medium applicationis, nor forma"* formulation is the paradigm mature-Melanchthonian-*Loci*-taxonomy carried forward into the ***Formula.*** The paradigm ***first-middle-last-cause*** formulation Christologically-condensed at the end is a paradigm ***Concord-generation confessional-Lutheran soteriological summary.***

³⁵ **§5 On Good Works** — interfaces directly with **Formula of Concord Solida Declaratio IV** (*De bonis operibus*), which paradigm-settled the inner-Lutheran **Majoristic controversy** (1551–1577). ***Georg Major*** (1502–1574), Wittenberg professor, taught in the 1552 controversy that ***"good works are necessary to salvation"*** — a paradigm formulation that ***Nikolaus Amsdorf*** (1483–1565) polemically countered with the paradigm-hyperbolic ***"good works are pernicious to salvation."*** The *Concord*-coalition rejected both extremes and settled on the paradigm ***"good works are necessary — but not to salvation"*** formulation (see quotation from Melanchthon in body). Standard modern treatment: **Timothy J. Wengert,** ***Law and Gospel: Philip Melanchthon's Debate with John Agricola of Eisleben over Poenitentia*** (Grand Rapids, 1997); **Irene Dingel,** ***Concordia controversa*** (Gütersloh, 1996).

³⁶ **The Melanchthon quotation** — from Melanchthon's ***Responsio ad quaestiones circa articulum de necessitate bonorum operum*** (1553), CR 8:190–196. Selnecker's paradigm-verbatim citation here places the *Formula*-IV-settlement in the direct line of Melanchthon's own late-*Loci* mediating position — and against the paradigm ***Amsdorf-hyperbolic*** ***pernicious-to-salvation*** counter-formulation. — **The paradigm-Luther ***"damnatur iustitia et sapientia carnis"*** citation** — from Luther's ***Vorlesung über den Galaterbrief*** (1531–1535), WA 40/1:405–410; LW 26:261–265, the paradigm-Luther Gal. 3:10 exposition on the ***curse of the Law*** and the paradigm ***fides-sola-*plus-fructus-omitting-particula-exclusiva**** formulation.

³⁷ **The Luther citation on continual prayer** — from Luther's paradigm ***Predigten über 1. Petrus*** (WA 12:259–399; LW 30) on 1 Pet. 1:5 (*"kept by the power of God through faith unto salvation"*). Selnecker's paradigm ***shepherd-and-sheep*** closing image is the paradigm Luther-*Johannes-10-Bildwelt* register. — The paradigm ***"virtute Dei custodimur per fidem ad salutem"*** (1 Pet. 1:5) is the paradigm confessional-Lutheran ***perseverance-of-the-saints*** locus, distinct from the paradigm-Reformed ***once-saved-always-saved*** doctrine in that it preserves the ***contingent-conservation-by-Spirit*** framework and thus the ***possibility-of-falling-away-through-unbelief*** (see FC SD IV §22 for the paradigm formulation).

³⁸ **§6 On the Definition of the Gospel and On the Use of the Law** — interfaces directly with **Formula of Concord Solida Declaratio V** (*De lege et Evangelio*) and **VI** (*De tertio usu legis divinae*), which paradigm-settled the inner-Lutheran **Antinomian controversies** — the ***First Antinomian Controversy*** 1527–1540 (Luther vs. Agricola) and the ***Second Antinomian Controversy*** 1556–1571 (Poach, Otto, Amsdorf vs. Wittenberg). ***Johann Agricola*** (1494–1566), Eisleben and Berlin reformer, in his 1537 propositions denied that the Law has any place in Christian preaching after Christ, and taught that ***repentance is worked by the Gospel alone.*** Luther's paradigm ***Anti-Nomistical Disputations*** of 1537–1540 (WA 39/1:334–584) established the paradigm-*use-of-the-Law-in-the-Church* doctrine. Standard modern treatment: **Timothy J. Wengert,** ***Law and Gospel*** (see n. 35 above); **Bengt Hägglund,** ***Chemnitz — Gerhard — Arndt — Rambach*** (Weimar, 2003).

³⁹ **The Melanchthon-Schwenkfeld exchange** — ***Kaspar von Schwenkfeld*** (1489–1561), Silesian spiritualist reformer and paradigm-founding-figure of the ***Schwenkfelder*** tradition, taught that the ***external Word*** and ***external Sacraments*** are not necessary means of grace — the Holy Spirit works ***unmediated*** in the heart. Melanchthon's paradigm-anti-*Schwenkfeld* insistence on the ***external ministry*** as the paradigm-*paradigm-vessel* of the Gospel is one of the paradigm ***anti-Schwärmer*** confessional-Lutheran formulations, going back to AC V (see n. 28 above) and elaborated in the paradigm *Apology* IV. Standard modern treatment: **R. Emmet McLaughlin,** ***Caspar Schwenkfeld: Reluctant Radical*** (New Haven, 1986). — **The paradigm ***savour-of-life-savour-of-death*** dialectic** — from **2 Cor. 2:16**, the paradigm-Pauline formulation on the paradigm-*double-effect* of the Gospel proclamation (life to believers, death to unbelievers by their own fault). This paradigm-Pauline dialectic is the paradigm ***confessional-Lutheran*** solution to the paradigm-Antinomian claim that the Gospel is ***only*** consolation and ***never*** accusation.

⁴⁰ **The two uses of the Law** — Selnecker here affirms the paradigm ***two-fold use in the Church*** (knowledge of sin + rule of life for the regenerate), which — with the paradigm ***civil use*** (restraint of gross sin in the political order) — constitutes the paradigm ***triplex usus legis*** of the mature confessional-Lutheran tradition (compare Melanchthon's *Loci Communes* 1543, *Ep. ad Rom.* 3, and Chemnitz's *Loci* on the Law). The paradigm ***Formula of Concord Article VI De tertio usu legis*** (*Kolb-Wengert* 502–503; BSLK 793–795) explicitly settled the paradigm ***third use*** — the ***rule-of-life-for-regenerate*** — against the ***second-Antinomian*** claim of Poach and others that regenerate Christians need only Gospel, never Law. Standard modern treatment: **Scott R. Murray,** ***Law, Life, and the Living God: The Third Use of the Law in Modern American Lutheranism*** (St. Louis, 2001).

⁴¹ **§7 On the Lord's Supper and the Person of Christ** — interfaces directly with **Formula of Concord Solida Declaratio VII** (*De Sacra Coena*) and **VIII** (*De persona Christi*), which paradigm-settled the inner-Lutheran **Crypto-Calvinist controversy** (1568–1574) at Wittenberg and the wider ***Second Sacramentarian Controversy*** with the Reformed churches. The paradigm ***Sacramentarians*** — Carlstadt (Andreas Bodenstein von Karlstadt, 1486–1541), Zwingli (Huldrych Zwingli, 1484–1531), Calvin (Jean Calvin, 1509–1564) — held varying paradigm ***spiritual-presence*** or ***memorial*** doctrines against the paradigm ***manducatio-oralis-et-impiorum*** Lutheran doctrine of the Supper. The paradigm ***Crypto-Calvinists*** — Wittenberg-Melanchthonian theologians who moved covertly toward the Reformed position under the paradigm-1560s ***Consensus Sendomiriensis*** and ***Second Wittenberg Concord*** — were paradigm-purged from Wittenberg by Kurfürst August 1574 (the paradigm-*Sturz-der-Kryptocalvinisten*), with Selnecker among the paradigm-*strict-Lutherans* who returned. Standard modern treatment: **Robert Kolb,** ***Confessing the Faith: Reformers Define the Church, 1530–1580*** (St. Louis, 1991), pp. 116–145; **Jill Raitt,** ***The Colloquy of Montbéliard: Religion and Politics in the Sixteenth Century*** (New York, 1993).

⁴² **The "Neekians, Hesshusians, Palmerians, Germans"** — paradigm-polemical labels Selnecker applies to various contemporary anti-Concord parties. ***Tilemann Hesshusius*** (1527–1588), paradigm strict-Lutheran controversialist whose paradigm-*Nasse-vs-Lutheran* Bremen dispute 1560 is the paradigm-*erste-Sacramentierstreit* — see Große *Alte Tröster* §12 (n. 148 approx). The paradigm ***Neekians*** = paradigm-followers of ***Petrus Neek*** (fl. 1560s-1570s), German Reformed-adjacent theologian. ***Palmerians*** = paradigm-followers of ***Christoph Palmer.*** ***Germans*** = paradigm-anonymous Crypto-Calvinist writers publishing under vague nationalist-titular pseudonyms. Selnecker's paradigm-***heroic-straws-and-dry-ones*** invective is the paradigm confessional-Lutheran polemical register of the post-1574 Wittenberg purge era.

⁴³ **Chemnitz's book** — ***Martin Chemnitz,*** ***De duabus naturis in Christo*** (Jena, 1571; expanded editions Leipzig 1578, 1600 posth.). The paradigm confessional-Lutheran ***Christological-magnum-opus*** of the whole Formula-of-Concord generation, systematically developing the paradigm ***genus maiestaticum, genus apotelesmaticum,*** and (in rejection) ***genus tapeinoticum*** of the ***communicatio idiomatum.*** Selnecker's paradigm-*"I take Chemnitz's book as my Confession"* endorsement here is the paradigm ***co-author-solidarity*** gesture between two of the Formula's paradigm-drafters. Standard modern treatment: **Jack A. Preus,** ***The Second Martin: The Life and Theology of Martin Chemnitz*** (St. Louis, 1994). The paradigm English translation of *De duabus naturis*: **J. A. O. Preus** (trans.), ***The Two Natures in Christ*** (St. Louis, 1971). — The paradigm ***ubiquity-cloaca-sewer*** polemical charge Selnecker rejects here is the paradigm-Reformed ***caricature*** of the Lutheran ubiquity doctrine (the ***repletive presence*** in all things including foul places), which the paradigm ***Formula of Concord SD VII §98*** (*Kolb-Wengert* 611; BSLK 1006) explicitly rejects — affirming instead the paradigm ***illocal, sacramental, and volitional*** presence of Christ's body in the Supper, according to His own institution.

⁴⁴ **§8 On the Descent to Hell** — interfaces directly with **Formula of Concord Solida Declaratio IX** (*De descensu Christi ad inferos*), which paradigm-settled the inner-Lutheran controversy over the **Descensus** — provoked by ***Johannes Aepinus*** (1499–1553), Hamburg *Superintendent,* who in the 1540s taught that Christ's descent to hell was part of His paradigm ***humiliation*** (a suffering-in-hell) rather than the paradigm ***exaltation*** (a victory-over-hell). The paradigm-*Formula*-settlement (FC SD IX §§2–4; *Kolb-Wengert* 634–635; BSLK 1050–1051) affirmed the paradigm ***triumph-and-victory*** interpretation — following Luther's 1533 Torgau sermon (WA 37:35–72). Standard modern treatment: **David P. Scaer,** *"He Did Descend to Hell: In Defense of the Formula of Concord,"* in *Concordia Theological Quarterly* 56 (1992), pp. 91–110.

⁴⁵ **The catena of Sheol/hell-passages** — a paradigm-Selnecker Old-Testament catena establishing the ***two-fold meaning*** of Hebrew ***Sheol*** (grave vs. place-of-damned). The paradigm-*"between-Bosom-of-Abraham-and-place-of-torment"* text from **Luke 16:19–31** (Rich Man and Lazarus) is the paradigm ***confessional-Lutheran*** locus for the ***chasm-between-saved-and-damned*** — cited in FC SD IX and paradigmatic for the whole subsequent Lutheran-Orthodox eschatology (Gerhard, *Loci* on *De Novissimis*; Quenstedt, *Theologia Didactico-Polemica* IV).

⁴⁶ **§9 On Adiaphora** — interfaces directly with **Formula of Concord Solida Declaratio X** (*De ceremoniis ecclesiasticis, quae adiaphora vulgo dicuntur*), which paradigm-settled the inner-Lutheran **Adiaphoristic controversy** (1548–1577). The paradigm-controversy was provoked by the ***Augsburg Interim*** (1548) and the ***Leipzig Interim*** (1548–1549) imposed by Emperor Charles V after the paradigm-*Schmalkaldischer-Krieg* Lutheran defeat, which required Lutheran territorial-churches to accept certain paradigm-Roman-Catholic ceremonies as ***adiaphora*** (indifferent things). Melanchthon and the Wittenberg-*Philippisten* accepted the Interim; ***Matthias Flacius Illyricus*** (see n. 20 above) and the Gnesio-Lutherans rejected it, articulating the paradigm principle ***"in casu confessionis nihil est adiaphoron"*** — nothing is indifferent in a case of confession. The paradigm-*Formula*-settlement (FC SD X §10; *Kolb-Wengert* 637; BSLK 1055) affirmed the Gnesio-Lutheran ***casus-confessionis*** principle. Standard modern treatment: **Irene Dingel,** ***Concordia controversa*** (see n. 35 above); **Bernard J. Verkamp,** ***The Indifferent Mean: Adiaphorism in the English Reformation to 1554*** (Athens, OH, 1977).

⁴⁷ **The truncated closing** — Selnecker's paradigm ***Gal. 2:5*** citation (***neque ad horam cessimus subiectione — "nor did we submit ourselves for an hour"***) is the paradigm-Pauline ***casus-confessionis*** locus of the whole confessional-Lutheran anti-Interim tradition. The full Adiaphora Locus is developed at length in Pars II *De libertate Christiana* — see the deferred coverage there. The paradigm ***Pars I closing*** of the Praemonitio is here fittingly *hurried,* since Selnecker's readers had all the *Formula of Concord* material fresh in mind (1577, only two years earlier), and the Praemonitio's function is *summary-orientation* rather than exhaustive treatment.

## From Tranche 3 · Catalogus + Locus I De Verbo Dei Opening (Chapters 1–2)

⁴⁸ **The Catalogus Librorum Didacticorum** — Selnecker's paradigm ***self-bibliography*** of 32 published works up to 1579. This is a paradigm-Renaissance-Reformation ***auto-bibliographic convention*** established by Melanchthon (his own *Loci*-editions carry Melanchthon's catalogue-appendix), and adapted by his Wittenberg-Kreis pupils (Chytraeus, Selnecker, Chemnitz) for the paradigm ***inner-Lutheran-teacher-network*** purpose of guiding students to the whole corpus. Standard modern bibliography of Selnecker's 335 attested works: **PRDL** (Post-Reformation Digital Library) author-entry `a_id=618`; **VD16** entries S 5470 through S 5620. — The list here confirms the paradigm Selnecker-Amtsträger's ***three-fold vocation*** — biblical exegete (Psalter-Prophets-Acts-Petrine-Johannine-Wisdom-Jonah-Lamentations), systematic dogmatician (Paedagogia, Institutiones, Catechesis, Exegesis Symbolorum, Analectus, Notatio), and polemicist (anti-Beza responses, De persona et maiestate Christi, Confutatio-Refutatio Sacramentaria) — the paradigm ***triplex-officium*** of a Wittenberg-Melanchthonian *Praeceptor.*

⁴⁹ **"In the words of the sole Master Christ we swear"** — the paradigm ***Selnecker-motto*** at the close of the Catalogus, echoing the paradigm ***Horatian*** *nullius addictus iurare in verba magistri* (Epistles I.1.14) turned to *Christian* use — a paradigm confessional-Lutheran ***sola-Christi-verba*** turn against the paradigm scholastic ***iurare-in-verba-Aristotelis*** or ***iurare-in-verba-Papae*** habits. The paradigm couplet is a paradigm-Selnecker ***elegiac distich*** of the sort he wrote frequently in his ***Analecta*** and prefaces.

⁵⁰ **Locus I De Verbo Dei** — the paradigm-opening Locus of Selnecker's *Institutio,* interfacing directly with the paradigm ***prolegomena-tradition*** of Melanchthon's *Loci Communes* (1521 → 1543 → 1555) which had established the paradigm ***De Sacra Scriptura*** as the paradigm-first Locus of dogmatic instruction.
