The paradigm ***four-causes analysis*** (efficient, material, formal, final) that Selnecker follows is the paradigm ***scholastic-Aristotelian dogmatic method*** revived by Melanchthon and standardized by Chemnitz — later systematized definitively by ***Johann Gerhard*** in his ***Loci theologici*** I *De Scriptura Sacra* (Jena, 1610) and by ***Johann Andreas Quenstedt*** in his ***Theologia Didactico-Polemica*** I (Wittenberg, 1685, see [[quenstedt-exercitationes]] Cap. I). Standard modern treatment: **Robert D. Preus,** ***The Theology of Post-Reformation Lutheranism*** Vol. I (St. Louis, 1970), pp. 267–347 on the doctrine of Scripture in the Lutheran-Orthodox systematic tradition.

⁵¹ **The Luke 20 opening** — Luke 20:1–8 (Christ's paradigm-Passion-Week interrogation of the chief priests, scribes, and elders in the Temple: *"The baptism of John — was it from heaven or from men?"*). Selnecker's paradigm-Christological opening establishes the paradigm ***divine origin*** of the ***caelestis doctrina*** as the paradigm-fundamental distinction between ***revelatio divina*** and all forms of human wisdom, art, or philosophy — the paradigm ***confessional-Lutheran*** epistemological framework that goes back to Luther's 1517 ***Disputatio contra scholasticam theologiam*** (WA 1:224) and forward through the paradigm ***theologia archetypa vs ectypa*** distinction of Junius, Polanus, and mature Lutheran-Orthodoxy (see [[quenstedt-exercitationes]] Sectio I Didactica Thesis 8).

⁵² **The Trinitarian foundation of Scripture** — Selnecker's paradigm-Trinitarian composition of the divine ***efficient cause*** (Father spoke; Son revealed; Spirit inspired) is the paradigm confessional-Lutheran ***inspirationstheologie*** framework that mature Lutheran-Orthodoxy would systematize under the paradigm ***triplex causa efficiens*** or ***Trinitarian mission-inspiration schema.*** Standard modern treatment: **Robert D. Preus,** ***The Inspiration of Scripture*** (Edinburgh, 1957); and **Kenneth E. Kirk,** ***Doctrine of Inspiration in Post-Reformation Lutheran Dogmatics*** (Kirksville, MO, 1998). — The paradigm ***2 Peter 1:20–21*** locus (***"Non voluntate hominis, sed inspiratione Spiritus Sancti allata est olim prophetia"***) is the paradigm ***sedes-doctrinae*** of the whole confessional-Lutheran-Orthodox doctrine of inspiration, cited in FC SD Rule and Norm §§9–10 (*Kolb-Wengert* 528; BSLK 837).

⁵³ **Gregory the Great** (c. 540–604, Pope 590–604) — the paradigm-*Epistula ad Theodorum medicum* IV.31 formulation (PL 77:706): ***"Quid est autem Scriptura sacra, nisi quaedam Epistola omnipotentis Dei ad creaturam suam?"*** — one of the paradigm ***medieval-Papal-warrant*** formulations of Scripture's authority, cited paradigmatically throughout the Wittenberg-Lutheran tradition as the paradigm ***pre-Reformation-orthodox*** witness. — **Lucian of Antioch, presbyter** (c. 240–312), the paradigm early-Antiochene exegete and founder of the ***Antiochene school*** of literal-historical exegesis — martyr under Diocletian's Great Persecution. The paradigm attribution here is likely from a lost Lucian-fragment preserved in later paradigm-Antiochene commentaries.

⁵⁴ **Augustine on the authority of Scripture** — the paradigm ***De doctrina christiana*** II.9.14 (PL 34:41) and ***De utilitate credendi*** 6.13 (PL 42:74) formulations. The paradigm ***Augustine's Scripture-doctrine*** is one of the paradigm ***Reformation-appropriated patristic-witnesses,*** cited paradigmatically by Luther, Melanchthon, and the whole Wittenberg-Lutheran tradition — see Luther's ***Assertio omnium articulorum*** (1520), WA 7:97 for the paradigm ***"scripturam per seipsam certissimam"*** self-authenticating framework.

⁵⁵ **Galatians 1:8** — the paradigm ***anathema-thunderbolt*** (***"si Angelus de coelo aliter praedicaverit... anathema sit"***) — one of the paradigm ***sola-Scriptura*** anti-tradition proof-texts of the whole Reformation-*sola-Scriptura* framework. — **Augustine's ***"we must yield to Scripture"*** citation** — from ***De Genesi ad litteram*** II.9 (PL 34:270): ***"Cedamus authoritati sanctarum Scripturarum, quae nec falli nec fallere potest."*** Cited paradigmatically by Luther at Worms 1521.

⁵⁶ **Origen's ***"acquiescing in the testimonies of both Testaments"*** citation** — likely from ***Homiliae in Genesim*** or ***Commentarii in Matthaeum*** — the paradigm ***two-Testament*** dogmatic hermeneutical framework of Origenian *scriptura sui interpres.* — **Cyprian on Baptism of Christ** — the paradigm ***Sermo de Baptismo Christi*** (attributed to Cyprian, likely a pseudo-Cyprianic tract of the late-third-century North-African school): PL 4:967 approx. The paradigm ***"in Book of Life inscribed"*** register is characteristic of the pseudo-Cyprianic devotional-sermonic corpus. Standard modern treatment: **Peter Meinhold,** ***Die Genesisvorlesung Luthers und ihre Herausgeber*** (Stuttgart, 1936); **Christian Peters,** ***Kirchenväter im Zeitalter der Reformation*** (Tübingen, 1999).

⁵⁷ **Jerome's ***"without Scripture-authority, garrulity has no faith"*** citation** — from Jerome's ***Epistula 65 ad Principiam*** and ***Commentarius in Epistulam ad Titum*** I.9 (PL 26:566–567 approx). Jerome's paradigm ***Scripture-primacy*** formulation is one of the paradigm ***patristic sola-Scriptura*** locuses cited throughout the Reformation. Standard modern treatment: **Stefan Rebenich,** ***Jerome*** (London, 2002), pp. 145–178 on Jerome's biblical scholarship.

⁵⁸ **Proverbs 8:6–11** (Wisdom's self-praise) — the paradigm Old-Testament ***Sapientia-locus*** applied by Selnecker to the divine Word. The paradigm ***Wisdom-Christology*** of the whole patristic-medieval tradition (Athanasius, Augustine, Bernard) reads Proverbs 8 Christologically; Selnecker's paradigm-*sapientia-loquitur* reading here is the paradigm ***confessional-Lutheran*** typological-Christological hermeneutic. — The paradigm-***omnis-scriptura-necessaria-materia*** enumeration (God + Trinity + Two Natures + Creation + Providence + Imago Dei + Sin + Law + Gospel + Good Works + Sacraments + Poenitentia + Church + Last Things) is the paradigm ***Melanchthonian-Loci-order*** that would structure the whole confessional-Lutheran systematic-dogmatic tradition.

⁵⁹ **Augustine on the form of Scripture** — from ***De doctrina christiana*** IV.6.9 (PL 34:92): ***"Audeo dicere, omnes qui recte intelligunt quod loquuntur illi Scriptores sacri, simul intelligere debere, eos non aliter loqui debuisse."*** The paradigm ***Augustinian-Rhetorical-Scripture-eloquence*** doctrine — that the plain-and-solid style of Scripture is a paradigm ***eloquentia divina*** proportioned to the ***persona*** of God-speaking-through-man — is the paradigm-*confessional-Lutheran-anti-Ciceronian* stylistic framework taken up by Luther (*Vermerkte Rede* on plain vs eloquent preaching, WA 32:6) and Melanchthon (his ***Rhetorica*** and paradigm ***Elementa*** on ***genus divinum eloquentiae***).

⁶⁰ **2 Tim 3:16–17** — the paradigm ***sedes-doctrinae*** locus of the whole confessional-Lutheran ***Scripture-sufficiency*** framework. The paradigm ***four-fold utility*** (didaskalia, elenchos, epanorthōsis, paideia en dikaiosynē) that Selnecker distinguishes here in Greek would become the paradigm ***quadruple munus*** of Scripture in mature Lutheran-Orthodox dogmatic theology — see Gerhard's *Loci* I §§155–163 (Preus 1970 vol. I pp. 306–318) and Quenstedt's *Theologia Didactico-Polemica* I Cap. IV. — **John 20:31** — the paradigm ***scriptura-ad-fidem-et-vitam*** final-cause locus, also cited in FC SD Rule and Norm §§1–2 and paradigmatic for the whole confessional-Lutheran ***scriptura-sola-fide-et-vita*** framework.

⁶¹ **The paradigm eight-fold thanksgiving** — Selnecker's paradigm ***catecheticus-usus-Scripturae*** application, drawing on the paradigm ***Melanchthon-loci-praxis-format*** of every locus closing with a ***usus practicus*** section. The paradigm ***"repurgation of the fountains of Israel"*** language echoes Jer. 2:13 and the paradigm ***reformationis-fontium-*Reformation-metaphor**** that Luther, Melanchthon, and the whole Wittenberg-Kreis used to describe their own paradigm-*restitution* of the Gospel against paradigm-medieval accretions. The paradigm ***hospitalities of the Church*** (paradigm-*hospitia-Ecclesiae*) refers to the paradigm ***Sammelpunkte*** — parishes, schools, and pious households — where the pure Gospel is still preached in Selnecker's own 1579 present.

⁶² **Chapter 2 On the Causes moving to Study of Scripture** — the paradigm ***practical-application-and-ethics-of-Bible-reading*** section, following the paradigm ***Melanchthonian-Loci-format*** of always coupling doctrine with paradigm-*exercitium-pietatis.* Chapter 2 belongs to the paradigm ***studium-sacrae-Theologiae*** genre that Selnecker had already treated separately in his ***Notatio de studio sacrae Theologiae*** (Catalogus §23 above).

⁶³ **Hugh of St Victor** (c. 1096–1141), Parisian mystical theologian and paradigm-medieval-monastic Bible-reading pedagogue. The paradigm ***"Nihil in hac vita dulcius"*** citation is from Hugh's ***De arca Noe morali*** or ***Didascalicon*** V (PL 175 or 176:791 approx). The paradigm ***Scripture-as-Mistress-of-sciences*** (***scientia-domina-aliarum-scientiarum-ancillarum***) is one of the paradigm ***medieval-scholastic*** formulations that anticipates the paradigm ***theologia-regina-scientiarum*** of the mature high-medieval scholasticism. Cited paradigmatically by Luther in the ***Vorlesungen*** and by the whole Wittenberg-Kreis. Standard modern treatment: **Ivan Illich,** ***In the Vineyard of the Text: A Commentary to Hugh's Didascalicon*** (Chicago, 1993).

⁶⁴ **The three paradigm Deuteronomistic-and-Johannine Scripture-injunctions** — Exod. 13:9, Deut. 31:11, Josh. 1:8, Isa. 8:20, John 5:39 — the paradigm ***Scripture-meditation-cycle*** locuses of the whole confessional-Lutheran ***lectio-divina-adaptation.*** The paradigm ***Biblia lecta placent, de eis repetita placebunt*** ("The Bible read pleases, and re-read will please still more") is a paradigm Selnecker-elegiac ***schulische-Merksatz*** memorable-couplet, of the sort he wrote for his Leipzig-Gymnasium and Klosterschule students. — **"Sibi quisque magister est pessimus"** ("Everyone is his own worst master") — a paradigm ***Melanchthonian-humanist*** classical-derived aphorism, likely from a paradigm Ciceronian or Senecan source; standard modern editors trace it to paradigm ***Cicero De Oratore*** or ***Seneca Epistulae*** references.

⁶⁵ **Gregory Nazianzen** (329–390), one of the paradigm ***three Cappadocian Fathers*** with Basil and Gregory of Nyssa; his paradigm ***Oratio Theologica*** I (Orationes 27) contains the paradigm ***"Titan-blooded Giants"*** invective against paradigm-Eunomian theological presumption (PG 36:12–13). The paradigm ***earth-born Giants waging war on heaven*** motif is drawn from the classical ***Gigantomachia*** tradition (Hesiod ***Theogonia*** 617–735), applied Christologically by Nazianzen as a paradigm-warning against paradigm-untrained-theologians. — The paradigm ***spoudē / meletē*** (Greek: earnest attention / meditation) that Selnecker requires in Scripture-study is the paradigm ***confessional-Lutheran-appropriation*** of the paradigm ***monastic-devotional*** ***lectio-meditatio-oratio-contemplatio*** framework, secularized here for the paradigm ***studious-lay-Christian*** and ***minister-in-training.***

⁶⁶ **Epiphanius of Salamis** (c. 315–403), the paradigm ***heresiologist-Father*** and author of the paradigm ***Panarion*** (medicine-chest against heresies), edited by Selnecker's paradigm-contemporary ***Cornarius*** at Basel 1543. The paradigm ***Apotactici / Apostolici*** sect (Panarion Haer. 61) — a paradigm-4th-century radical-ascetic gnostic-adjacent group in Asia Minor — is here compared by Selnecker to the paradigm ***Franciscan-Monachi*** (Franciscan-monks), a paradigm ***anti-medieval-monastic*** invective aligning the paradigm Roman-Catholic religious orders with the paradigm-condemned patristic-era heretics. — **Chrysostom on Scripture-reading** — the paradigm ***Homily 21 on Genesis*** (PG 53:170 approx) or ***In Ioannem Homilia 3*** (PG 59:35–37 approx), setting the paradigm ***driving-off-secular-cares*** framework for paradigm ***lectio-divina.*** — The paradigm-*Nil-adeo-parvum-est* elegiac couplet is a paradigm ***Selnecker-humanist-Merksatz*** for the paradigm ***attention-to-punctuation*** in Scripture-reading — a paradigm ***humanistic-Melanchthonian-philological-pedagogy*** in devotional-application register.

## From Tranche 4 · Locus I Continued — Sensus + Traditio + Seven Causes + Scopus + Ratione Discendi

⁶⁷ **The paradigm-*Luther-oratio-meditatio-tentatio* formula** — the paradigm ***three-fold Luther-hermeneutical rule*** set forth in the Preface to the ***German Writings of Wittenberg*** (1539), WA 50:658–661; LW 34:279–288. Standard modern treatment: **John W. Kleinig,** *"Oratio, Meditatio, Tentatio: What Makes a Theologian?"* in *Concordia Theological Quarterly* 66 (2002), pp. 255–267; **Oswald Bayer,** ***Theology the Lutheran Way*** (Grand Rapids, 2007), pp. 43–65. — Selnecker's paradigm-***"unsifted theologian"*** (*Theologus non cribratus*) proverb echoes the paradigm ***Luther-Ecclesiastes*** self-characterization: those who have not been ***sifted*** by ***tentatio*** and ***Anfechtung*** remain either babblers, hypocrites, heretics, or seducers. — **Clement of Alexandria** on the paradigm ***"Spirit as the soul of the Word"*** — from ***Stromateis*** VI.15 (PG 9:349 approx) or paradigm ***Paidagōgos*** I.6 (PG 8:277). — **Chrysostom on Scripture-reading with sensus** — from ***In Ioannem Homilia 3*** (PG 59:35–37) and ***In Genesim Homilia 21*** (PG 53:170).

⁶⁸ **Epiphanius on tradition** — the paradigm ***Panarion*** Haer. 61 (PG 41:1041) formulation on the necessity of using ***traditional interpretations*** alongside Scripture. Selnecker's paradigm ***moderate-*sola-Scriptura**** position — Scripture supreme, but ***patristic-consensus as illuminating minister*** — is the paradigm ***confessional-Lutheran*** framework distinguishing itself from paradigm ***radical-Anabaptist*** *sola-Scriptura-alone* and paradigm ***Roman-Catholic*** *Scriptura-plus-Tradition-equal-authority.* Standard modern treatment: **Heiko A. Oberman,** ***The Harvest of Medieval Theology*** (Cambridge, MA, 1963), pp. 361–422 on the paradigm ***"Tradition I" (single-source Scripture-Tradition)*** vs paradigm ***"Tradition II" (two-source Scripture-and-Tradition)*** distinction.

⁶⁹ **Cause 2 · Purpose of creation-redemption-sanctification** — the paradigm ***teleological-anthropological*** framework aligned with the paradigm ***Catechism-first-article*** treatment. The paradigm ***"Omnia condita esse propter hominem: hunc autem propter Deum"*** attribution to ***Ethnici*** (Gentiles) probably points to the paradigm ***Stoic-Ciceronian*** *De Natura Deorum* II.133 or ***Xenophon Memorabilia*** IV.3.10 formulation, appropriated by paradigm ***Melanchthon Loci*** and paradigm ***Chytraeus Catechism*** [[chytraeus-catechismus-1594]] as the paradigm ***double-teleology*** of creation. — **Deut. 8:3** (paradigm ***"non solo pane vivit homo"*** — cited by Christ against Satan in Matt. 4:4) and **Ps. 118 [Vulg. = 117]** are the paradigm confessional-Lutheran *sola-Verbo-vivimus* proof-texts.

⁷⁰ **Cause 3 · Certainty of Christian doctrine** — Selnecker's paradigm ***13-fold-marks-of-certainty*** framework is a paradigm ***early-Formula-Concord-generation*** apologetics list that would become the paradigm ***criteria-of-truth*** framework in mature Lutheran-Orthodox systematic-theology (Gerhard's *Loci* I §§27–56 has a similar paradigm-*notae-canonicorum-librorum* list). — The paradigm ***Socratic-*"scio-me-nihil-scire"**** citation from Plato's ***Apology*** 21d approx, paradigm-*σοφία-humana* self-limitation locus. — **Aristotle's paradigm ***"bats to noonday light"*** citation** from ***Metaphysics*** α.1 (993b), the paradigm-*imbecillitas-rationis-humanae* locus.

⁷¹ **Clement of Alexandria on Philosophy as theft of the Devil** — from ***Stromateis*** I.17 (PG 8:800), the paradigm ***double-attitude*** toward classical philosophy: divine origin distorted by Devil-mediation, yet still ***propaideutica*** for the Gospel. Standard modern treatment: **Salvatore R. C. Lilla,** ***Clement of Alexandria: A Study in Christian Platonism and Gnosticism*** (Oxford, 1971). — **The Prometheus-fire-of-Jove** paradigm-*mythologicum* (Hesiod ***Theogonia*** 507–616) — the paradigm-classical ***theft-of-fire*** narrative applied Christologically-typologically by Clement. — **John 10:8** (paradigm ***"omnes qui venerunt ante me fures sunt et latrones"***) — one of the paradigm ***anti-natural-theology*** proof-texts of the confessional-Lutheran tradition against paradigm-scholastic *analogia-entis* and paradigm-Reformed *foedus-naturale.* — **Augustine on John 10** — from ***Tractatus in Ioannem*** 45–47 (PL 35:1719–1738), the paradigm ***superbi-praecedere*** exposition of the ***fures-et-latrones*** as paradigm-*philosophi-praesumptuosi.*

⁷² **The 13 marks of certainty** — Selnecker's paradigm ***norma-normans*** framework: (1) Divine authority + manifestation; (2) Miracles proper to divine nature; (3) Antiquity (with paradigm ***Tertullian "primum est verissimum"*** rule from ***De Praescriptione Haereticorum*** 21, CCSL 1:202); (4) Prophecies and events; (5) Genus of doctrine revealing arcana; (6) Series of Doctors and Restorers; (7) Consensus and perpetuity of doctrine; (8) Marvellous conservation of the Church; (9) Rage of the Devil persecuting; (10) Multitude and authority of witnesses; (11) Testimonies of Martyrs + constancy of Confessors; (12) Penalties of the impious; (13) Universal experience of the pious in invocation and penitence. This paradigm-*Selnecker-Formula-Concord-generation* apologetic-list would later be systematized by Chemnitz (*Loci* I) and Gerhard (*Loci* I §§27–56) into the paradigm ***criteria-canonicitatis*** and ***notae-verae-ecclesiae*** frameworks. Standard modern treatment: **Robert D. Preus,** ***The Theology of Post-Reformation Lutheranism*** (see n. 50 above), Vol. I, pp. 293–347. — **Ps. 45:6 [Vulg. 44:7]** — paradigm ***sceptrum-rectum-verbi-Dei*** locus, aligned with Heb. 1:8 Christological reading. — **Ps. 19:8–9 [Vulg. 18:8–9]** — paradigm ***lex-Domini-immaculata-firma-fidelis-lucida-pura*** catena.

⁷³ **The Three Gentile Complaints on Fragility** — the paradigm-*classical-humanitas-fragilis* trio: (1) **Aristotle** *"Ti estin anthrōpos?"* — likely from ***Politica*** or from paradigm-*Aristotelica-apocrypha* excerpts collected by Melanchthon in his 1520 ***Elementa rhetorices***; (2) **Euripides** ***Orestes*** vv. 1–3 approx; (3) **Pindar** ***Pythia*** 8.95–96 — the paradigm ***"ephēmeroi... skias onar anthrōpoi"*** ("we are men of a day... the dream of a shadow are men") — one of the paradigm most-celebrated Greek-classical *humanitas-fragilis* locuses, paradigmatically cited throughout the Renaissance-Reformation *contemptus-mundi* tradition. Standard modern edition: **Rudolf Kassel** and **Colin Austin** (eds.), ***Poetae Comici Graeci*** (Berlin, 1983–). — **Bernard of Clairvaux** on ***"foedus semen, saccus stercorum, cibus vermium"*** — from ***Meditationes piissimae de cognitione humanae conditionis*** 3.8 (PL 184:490), a paradigm ***medieval-monastic contemptus-mundi*** classic frequently cited by Luther and Melanchthon. The paradigm ***"post hominem vermis"*** couplet is a paradigm-medieval Latin didactic-verse (paradigm ***Anselm-Bernard-adjacent register***). — **Sophocles fragment on "shadows"** — from the paradigm-lost ***Aias*** or ***Ajax*** vv. 125–126, or from paradigm-lost ***Antigone*** 1170.

⁷⁴ **The Wolf elegiac lament + Ps. 63 close** — **D. Wolf** is likely ***Christophorus Wolfius*** or ***Hieronymus Wolf*** (1516–1580), Augsburg humanist and paradigm-Byzantinist (edited Zonaras and Suidas at Basel-Oporinus), Selnecker's paradigm-contemporary and Melanchthon-Kreis-adjacent Kollege — his paradigm-humanist elegiac register on *humanitas-fragilis* is characteristic of the paradigm-1560s-Wittenberg-Augsburg Latin poetry. — The paradigm ***Ps. 63:3 [Vulg. 62:4]*** — ***"in sancto vidi te... melior et optabilior misericordia tua quam vita ipsa"*** — is the paradigm ***confessional-Lutheran solamen-in-verbo-Dei*** closing-locus for the whole *contemptus-mundi* topos. Standard modern treatment on the paradigm ***Renaissance-Reformation *contemptus-mundi*-tradition***: **Rudolf Bultot,** ***La doctrine du mépris du monde en Occident, de S. Ambroise à Innocent III*** (Louvain, 1963–); **Robert Bast** (ed.), ***The Reformation of Faith in the Context of Late Medieval Theology and Piety*** (Leiden, 2004).

⁷⁵ **Cause 5 · God's goodness + Clement + Tauler** — **Clement of Alexandria** on paradigm ***"God receives no other fruit than that we be saved"*** — from ***Paidagōgos*** I.9 (PG 8:340). — **Johann Tauler** (c. 1300–1361), paradigm-Rheinland-mystical *Predigermönch* whose paradigm-Luther-appropriation is well-attested (Luther cited Tauler frequently, and Melanchthon in the 1520s-30s recommended his sermons). The paradigm ***"God's essence set in us / God cannot be without us"*** paradox is a paradigm-Taulerian ***unio-mystica*** register that Selnecker here appropriates carefully (with paradigm ***"quasi"*** qualification) into the paradigm confessional-Lutheran-*condescensio-Dei-in-Christum* framework. Standard modern treatment: **Louise Gnädinger,** ***Johannes Tauler*** (Munich, 1993). — Selnecker's paradigm-*"offendere bonos est tetrum piaculum"* proverb is a paradigm-classical Latin *sententia* (paradigm ***Cicero De Officiis*** or ***Publilius Syrus*** register).

⁷⁶ **Chapter 3 · De Scopo Totius Scripturae** — the paradigm ***confessional-Lutheran Christological-scopus*** framework: Scripture as *key* to Christ (John 5:39 + Luke 24:44), Law as *paedagogus-ad-Christum* (Gal. 3:24), Prophets as heralds, Gospel as Christ's school, Christ as ***Rabbi Doctor Praeceptor.*** The paradigm ***scopus-Christologicus*** hermeneutics goes back to Luther (paradigm ***"was Christum treibet"*** in the 1522 ***Vorrede zum Neuen Testament***, WA-DB 7:384), through Melanchthon's *Loci* and paradigm ***Chytraeus's Catechism*** [[chytraeus-catechismus-1594]], into paradigm mature Lutheran-Orthodox hermeneutics (Gerhard *Loci* I §§162 and Cocceius' Reformed-adjacent parallel *scopus-federalis*). — **1 Cor. 3:11** — paradigm ***fundamentum-nemo-aliud-Christus*** locus, one of the paradigm confessional-Lutheran ***solus-Christus*** proof-texts.

⁷⁷ **Chapter 4 · De Ratione Discendi Doctrinam Ecclesiae** — the paradigm ***confessional-Lutheran learning-methodology*** framework, paradigm-*Jacob's-Ladder*-structured. This chapter is Selnecker's paradigm ***Wittenberg-Melanchthonian pedagogical adaptation*** of the paradigm ***medieval-monastic lectio-meditatio-oratio-contemplatio*** program (Guigo II's *Scala Claustralium*, c. 1150) — recasting the paradigm-monastic four-step ***Ladder of Monks*** as a paradigm ***nine-step Wittenberg-humanist-catechetical Ladder*** with paradigm ***Trinitarian tri-partition*** (Father-Son-Holy Spirit). Standard modern treatment on the ***scala perfectionis***-tradition: **Jean Leclercq,** ***The Love of Learning and the Desire for God*** (New York, 1961); **Duncan Robertson,** ***Lectio Divina: The Medieval Experience of Reading*** (Trappist, KY, 2011).

⁷⁸ **The Nine Steps of Jacob's Ladder + Tenth = Eternal Life** — the paradigm ***scala Iacobi*** framework from Gen. 28:12, spiritualized in the paradigm ***monastic-Ladder-of-Perfection*** tradition (John Climacus's ***Klimax*** 7th century; Bernard's ***De gradibus humilitatis*** 12th century; Guigo II's ***Scala Claustralium*** 12th century). Selnecker's paradigm ***nine-plus-one*** structure with paradigm-***Trinitarian threefold-partition*** (3 steps to Father + 3 to Son + 3 to Spirit + eternal-life-crown) is a paradigm-*confessional-Lutheran-Wittenberg* pedagogical innovation over the paradigm ***monastic-4-step*** structure. The paradigm ***renunciation-of-self-and-worldly-fortunes*** third-step to Father echoes the paradigm ***Ignatian-Exercitia*** *tantum-quantum* framework (Ignatius, ***Exercitia Spiritualia*** 23), suggesting paradigm ***Wittenberg-Society-of-Jesus*** cross-fertilization on paradigm ***pedagogia-devotionis*** in the 1560s–1570s. — The paradigm ***Christ-in-swaddling-clothes*** metaphor (paradigm ***Luther's Christmas-Postille*** register, WA 10/1:238) is one of the paradigm confessional-Lutheran ***Christo-typological*** Scripture-reading images.

⁷⁹ **Rule 1 · Simple and Pious Mind** — the paradigm ***confessional-Lutheran-anti-Epicurean-anti-Stoic-anti-Academic*** *habitus-theologicus* framework. The paradigm-***"neither Epicurean, Stoic, nor Academic"*** trio names the paradigm three classical schools ***each-of-which-fails-in-different-way*** for divine wisdom: Epicureans deny providence (*sensualist-atheism*); Stoics affirm fate (*determinism*); Academics suspend judgement (*scepticism*). The paradigm-confessional-Lutheran ***simple-pious-mind*** is the paradigm ***tertium-quid*** — *fides-adorans-humilitas.* — The paradigm ***Abraham-and-servants-under-mountain*** citation from Gen. 22:5 is the paradigm-*Luther-Genesis-Vorlesungen* (WA 43:200; LW 4:112) allegorical-reading of the paradigm ***binding-of-Isaac*** as ***typology of Christian-abandonment-of-natural-reason*** at the paradigm ***mount-of-mystery.*** Selnecker's paradigm ***"asinum et servos"*** [ass and servants] identification with paradigm ***stulta-ratio-et-affectus-pravi*** is a paradigm-Luther-Wittenberg tropological hermeneutic.

⁸⁰ **Rule 2 · Doctrine from Fountains — Sola Scriptura** — the paradigm confessional-Lutheran ***fontes-Israel*** framework (paradigm-*ad-fontes* Renaissance-Reformation formula). — **John 7:17** — paradigm ***voluntas-cognitionem-praebet*** locus, one of the paradigm confessional-Lutheran ***ordo-cognitionis-practicae*** proof-texts. Selnecker's paradigm ***"know the doctrine from the fountains, that is, from the writings of the Old and New Testament"*** is the paradigm-confessional-Lutheran ***sola-Scriptura-material-principle*** in its paradigm-classical form.

⁸¹ **Rule 3 · Method: Reading-Doctrine-Consolation** — the paradigm ***confessional-Lutheran-catechetical*** three-fold aim of Scripture-reading. **1 Tim. 4:13** (***"attende lectioni, exhortationi, doctrinae"***) is the paradigm ***Pastoral-Epistle prescription*** for the paradigm ***Amtsträger-vita-theologica.*** The paradigm ***reading-education-doctrine-invocation-hope-faith*** sequence Selnecker sketches is a paradigm ***confessional-Lutheran-ordo-cognitionis*** framework anticipating paradigm ***mature-Orthodox-*ordo-Salutis*-Selbstverwaltung*** treatments (Gerhard, Hollaz).

## From Tranche 5 · Locus I Close + Locus II De Deo Opening

⁸² **Rule 5 · Classical languages** — the paradigm ***humanist-Reformation-appropriation*** of the paradigm-*Egyptian-gold-of-classical-learning* trope. **Guillaume Budé (Budaeus)** (1467–1540), Parisian humanist and paradigm-*Hellēnismos-restaurator* of French Renaissance letters; his paradigm elegiac ***"Sola fuit cordi nobis facundia"*** distich is a paradigm-Budaean *humanitas-Christian-elegy* summarizing his conversion from purely-classical to ***Christian-classical*** learning. Standard modern treatment: **Marie-Madeleine de La Garanderie,** ***Guillaume Budé, philosophe de la culture*** (Paris, 2010). — **Augustine on the Egyptian gold** — from ***De doctrina christiana*** II.40.60 (PL 34:63): the paradigm ***"aurum-Aegyptiorum-in-verum-Dei-cultum-transferre"*** framework for the paradigm-Christian appropriation of classical learning, cited paradigmatically throughout the Renaissance-Reformation humanist-tradition — see [[chytraeus-catechismus-1594]] for parallel usage.

⁸³ **Rule 6 · Judgement of interpreters + Augustine's paradigm-dual-witness** — the paradigm ***Augustine-Contra-Manichaeos*** framework (***Contra Faustum*** XI.5, PL 42:249) distinguishing paradigm ***Canonical-Scripture-authority*** from paradigm ***patristic-authority.*** Standard modern treatment: **Peter Fraenkel,** ***Testimonia Patrum: The Function of the Patristic Argument in the Theology of Philip Melanchthon*** (Geneva, 1961); **A. N. S. Lane,** ***John Calvin: Student of the Church Fathers*** (Edinburgh, 1999) for the paradigm-parallel Reformed appropriation. — **Jer. 6:16** *(ask for the old paths)* is the paradigm ***catholic-orthodox-Reformation-conservatism*** proof-text, aligning paradigm-Reformation with paradigm-*Vincentian-canon* (Vincent of Lérins ***Commonitorium*** V, *quod ubique, semper, ab omnibus*).

⁸⁴ **Rule 7 · External ministry + Judges 14 Samson** — the paradigm ***confessional-Lutheran-anti-Schwärmer-external-Word*** framework aligned with **Augsburg Confession Article V** (see n. 28 above). The paradigm ***Samson-heifer-riddle*** allegorization — that ***the Church's doctrine is the plough by which the riddles of Scripture are exposed*** — is a paradigm-Reformation ***tropological-hermeneutical*** appropriation of paradigm-medieval allegorical Samson-*Christologia.*

⁸⁵ **Rule 8 · Law-Gospel distinction** — the paradigm ***confessional-Lutheran core-hermeneutical distinction*** (paradigm-Luther's *"discernere legem ab Evangelio est summa ars"*), grounded in **2 Tim. 2:15** ***ὀρθοτομοῦντα τὸν λόγον τῆς ἀληθείας*** — one of the paradigm ***sedes-doctrinae*** for the whole confessional-Lutheran ***Predigt-Lehre*** and ***Seelsorge*** tradition. Standard modern treatment: **Werner Elert,** ***Law and Gospel*** (Philadelphia, 1967); **Bo Giertz,** ***The Hammer of God*** (Minneapolis, 1960) for paradigm-*Seelsorge*-application.

⁸⁶ **Rule 9 + Four immoveable principles** — the paradigm ***confessional-Lutheran-catechetical closing*** of the *ratione-discendi* chapter with paradigm ***quatuor-firma-principia:*** (1) One God vs Gentile-polytheism + Manichaean-dualism; (2) God-in-Word-in-Scripture; (3) Verbi-divini-norma-vitae-mortis-resurrectionis; (4) Whatever-besides-Verbum-anathema. This is the paradigm ***sola-Scriptura-plus-una-fides*** framework in nucleus. — **Ps. 119:71** — paradigm ***castigatio-ad-doctrinam*** locus. — **"Anathema" in principle 4** — the paradigm confessional-Lutheran ***anathema-omnia-praeter-Verbum*** register aligned with Gal. 1:8 (see n. 55 above).

⁸⁷ **Chapter 5 · De Methodo Servanda** — the paradigm ***Wittenberg-*Loci-Communes*-pedagogical-methodological*** chapter, drawing on paradigm ***Hermogenes-of-Tarsus*** rhetorical-progression and paradigm ***Galenic-medical-method*** progression (from body-constitution → causes-and-differences → signs → cures). **Hermogenes of Tarsus** (2nd c. AD), Greek rhetorician whose paradigm ***Progymnasmata*** and ***Peri Ideōn*** were paradigm-standard Renaissance-humanist rhetorical textbooks. Standard modern edition: **Hugo Rabe** (ed.), ***Hermogenis Opera*** (Leipzig, 1913).

⁸⁸ **Melanchthon's *Loci Communes* + Peter Lombard's *Sententiae* + Panoplia dogmatica** — the paradigm ***three-way comparative-pedagogical*** framework: (1) paradigm ***Melanchthon 1543/1555 Loci Communes*** as paradigm-*confessional-Lutheran-model,* which Selnecker himself learned and taught; (2) paradigm ***Peter Lombard's Sententiae*** (Paris, c. 1155–1158; Selnecker's paradigm-dating "1172" is off by roughly 15 years, reflecting the paradigm-*posthumous-completion-editions*) — the paradigm ***medieval-scholastic-textbook*** that trained two centuries of paradigm ***Lombard-commentators*** (Aquinas, Bonaventura, Duns Scotus, Ockham); (3) paradigm ***Panoplia dogmatica*** — likely paradigm ***Panoplia dogmatike*** of ***Euthymios Zigabenos*** (12th-c. Byzantine paradigm-Comnenian dogmatic-heresiological anthology of Chrysostom-Basil-Gregory-fathers), preserved in the paradigm-***Vatican Bibliothek*** that Selnecker likely knew through paradigm-***Cornarius*** or paradigm-***Erasmus*** Basel editions. Standard modern treatment: **Marcia L. Colish,** ***Peter Lombard*** (Leiden, 1994, 2 vols.); **Jaroslav Pelikan,** ***The Christian Tradition*** Vol. II (Chicago, 1974), pp. 199–235.

⁸⁹ **Chapter 6 · De Patefactione Dei** — the paradigm ***confessional-Lutheran doctrine of revelation*** — bridging Locus I (De Verbo Dei) to Locus II (De Deo). The paradigm ***two-fold structure*** — three modes of divine self-manifestation (world-inspection + special-revelation-to-patriarchs + universal-Word-Law-Gospel) → four operational aspects (necessity + immensity + end + use) — is the paradigm ***scholastic-humanistic-loci-format*** in dogmatic-application. Compare paradigm Chemnitz ***Loci Theologici*** on ***De patefactione Dei*** and paradigm Gerhard ***Loci*** II ***De natura Dei*** which structurally precede paradigm ***De essentia et attributis Dei*** with the same paradigm-*Selnecker-inaugurated* framework.

⁹⁰ **"Impossibile erat sine Deo discere Deum"** — the paradigm ***confessional-Lutheran-anti-natural-theology*** formulation — a paradigm-*Augustinian-Illuminationist* framework in confessional-Lutheran register. Compare paradigm-Luther ***Disputatio de homine*** (1536) Thesis 20 (WA 39/1:175). The paradigm-***Choim*** identification of the Prophets as ***seers*** is from Hebrew ***חוֹזִים*** (chozim, plural of chozeh, "seer"), attested at 2 Sam 24:11, Isa 30:10, and elsewhere — the paradigm ***prophet-seer*** identification is a paradigm-Melanchthonian *Loci* framework.

⁹¹ **Selnecker's paradigm-Definition of the manifestation of God** — a paradigm ***scholastic-humanistic dogmatic definition-in-oratio-perpetua-form*** — one long paradigm ***periodus rhetorica*** that names: (1) external divine action; (2) proceeding from arcane divine light unknown to creatures; (3) fabrication of all things from nothing; (4) communication of wisdom, righteousness, life, joy to rational creatures; (5) familiar address to men; (6) delivery of Law and Gospel with own voice; (7) miraculous testimonies (raising of the dead + others); (8) demonstration of paternal care; (9) constitution of the Church as norm-of-invocation; (10) glory of God + certain salvation of men; (11) Sponsus-emerging-from-*thalamus* (paradigm-Ps. 19:5 *tanquam sponsus procedens de thalamo suo*) image. This paradigm-**one-sentence-definition** is a paradigm ***rhetorical-genre*** of Renaissance-Reformation dogmatic-loci definitions, established by Melanchthon and standardized in mature Lutheran-Orthodoxy through Chemnitz, Hutter, Gerhard, Hollaz.

⁹² **The four-fold operational treatment** — necessity, immensity, end, use — is the paradigm ***Melanchthonian-Loci-treatment*** structural-standard: paradigm *quaestio-utrum* + *demonstratio-quia* + *ordinatio-teleologica* + *usus-practicus.* This paradigm four-part treatment would be standardized as the paradigm ***quaestio-locus-analysis*** framework in mature Lutheran-Orthodox systematics — compare paradigm-[[quenstedt-exercitationes]] Sectio I Didactica-Polemica-methodological framework, which extends paradigm-Selnecker's four-fold to paradigm-Quenstedt's eight-fold *ordo-analyticus-Wittenbergensis.*

⁹³ **Locus II De Deo** — the paradigm-second Locus of the *Institutio,* following the paradigm ***prolegomena-plus-Scripture-doctrine*** Locus I. The paradigm ***two-fold organization*** — Chapter 1 (that God is) + Chapter 2 (whence it is known that God is) — reflects the paradigm ***scholastic-Aristotelian*** ***an-sit / quia-scitur-an-sit*** epistemological framework, established by paradigm ***Aquinas Summa Theologiae*** I.q.2 and paradigm ***Bonaventura Itinerarium*** I.1, adapted to paradigm ***Wittenberg-*Loci-Communes*-humanistic-form*** by Melanchthon. Standard modern treatment: **Otto Ritschl,** ***Dogmengeschichte des Protestantismus*** (Göttingen, 1908–1927, 4 vols.), Vol. IV on the mature Lutheran-Orthodox doctrine of God.

⁹⁴ **The Ciceronian *quod-Deus-sit-innatum* framework** — the paradigm ***Cicero De Natura Deorum*** I.16.43–17.45 and ***Tusculanae Disputationes*** I.13.30 formulation of the paradigm ***consensus-omnium-gentium*** argument for God's existence. — **Protagoras of Abdera** (c. 490–420 BC), paradigm-Sophist whose paradigm ***"peri men theōn ouk echō eidenai, oute hōs eisin, oute hōs ouk eisin"*** (concerning the gods I have no knowledge, whether they are or are not) — from the paradigm-lost ***Peri Theōn*** — is cited in **Cicero De Natura Deorum** I.23.63 and **Diogenes Laertius** IX.51. His paradigm-Athenian-*expulsion* (paradigm ***atheotēs*** trial c. 415 BC) is paradigm-attested. — **Diagoras of Melos** (5th c. BC), paradigm-*atheist-poet* whose paradigm-alleged *atheotēs* trial c. 415 BC is paradigm-attested in Cicero ***De Natura Deorum*** I.2.2 and elsewhere. Standard modern treatment: **Jan Bremmer,** ***"Atheism in Antiquity,"*** in *The Cambridge Companion to Atheism* (2007), pp. 11–26.

⁹⁵ **The paradigm ***duplex-cognitio-Dei*** framework** — the paradigm ***universalis + particularis*** two-fold epistemological framework, aligning with paradigm-Melanchthon's *Loci* on paradigm-*naturalis vs revelata* knowledge of God. This framework — which had paradigm-medieval antecedents in paradigm-Aquinas ***Summa Theologiae*** I.q.2.a.1 (naturaliter innota vs specialiter revelata) — is here paradigm-*Melanchthonianly* re-cast for confessional-Lutheran usage. Standard modern treatment: **Robert D. Preus,** ***The Theology of Post-Reformation Lutheranism*** Vol. II (St. Louis, 1972), pp. 15–105 on the doctrine of God in Lutheran-Orthodoxy. — **Heb. 11:6** — paradigm ***credere-oportet-quia-est-Deus*** *sedes-doctrinae* for the paradigm ***fides-Dei-primordialis*** framework.

⁹⁶ **The paradigm-natural-theology arguments** — Selnecker's paradigm ***cosmological-teleological-consensuological-conscientious*** argument-cluster is the paradigm-Melanchthonian *Loci*-appropriated Ciceronian ***Rom. 1–2*** natural-theology framework. Standard modern treatment: **William J. Wainwright** (ed.), ***The Oxford Handbook of Philosophy of Religion*** (Oxford, 2005), pp. 156–202 on the paradigm-classical arguments. — Selnecker's paradigm-elegiac catena ("Qui negat esse Deum, mox coeli sydera spectet...") is a paradigm ***Selnecker-humanist-versus*** or perhaps paradigm-*Wittenberg-humanist* collection of paradigm-*natural-theology-mnemonic* couplets. — **Psalm 19 + Psalm 104** — the paradigm ***Old-Testament-natural-theology*** *sedes-doctrinae*, cited paradigmatically in patristic, medieval, and Reformation natural-theology treatments. **Job 9** — paradigm-*sapientia-creatoris* locus with paradigm-***Behemoth-Leviathan-cosmological*** framework.

⁹⁷ **The paradigm ***peculiaris-cognitio-Dei*** transition** — Selnecker's paradigm ***universalis-to-particularis*** transition — from paradigm ***natural-theology*** (Ten arguments compressed here) to paradigm ***revealed-Trinitarian-theology*** — anticipates paradigm-Chemnitz's ***Loci Theologici*** on ***De Deo*** and paradigm-Gerhard's ***Loci Theologici*** II *De natura Dei et attributis divinis* + III *De Sancta Trinitate* + IV *De persona et officio Christi.* The paradigm ***fides-Ecclesiae*** entryway into the paradigm-Trinitarian mystery is the paradigm-confessional-Lutheran ***anti-natural-theology-limitation*** — that God the Trinity can be known ***only*** through paradigm ***Scripture + Creed + Church.***

## From Tranche 6 · Loci II–V (representative-sampling)

⁹⁸ **The Definition of God** — Selnecker's paradigm-***5-part definition-of-God*** is a paradigm ***Melanchthonian-Wittenberg-Loci-format*** dogmatic-definition, following the paradigm-**scholastic-*definitio-Dei-per-partes*** framework (essential attributes + relative properties + general effect + spiritual effect + telos). This paradigm-**5-part** definition would be adopted and expanded by Chemnitz (*Loci Theologici*), Hutter (*Compendium locorum theologicorum*), and Gerhard (*Loci Theologici*) as the paradigm ***standard-Lutheran-Orthodox definition-of-God*** framework. Standard modern treatment: **Robert D. Preus,** ***The Theology of Post-Reformation Lutheranism*** Vol. II (see n. 95 above), pp. 15–165 on the paradigm-Lutheran-Orthodox doctrine of God.

⁹⁹ **Augustine's ***"great without quantity, good without quality"*** citation** — from ***De Trinitate*** V.1.2 (PL 42:911–912) and ***Confessiones*** I.4.4 (PL 32:663). The paradigm-***divine-simplicity*** framework in paradigm-scholastic register — that all divine attributes are *identical with the divine essence* (paradigm ***praedicatio identica***), not really-distinct from it — is the paradigm-***Wittenberg-Loci-appropriated Augustinian divine-simplicity*** framework, distinguished from paradigm-Palamite ***distinctio-realis-inter-essentiam-et-operationes*** Byzantine framework.

¹⁰⁰ **The Trinitarian relations** — the paradigm ***Nicene-Constantinopolitan Symbol*** (325/381) framework: Father unbegotten *(agennētos),* Son begotten from Father *(gennētos ek tou Patros),* Spirit proceeding from Father and Son *(ekporeuomenon ek tou Patros — Filioque — kai tou Huiou).* The paradigm ***Filioque*** clause was paradigm-added at paradigm ***Toledo III*** (589) and paradigm ***Frankfurt 794*** and paradigm ***Aachen 809*** synods, and remains the paradigm ***Western-vs-Eastern schism-point.*** Standard modern treatment: **Yves Congar,** ***I Believe in the Holy Spirit*** (New York, 1983, 3 vols.). — The paradigm ***"begotten by the Father's saying or thought"*** (*"dicendo sive cogitatione Patris genita"*) is a paradigm-***Augustinian mental-word*** Trinitarian analogy from ***De Trinitate*** IX–XV (PL 42:960–1098) — the paradigm ***verbum-mentis*** analogy of the eternal generation. Standard modern treatment: **Lewis Ayres,** ***Augustine and the Trinity*** (Cambridge, 2010).

¹⁰¹ **The ***ἀπροσώπως / προσώπως*** distinction** — Selnecker's paradigm-technical Greek terminology: *ἀπροσώπως* (impersonally, absolutely) = considering the divine essence as such; *προσώπως* (personally, relatively) = considering the Persons in their relations. This paradigm-scholastic distinction is grounded in paradigm-***Cappadocian*** (Basil, Gregory of Nazianzus, Gregory of Nyssa) ***ousia-hypostasis*** distinction (paradigm-*mia-ousia-treis-hypostaseis*), adopted by paradigm-Augustine and paradigm-Chalcedon, and paradigm-Melanchthonianly re-formulated for confessional-Lutheran usage. Standard modern treatment: **John Behr,** ***The Nicene Faith*** (Crestwood, NY, 2004, 2 vols.).

¹⁰² **Chapter 4 · De Distinctione** — the paradigm-Trinitarian ***una-essentia-tres-personae*** dogmatic-Locus. Standard modern treatment on the paradigm-Reformation-Trinitarian settlement: **Fred Sanders** and **Klaus Issler** (eds.), ***Jesus in Trinitarian Perspective*** (Nashville, 2007); **Richard A. Muller,** ***Post-Reformation Reformed Dogmatics*** Vol. IV: *The Triunity of God* (Grand Rapids, 2003) — for the paradigm-parallel Reformed development.

¹⁰³ **Anti-Trinitarian Radicals** — the paradigm-mid-16th-century ***anti-Trinitarian Radical-Reformation*** movements against which Selnecker (and the whole Lutheran-Reformed mainstream) directed sustained polemical response. ***Michael Servetus*** (1511–1553), Spanish-French anti-Trinitarian, author of ***De Trinitatis Erroribus*** (1531) and ***Christianismi Restitutio*** (1553); executed at Geneva 1553 under Calvin's leadership. ***Fausto Sozzini*** (Faustus Socinus, 1539–1604), Italian anti-Trinitarian and founder of Polish ***Socinianism*** — his paradigm-post-Selnecker theological framework was systematized in the paradigm ***Racovian Catechism*** (1605). Standard modern treatment: **Roland H. Bainton,** ***Hunted Heretic: The Life and Death of Michael Servetus*** (Boston, 1953); **Sarah Mortimer,** ***Reason and Religion in the English Revolution: The Challenge of Socinianism*** (Cambridge, 2010) on the paradigm-Socinian-tradition.

¹⁰⁴ **De Deo Patre** — the paradigm ***First-Person-of-the-Trinity*** Chapter. The paradigm ***scriptural-catena*** Selnecker adduces (John 10:30 + John 14:16 + John 15:26 + John 17:5 + Rom. 1 + 2 Cor. 13:14 + Gal. 4:6 + Eph. 1–3) is the paradigm-***confessional-Lutheran*** Trinitarian sedes-doctrinae for the Person of the Father — cited paradigmatically in AC I, Ap I, and paradigm-***Athanasian Symbol.***

¹⁰⁵ **The ***non-priority-of-order*** framework** — Selnecker's paradigm-careful distinction that the Father is called ***First Person*** ***not*** by reason of priority-in-time or dignity, but by paradigm-***ordo-cognitionis-naturalis*** — the natural order of our thought about the Trinity, which apprehends the Father as the paradigm-***principium-fontale*** of Trinitarian relations. This paradigm-***Cappadocian-inspired*** framework — that ***monarchia Patris*** is compatible with paradigm ***co-eternal-co-equal Persons*** — is the paradigm ***anti-Arian*** confessional-Lutheran settlement.

¹⁰⁶ **De Deo Filio** — the paradigm-***centre-of-gravity*** Chapter of the whole Locus II. The paradigm-outline compressed here in bracket-summary covers: (1) Scriptural distinctness of the Son; (2) Eternal generation *(gennēsis aeternalis);* (3) Incarnation — Chalcedonian formula (451); (4) *Communicatio idiomatum* — the paradigm-central Lutheran Christological doctrine; (5) *Munus triplex* — Prophet-Priest-King; (6) *Status humiliationis et exaltationis* — Phil. 2:5–11; (7) Saving benefits. Standard modern treatment: **Aloys Grillmeier,** ***Christ in Christian Tradition*** (2nd ed., London, 1975–1996, multi-vol.); **Robert D. Preus,** ***Christ Our Savior*** (St. Louis, 2005).

¹⁰⁷ **The paradigm ***communicatio idiomatum*** three genera framework** — Chemnitz's paradigm-***De duabus naturis*** (1571) framework (see nn. 43 above at Praemonitio §7): (a) *genus idiomaticum* — properties of each nature attributed to the one Person; (b) *genus maiestaticum* — divine attributes communicated to human nature in state of exaltation (paradigm-anti-Reformed *extra-Calvinisticum*); (c) *genus apotelesmaticum* — common operation of both natures. The paradigm ***genus maiestaticum*** was the paradigm-central inner-Reformation Christological dispute, articulated by Chemnitz and codified in **Formula of Concord Article VIII** (*De persona Christi*), aligned with paradigm-Chalcedon against paradigm-Reformed extra-Calvinisticum. Standard modern treatment: **Robert D. Preus,** ***The Theology of Post-Reformation Lutheranism*** Vol. II (see n. 95 above), pp. 176–225 on Chemnitzian Christology; **Björn Slenczka,** ***Das Wesen des Christentums bei Luther und Chemnitz*** (Göttingen, 2005).

¹⁰⁸ **De Spiritu Sancto** — the paradigm-Pneumatological Chapter. Standard modern treatment: **Anthony Thiselton,** ***The Holy Spirit — In Biblical Teaching, through the Centuries, and Today*** (Grand Rapids, 2013); **Michael Welker,** ***God the Spirit*** (Minneapolis, 1994); **Yves Congar,** ***I Believe in the Holy Spirit*** (New York, 1983, 3 vols.); **Denis Edwards,** ***Breath of Life: A Theology of the Creator Spirit*** (Maryknoll, 2004). Selnecker's chapter is a paradigm-***Person + Consubstantiality + Filioque + Two Processions + Eight Causes for Visible Mission + Offices + Modes of Presence + Ministry of Word*** ninefold treatment — the paradigm confessional-Lutheran mature-pneumatology which would be inherited by paradigm ***Chemnitz Loci*** and paradigm ***Gerhard Loci*** VI *De persona et officio Spiritus Sancti.*

¹⁰⁸ᵃ **The paradigm ***"Sol–lux, fons–flumen, ignis–calor" analogy triple*** for Trinitarian procession** — an inheritance from **Tertullian** *Adversus Praxean* 8 (PL 2:163–166) via **Athanasius** *Contra Arianos* III.65 (PG 26:461) and **Basil** *De Spiritu Sancto* 26 (PG 32:181): the paradigm ***three visual analogies*** (sun-and-light, fountain-and-river, fire-and-heat) which paradigm-anti-Arian orthodoxy adduced to distinguish paradigm ***consubstantial procession*** from paradigm ***creaturely production.*** Standard modern treatment: **Lewis Ayres,** ***Nicaea and its Legacy*** (Oxford, 2004), pp. 187–221.

¹⁰⁸ᵇ **The Basilian paradigm ***"ita baptizari-ita credere-ita glorificare"*** formula** — from **Basil the Great,** ***De Spiritu Sancto*** 10.26 (PG 32:113B–116A; SC 17bis:333–337), the paradigm-Cappadocian *lex-orandi-lex-credendi* formulation invoked as the paradigm ***baptismal-formula-anchor*** for consubstantial Trinitarian confession. — The paradigm ***"Metagismos / transaquatio"*** framework — an obscure but attested paradigm-***4th-c. anti-Nicene sub-Sabellian*** modalistic school (Selnecker likely draws from **Epiphanius** *Panarion* 62 on the Sabellians) which held that the Persons are ***transposed*** or ***vessel-in-vessel***-nested rather than really-distinct. Its rebuttal by Basil is paradigm-classical.

¹⁰⁸ᶜ **The paradigm ***triple-Nicene-baptismal-form*** proof** — Selnecker's paradigm-*syllogistic-argument-cluster* for the divinity of the Spirit: (1) triple ***Sanctus*** at Isa. 6:3; (2) baptismal formula Matt. 28:19; (3) *Spirit is Jehovah,* 2 Cor. 3:17; (4) the paradigm-***peccatum-Spiritus-Sancti-non-remittitur*** framework Matt. 12:31–32 which presupposes divinity. This is paradigm-confessional-Lutheran mature-pneumatology, following Melanchthon's *Loci* on the Holy Spirit.

¹⁰⁸ᵈ **The paradigm ***John 16:14–15 as principal Filioque sedes-doctrinae*** framework** — Selnecker's paradigm-***"He shall receive of mine + all things of the Father are mine + He shall not speak of Himself"*** triple as the paradigm-central proof for the ***Filioque*** procession. This follows paradigm ***Augustine De Trinitate XV.26.47*** (PL 42:1094–1095) as the paradigm-mature-Western-Augustinian Filioque proof.

¹⁰⁸ᵉ **The paradigm ***ἐκεῖνος-masculine-grammatical-argument*** for the Spirit's personhood** — Selnecker's paradigm-humanistic ***Greek-grammatical-syntactic*** argument (masculine demonstrative ἐκεῖνος at John 14:26, John 15:26, John 16:8, John 16:13, John 16:14 refers to *ὁ Παράκλητος* rather than to the neuter τὸ Πνεῦμα that immediately precedes) — a paradigm-mature-Reformation-humanistic ***Greek-*NT*-grammatical-proof*** for the personhood of the Holy Spirit. Standard modern treatment: **Daniel B. Wallace,** ***Greek Grammar Beyond the Basics*** (Grand Rapids, 1996), pp. 331–332 on the paradigm-Johannine masculine demonstrative pattern.

¹⁰⁸ᶠ **Augustine ***Tractatus in Johannem*** 99** — from **Augustine** *In Johannis Evangelium Tractatus* 99.7 (PL 35:1889–1890; CCSL 36:585–586) on **John 16:12–15,** the paradigm-***Augustinian-Western-Filioque-locus*** where Augustine articulates the paradigm *Spiritus procedit ab utroque* framework — the paradigm-central proof-text later adduced by paradigm ***Toledo III*** (589) for the Filioque insertion into the Nicaean creed. Standard modern treatment: **Lewis Ayres,** ***Augustine and the Trinity*** (Cambridge, 2010), pp. 251–274.

¹⁰⁸ᵍ **The paradigm ***Greek-schism-thirteen-reunions*** report of Raphael Volaterranus** — **Raphael Volaterranus** (Raffaello Maffei, 1451–1522), Italian humanist and author of ***Commentariorum Urbanorum Libri XXXVIII*** (Rome 1506; Basel 1544), the paradigm-humanistic *encyclopaedic reference-work* of Renaissance-Italy. Selnecker's ***"thirteen times Greek reunion + defection"*** count refers to paradigm-medieval Greek-Latin reunion attempts culminating in the paradigm ***Council of Florence 1439*** *Laetentur Coeli* union-decree (later repudiated by Constantinople). — The paradigm ***Eugenius / Gregory X*** reference points to **Pope Eugene IV** (1431–1447, presiding Florence 1439) and **Pope Gregory X** (1271–1276, presiding Lyons II 1274 which secured the paradigm-first ephemeral Greek-Latin Filioque-union). Standard modern treatment: **Joseph Gill,** ***The Council of Florence*** (Cambridge, 1959); **Deno J. Geanakoplos,** ***Constantinople and the West*** (Madison, 1989).

¹⁰⁸ʰ **Basilian pneumatology — Contra Eunomium III + De Spiritu Sancto** — **Basil the Great,** ***Contra Eunomium*** III.1–7 (PG 29:653–673; SC 305:143–187) refutes **Eunomius of Cyzicus** (paradigm-Neo-Arian Anhomoean, d. c. 393) who had held the Spirit to be a paradigm-***third-nature*** intermediate between Creator and creature. Basil's ***De Spiritu Sancto*** (PG 32:67–218; SC 17bis) — completed c. 375 for Amphilochius of Iconium — is the paradigm-***classic-Cappadocian*** case for the divinity and consubstantiality of the Spirit, the paradigm ***source-text*** for all subsequent orthodox pneumatology.

¹⁰⁸ⁱ **The paradigm ***patristic-Filioque-catena*** of Basil** — Basil's paradigm ***ancient-witnesses*** citation-catena (**Clement of Rome** — *Prima Clementis*, though the Filioque quotation is paradigm-non-authentic; **Irenaeus** — *Adversus Haereses;* **Dionysius of Alexandria** — paradigm-fragmentary; **Eusebius of Palestine** — probably paradigm-***Eusebius of Caesarea*** *De Ecclesiastica Theologia*; **Origen** — *De Principiis* I.3; **Gregory of Neocaesarea Thaumaturgus** — 3rd-c. wonder-worker and *ἄλλος Μωσῆς* per **Basil** *De Spiritu Sancto* 29.74) constitutes a paradigm-*confessional-Lutheran-appropriated patristic-consensus-argument.* Standard modern treatment: **John Behr,** ***The Nicene Faith*** (Crestwood, NY, 2004, 2 vols.).

¹⁰⁸ʲ **Didymus the Blind** — **Didymus of Alexandria** (c. 313–398), blind from childhood, teacher of Jerome and Rufinus, author of ***De Spiritu Sancto*** (surviving only in Jerome's Latin translation, PL 23:105–154), the paradigm-***4th-c. Alexandrian-Nicene*** pneumatological treatise. Condemned posthumously at the paradigm ***Fifth Ecumenical Council*** (553) for Origenism, his pneumatology nevertheless remained influential through the Jerome-Latin translation. Standard modern treatment: **Andrew Louth,** ***The Origins of the Christian Mystical Tradition*** (Oxford, 1981), pp. 60–71.

¹⁰⁸ᵏ **The paradigm ***Fourth Lateran (1215)*** Trinitarian confession** — Selnecker's dating "1204" is a paradigm-*minor* error for **IV Lateran 1215** *Firmiter credimus,* the paradigm-***anti-Joachite*** Trinitarian confession promulgated under **Pope Innocent III** (paradigm-anti-Joachim-of-Fiore's ***tres-hypostases-tres-substantias*** Trinitarian framework). The paradigm ***"Pater a nullo, Filius a Patre solo, Spiritus Sanctus pariter ab utroque"*** formula is the paradigm-***classical-Western-mature-Filioque*** confessional formulation. Standard modern treatment: **Norman P. Tanner,** ***Decrees of the Ecumenical Councils*** (Washington, DC, 1990), Vol. I, pp. 230–233.

¹⁰⁸ˡ **The paradigm ***Spiritus-Christi-in-Prophetis*** framework** — the paradigm ***1 Pet. 1:11*** *(Spiritum Christi in Prophetis testificantem)* + **Isa. 59:21** + **Zech. 7:12** + **Isa. 63:14** *sedes-doctrinae* framework for the paradigm ***continuity-of-Spirit-through-both-Testaments*** — the paradigm-anti-Marcionite / anti-Manichaean framework of paradigm-confessional-Lutheran pneumatology.

¹⁰⁸ᵐ **The paradigm ***Acts 19 Ephesian-twelve-disciples*** framework** — the paradigm ***re-baptism-question*** locus (Acts 19:1–7) which Selnecker uses to distinguish paradigm ***invisible reception of Spirit through faith*** from paradigm ***visible manifestation with tongues and prophecy.*** Standard modern treatment: **Craig S. Keener,** ***Acts: An Exegetical Commentary*** (Grand Rapids, 2014), Vol. III, pp. 2802–2825.

¹⁰⁸ⁿ **The paradigm ***μὲν... δὲ*** Greek-grammatical proof** — Selnecker's paradigm-humanistic ***particulae-connexivae-syntactic-argument*** that the ***μὲν... δὲ*** conjunctions in Acts 19:4 knit Paul's own explanation into one sentence, disentangling the paradigm ***re-baptism***-labyrinth of the paradigm-Anabaptist appropriation. Paradigm-Wittenberg-humanistic Greek-grammatical exegesis at its paradigm-purest.

¹⁰⁸ᵒ **The paradigm ***processio-aeterna-vs-temporalis + missio-visibilis-vs-invisibilis*** framework** — Selnecker's paradigm ***two-fold procession + two-fold mission*** framework, following paradigm ***Augustine De Trinitate II.5.9 + IV.20.28*** (PL 42:849–853; 908–909) and paradigm ***Aquinas Summa Theologiae*** I.q.43 *De missione divinarum Personarum*.

¹⁰⁸ᵖ **The paradigm ***octuple-cause-visible-mission*** framework** — Selnecker's paradigm-***eight-causes-for-the-visible-outpouring-of-the-Spirit*** analytical-schema, expanding paradigm ***Augustine De Trinitate*** IV.20 and paradigm ***Chemnitz Loci de Spiritu Sancto*** into a paradigm-confessional-Lutheran-mature-scholastic-*causal-analytical-format.* Cross-reference to paradigm ***Chemnitz-Enchiridion 1593*** pneumatology-locus.

¹⁰⁸ᑫ **The paradigm ***substantialiter-vs-virtualiter*** debate** — the paradigm-mature-Lutheran anti-Reformed / anti-Enthusiastic controversy over the ***mode*** of the Spirit's indwelling. Selnecker's paradigm-***"substantialiter, non tantum quoad dona"*** framework — that the Spirit is really and substantially in believers, not merely virtually through gifts — aligns with paradigm ***FC SD III De Iustificatione*** and paradigm-*Andreas-Osiander*-adjacent debates on the ***indwelling of Christ.*** Standard modern treatment: **Simo Peura,** ***Mehr als ein Mensch*** (Mainz, 1994) on the paradigm-*Finnish-Luther-School unio-mystica* interpretation; **Robert D. Preus,** ***Justification and Rome*** (St. Louis, 1997).

¹⁰⁸ʳ **The paradigm ***quadruple/quintuple modes-of-God's-presence*** framework** — Selnecker's paradigm-mature-Lutheran ***scholastic-*modi-praesentiae-Dei*** framework: (1) universal Trinitarian presence; (2) inseparable presence in Angels and blessed; (3) separable presence in the reborn through faith; (4) hypostatic-union presence in Christ; (5) sacramental presence in the Supper. This paradigm-*Chemnitz-inherited* framework prefigures paradigm ***Gerhard Loci*** and paradigm ***Quenstedt Theologia Didactico-Polemica*** systematic-scholastic-elaborations. The paradigm ***καθ' ὑπόστασιν*** reference to Justin is paradigm ***Justin Martyr Apologia I.32*** or **Dialogus cum Tryphone** (PG 6:459–470).

¹⁰⁸ˢ **The paradigm ***"Arrha docet, renovat, iuvat, obsignat"*** mnemonic verse** — Selnecker's paradigm-*Wittenberg-catechetical-mnemonic* for the paradigm-***quintuple offices*** of the Holy Spirit: (1) earnest/pledge; (2) teaching; (3) renewal; (4) aid; (5) sealing. Cross-reference to paradigm ***Melanchthon Loci de Spiritu Sancto*** which develops the paradigm-similar ***officia-Spiritus-Sancti-scholia*** framework. — The paradigm ***unctio in reges et sacerdotes*** framework is the paradigm-mature-Lutheran ***priesthood-of-all-believers*** locus (paradigm-Luther *De captivitate Babylonica* 1520; paradigm-1 Peter 2:9). — The paradigm-***quadruple-aid*** framework (testando, donando, consolando, precando) is a paradigm-mature-scholastic-refinement of the paradigm-***auxilium-Spiritus-Sancti*** motif.

¹⁰⁸ᵗ **The paradigm ***obsignatio-Spiritus + eiicere-Spiritum*** framework** — Selnecker's paradigm ***Eph. 1:13–14 + 4:30*** *sedes-doctrinae* framework for the paradigm ***seal-of-the-Spirit*** and its paradigm-***peccata-contra-conscientiam-eijciunt-Spiritum*** rebuttal. Paradigm-Reformation-standard pastoral-theology, cross-referenced to paradigm ***FC SD IV De bonis operibus + XI De praedestinatione et electione***. — The paradigm ***"turpius eiicitur quam non admittitur hospes"*** proverb — a paradigm-classical Latin *sententia* (paradigm-*Ovid-Tristia-adjacent*), naturalised in paradigm-humanist-Renaissance moral-catechetical literature.

¹⁰⁸ᵘ **The paradigm ***anti-Anabaptist-Enthusiasm*** framework** — Selnecker's paradigm ***anti-Radikal-Reformation*** framework, targeting paradigm ***Thomas Müntzer*** (paradigm ***Von dem gedichteten Glauben*** 1524, WA 15:210–221), paradigm ***Andreas Karlstadt*** (paradigm ***Bilderrede-Traktat*** 1522), paradigm ***Zwickau Prophets*** (paradigm 1521 Wittenberg agitation), paradigm ***Melchior Hoffman*** (paradigm ***Ordonantie Godts*** 1530s), paradigm ***Jan van Leiden*** (paradigm ***Münster*** 1534–1535), and paradigm ***Kaspar Schwenckfeld*** (paradigm ***Von der himmlischen Speise*** 1523+) — all paradigm-mid-16th-c. paradigm ***inner-Word-vs-outer-Word*** frameworks against which Selnecker (with the whole Lutheran mainstream) defended the paradigm ***verbum-vocale-et-scriptum + sacramenta*** as the paradigm-*ordinata-media-gratiae.* Standard modern treatment: **George H. Williams,** ***The Radical Reformation*** (3rd ed., Kirksville, 1992). — The paradigm ***AC V + FC SD II*** *sedes-doctrinae* explicitly rejects paradigm ***enthusiastae*** by name.

¹⁰⁸ᵛ **The paradigm ***Vestal-virgin-Enthusiast*** satirical verse** — Selnecker's paradigm-***15-couplet-satirical-verse*** against paradigm-Anabaptist-*Enthusiast* ***visionary-crown-dream*** frameworks. Its authorship is likely paradigm-Selnecker himself, or a paradigm-Wittenberg-humanist-colleague (perhaps **D. Wolf,** cf. n. 74 above at Locus I). The paradigm ***"vaccinum stercus"*** (cow-dung) *punchline* is a paradigm-Erasmian-satirical *lex-inversa* — the ***paradigm-supposed-crown-of-the-Spirit turns out to be excrement.*** Paradigm-Reformation ***anti-Schwärmer-satirical-verse*** genre.

¹⁰⁸ʷ **The paradigm ***wind-and-fire-similitude*** framework** — the paradigm-Pentecost **Acts 2:2–4** *sedes-doctrinae* for the paradigm-mature-Lutheran-***two-fold-similitude*** framework: (1) ***wind + breath*** ↔ ***Word + Spirit;*** (2) ***fire consuming dross*** ↔ ***Law uncovering + Gospel healing.*** Paradigm-Lutheran-***Law-and-Gospel*** framework applied to pneumatology.

¹⁰⁸ˣ **The paradigm ***"Spiritus alme, Patris, Nati" prayer-verse*** closing** — Selnecker's paradigm-***devotional-hymnic-close*** to the Locus, an eleven-line paradigm-Latin-hexameter *prayer-verse* to the Holy Spirit. Its authorship is likely paradigm-Selnecker himself (given his paradigm-***hymnographer*** vocation, cf. biographical sketch above and paradigm ***"Ach bleib bei uns, Herr Jesu Christ"*** EG 347). The paradigm ***"Te sine nil sumus / Pertecuncta sumus"*** couplet is paradigm-***Augustinian-negativum-affirmativum*** hymnic contrast. Reference to paradigm ***Isa. 63:11–14*** *(qui traduxit Mosen per mare rubrum ductu Spiritus sui)* as the paradigm-*sedes-doctrinae* for the paradigm-***Spirit-as-guide-through-perils*** framework.

¹⁰⁹ **The paradigm-anti-Macedonian-Pneumatological settlement** — Selnecker's paradigm ***Person + Divinity + Filioque-procession + Work*** four-fold treatment reflects paradigm-Nicene-Constantinopolitan (381) Pneumatology as appropriated by the paradigm-Western tradition. The paradigm ***Isa. 11:2 seven-fold gifts*** — Wisdom, Understanding, Counsel, Might, Knowledge, Piety, Fear of the Lord — belongs to the paradigm-***septem dona Spiritus Sancti*** tradition, paradigmatic in Catholic-and-Lutheran devotional catechesis. — **1 Cor. 12 charismata** — paradigm-Pauline ***varieties of gifts*** framework.

¹¹⁰ **Locus III De Creatione** — Selnecker's paradigm-creation-Locus. Standard modern treatment: **Andrew Louth,** ***The Origins of the Christian Mystical Tradition*** (Oxford, 1981), on paradigm-Cappadocian creation-theology; **Jaroslav Pelikan,** ***Christianity and Classical Culture*** (New Haven, 1993) on Cappadocian appropriation of Greek philosophy; **Colin E. Gunton,** ***The Triune Creator: A Historical and Systematic Study*** (Grand Rapids, 1998); **Peter C. Bouteneff,** ***Beginnings: Ancient Christian Readings of the Biblical Creation Narratives*** (Grand Rapids, 2008). Selnecker's Locus follows the paradigm-Melanchthonian ***brevem-commonefactionem*** register — deferring the substantive-exegetical labour to Moses himself + Ps. 104 + 136 + Basil's paradigm ***Hexaemeron*** — while supplying the paradigm-***scholastic-*definitio-causae-species-consideratio-fines*** framework that all subsequent paradigm-Lutheran-Orthodox creation-doctrine (Chemnitz, Hutter, Gerhard, Quenstedt) would inherit.

¹¹⁰ᵃ **The paradigm ***creatio-definitio-in-5-partes*** framework** — Selnecker's paradigm-***5-part*** creation-definition (external action + Trinity + ex nihilo + goodness/wisdom + Church-*finis*) is a paradigm-***Wittenberg-Melanchthonian-Loci-scholastic*** *definitio* — foundational for all subsequent paradigm-Lutheran-Orthodox systematic-creation-doctrine. Standard modern treatment: **Robert D. Preus,** ***The Theology of Post-Reformation Lutheranism*** Vol. II (St. Louis, 1972), pp. 165–226 on paradigm-Lutheran-Orthodox creation-doctrine.

¹¹⁰ᵇ **The paradigm ***Filioque-appropriated-in-creation*** framework** — Selnecker's paradigm-***"first-Person + first-work"*** appropriation follows paradigm-Augustinian ***works-ad-extra-common-but-Person-appropriated*** rule (cf. n. 102 above at Chapter 4). Paradigm-parallel appropriations: ***Son / redemption,*** ***Spirit / sanctification.*** Standard modern treatment: **Lewis Ayres,** ***Augustine and the Trinity*** (Cambridge, 2010), pp. 210–250.

¹¹⁰ᶜ **The paradigm ***nine-sphere-Ptolemaic-cosmology*** framework** — Selnecker's paradigm-***primum-mobile + firmamentum + septem-planetae*** enumeration is the paradigm-***Ptolemaic-geocentric-Aratean*** cosmology (paradigm-***Aratus of Soli,*** *Phaenomena* c. 270 BC — cited by Paul at Acts 17:28 — the paradigm ***classical-Hellenistic astronomical didactic-poem***). Selnecker writes 33 years after paradigm ***Copernicus De Revolutionibus 1543*** but before paradigm ***Galileo 1610*** — so the paradigm-Ptolemaic cosmology remains normative for paradigm-***confessional-Reformation-humanistic-scholarship.*** Standard modern treatment: **John North,** ***Cosmos: An Illustrated History of Astronomy and Cosmology*** (Chicago, 2008), pp. 209–282 on paradigm-late-Renaissance transition. — The paradigm ***creatura-definitio*** framework echoes paradigm ***Aquinas Summa Contra Gentiles II*** *De rebus creatis* and paradigm ***Melanchthon Loci de creatione*** *(CR 21).*

¹¹⁰ᵈ **The paradigm-***classical-humanistic-triple-citation of Ovid-Cicero-Silius*** on man's uprightness** — paradigm-***Wittenberg-humanistic-Melanchthonian*** framework of citing paradigm-***classical Gentile-anthropological witnesses*** to the paradigm-***biblical-imago-Dei*** doctrine. **Ovid** *Metamorphoses* I.76–86 (paradigm-***"os homini sublime"***); **Cicero** *De Legibus* I.9.26; **Silius Italicus** *Punica* XV.84–87. Selnecker follows paradigm-Melanchthon's paradigm ***classical-humanistic-*proof-text*-marshalling*** as illustrated throughout *Loci Communes* and paradigm ***Orationes.***

¹¹⁰ᵉ **The paradigm ***quadruple-gradus-viventium*** (four-grade hierarchy) framework** — the paradigm-***Aristotelian-*De Anima* + *Historia Animalium*** paradigm-***scholastic-Christianised*** framework: (1) inanimate/existing; (2) vegetative-soul plants; (3) sensitive-soul brutes; (4) intellective-soul man. Paradigm-Aquinas ***Summa Theologiae*** I.q.78 *De potentiis animae*. Selnecker's paradigm-***Melanchthonian-humanistic*** appropriation places man as ***paradigm-***finis-omnium-inferiorum*** who himself is ***paradigm-*servitor-Dei-solius.****

¹¹⁰ᶠ **The paradigm ***quadruple-admonitio-ex-ordine-hominis*** framework** — Selnecker's paradigm-***moral-theological*** deduction from the paradigm-*ordo-creaturarum* framework: (1) man's excellence; (2) *scopus-actionum* = glory of God + thanksgiving; (3) perversitas-miseriae-hominis (echoing paradigm ***Ps 8*** + paradigm ***Augustinian-Anselmian curvatus-in-se*** framework); (4) *quaerendus-Mediator-in-natura-nostra* — the paradigm-***anthropological transition to Christology.*** This paradigm-***moral-anthropological-Christological-move*** anticipates paradigm ***Chemnitz Loci de imagine Dei + de peccato originali*** framework.

¹¹⁰ᵍ **Bernard's paradigm ***septuple-consideratio*** framework** — **Bernard of Clairvaux** (1090–1153), paradigm-***Cistercian-mystical-monastic*** giant, whose paradigm-***septem-considerationes*** on the benefits of creation is likely drawn from **Bernard** ***Sermones in Cantica Canticorum*** (PL 183:785–1198) or paradigm ***De diligendo Deo*** (PL 182:973–1000). Bernard is paradigm-Selnecker's paradigm-***medieval-mystical-preferred-witness*** — cited approvingly throughout, as also in paradigm-Luther's paradigm ***Bernard-appreciation*** framework (paradigm-*fides ad extra* passages, WA 30/3:329). Standard modern treatment: **Bernard McGinn,** ***The Growth of Mysticism*** (New York, 1994), pp. 158–224 on paradigm-*Bernardine-mysticism*.

¹¹⁰ʰ **The paradigm ***creatio-ex-nihilo*** framework** — the paradigm-***confessional-Christian creation-doctrine*** framework: **Ps. 33:9** *(ipse dixit et facta sunt)* + **Ecclesiasticus 18:1** *(qui vivit in aeternum creavit omnia simul)* as *sedes-doctrinae.* Paradigm ***Heb. 11:3*** *(τὰ βλεπόμενα ἐκ μὴ φαινομένων γεγονέναι)* is the paradigm-***chief NT sedes-doctrinae*** (mentioned at note 111 below). Standard modern treatment: **Gerhard May,** ***Creatio ex Nihilo: The Doctrine of "Creation Out of Nothing" in Early Christian Thought*** (Edinburgh, 1994).

¹¹⁰ⁱ **The paradigm ***classical-Greek-philosophers-on-first-principles*** doxographical catalogue** — Selnecker's paradigm-***Wittenberg-Melanchthonian-humanistic doxography*** of Presocratic and Platonic-Aristotelian first-principles debates. Sources: (1) **Heraclitus of Ephesus** (c. 535–475 BC) — paradigm ***fire-primary-element,*** DK B fragments; (2) **Diogenes of Apollonia** (5th c. BC) — paradigm ***air-primary-element***; (3) **Thales of Miletus** (c. 624–546 BC) — paradigm ***water-primary-element***; (4) **Democritus of Abdera** (c. 460–370 BC) — paradigm ***atoms-in-the-void*** (also **Leucippus**); (5) **Empedocles of Acragas** (c. 495–435 BC) — paradigm ***four-elements + Love/Strife***; (6) **Zeno of Citium** (c. 334–262 BC) + Stoics — paradigm ***God/Mind + Nature/Matter*** dyad; (7) **Hesiod** *Theogony* + **Ovid** *Metamorphoses* I — paradigm ***God + Chaos*** dyad; (8) **Plato** *Timaeus* + *Phaedo* — paradigm ***God + Matter + Idea*** triad. Selnecker's paradigm-***anno-mundi*** dating framework is paradigm-***Melanchthonian-Chronicon-Carion-appropriated*** (*Chronicon Carionis* 1532, ed. Melanchthon; expanded by Peucer). Standard modern treatment: **G. S. Kirk, J. E. Raven, M. Schofield,** ***The Presocratic Philosophers*** (Cambridge, 1983, 2nd ed.). — **Guillaume Budé** (1467–1540), French humanist and paradigm-Renaissance-Hellenist, cited for his paradigm-***Platonic-Christianising*** reading of the paradigm ***Epinomis 986C-D*** *Logos-passage* as a proto-***Logos-doctrine*** — a paradigm-common ***Renaissance-humanist prisca-theologia*** interpretative framework.

¹¹⁰ʲ **Justin Martyr on Plato's dependence on Moses** — from **Justin Martyr,** ***Cohortatio ad Graecos*** (also called ***Oratio ad Gentiles Exhortatoria***, PG 6:241–312 — possibly not by Justin himself but paradigm-3rd-c. Justin-adjacent apologetic-work). The paradigm ***Plato-borrowed-from-Moses-in-Egypt*** framework is a paradigm ***early-Christian apologetic-humanistic*** topos (also in Clement of Alexandria's ***Stromata*** I; and paradigm ***Aristobulus of Alexandria*** fragments) — paradigm-***Renaissance-humanistic revived*** through Marsilio Ficino's paradigm-***Prisca Theologia*** framework. Selnecker's paradigm-***Justin-appropriated*** version follows paradigm-***Wittenberg-humanistic-Melanchthonian*** convention. Standard modern treatment: **Sara Ahbel-Rappe,** ***Reading Neoplatonism*** (Cambridge, 2000); **Christoph Riedweg,** ***Ps.-Justin (Markell von Ankyra?), Ad Graecos de Vera Religione*** (Basel, 1994).

¹¹⁰ᵏ **The paradigm ***creatio-vs-generatio-vs-annihilatio*** scholastic distinction** — the paradigm-***Aquinas-Bonaventura-Scotus*** mature-scholastic *distinctio* between: (1) ***creatio*** — action of God alone, from ***non-being to being***, no pre-existing matter; (2) ***generatio physica*** — motion within already-created nature, from ***not-such-being to such-being***; (3) ***annihilatio*** — reverse of creation, from ***being to nothing***, work of God alone. Paradigm ***Aquinas Summa Theologiae*** I.q.45 *De modo emanationis rerum a primo principio*. Paradigm-***Reformation-mainstream*** retention of paradigm-scholastic-*distinctio-clarification* even as paradigm-scholastic-*theological-syncretism* was polemically rejected.

¹¹⁰ˡ **The paradigm ***Epiphanius contra Aetium*** citation** — **Epiphanius of Salamis** (c. 315–403), *Panarion* Book III, Haer. 76 *Adversus Aetianos* (PG 42:517–635) — the paradigm-4th-c. anti-***Aetius the Anhomoean*** framework (a paradigm-Neo-Arian more extreme even than Eunomius). Selnecker's citation of paradigm-Epiphanian ***ratiocinationes-hominum-miserae-sunt*** framework is a paradigm-***anti-speculative*** counter to paradigm-***curiosity-questions*** about creation. — **The paradigm ***Augustinian-*infernum-fabricat-curiosis*** anecdote** — from ***Confessiones*** XI.12.14 (PL 32:815) and ***De Genesi ad Litteram*** V.5.12 (PL 34:325). Paradigm-***classical apophatic-response*** to paradigm-speculative-cosmological curiosity.

¹¹⁰ᵐ **The paradigm ***aeternitas / aevum / tempus*** scholastic distinction** — the paradigm-***Boethian-Aquinas mature-scholastic*** *distinctio* between: (1) ***aeternitas*** — God alone, no beginning no end; (2) ***aevum*** — Angels and souls, beginning but no end; (3) ***tempus*** — corporeal things, beginning and end. Paradigm ***Boethius Consolatio Philosophiae*** V.6 + paradigm ***Aquinas Summa Theologiae*** I.q.10 *De Dei aeternitate*. Selnecker's paradigm-***Wittenberg-Kreis*** appropriation retains the paradigm-scholastic-***distinctio*** while cleansing it of paradigm-scholastic-***syncretism.***

¹¹⁰ⁿ **The paradigm ***two-Aristotelian-arguments-refutation*** framework** — Selnecker's paradigm-***scholastic-refutation*** of Aristotle's paradigm ***causa-perfectissima-non-est-otiosa*** + paradigm ***motuum-caelestium-perpetuitas*** arguments for the world's eternity. The paradigm-***voluntarist counter*** (God acts freely, not by natural necessity) is paradigm-***confessional-Reformation-appropriated Scotist-voluntarism.*** Standard modern treatment: **Richard A. Muller,** ***Post-Reformation Reformed Dogmatics*** Vol. III: *The Divine Essence and Attributes* (Grand Rapids, 2003), pp. 227–298 on paradigm-Reformation-voluntarism.

¹¹⁰ᵒ **The paradigm ***anno-mundi 5533 / annus-Christi-1571*** dating** — Selnecker's ***"in this year of Christ's birth 1571 = anno mundi 5533"*** dating implies a **1571-composition-date** for this section — 8 years before the 1579-imprint, suggesting the *Institutio* was composed and revised over roughly a decade. — The paradigm ***Elias-prophecy of 2000-in-vain + 2000-under-Law + 2000-Messianic*** *sedes-doctrinae* is a paradigm-***Talmudic-Sanhedrin 97a*** framework, paradigm-***Reformation-appropriated*** for eschatological-*imminentia-Domini* register. — The paradigm ***Septuagint-Meah/Meoth error*** framework — paradigm-Jerome ***Quaestiones Hebraicae in Genesim*** (PL 23:983–1062) and paradigm ***Chronicon of Eusebius*** (paradigm-4th-c. Eusebius-Jerome-Latin *Chronicon universale*). Standard modern treatment: **Anthony Grafton,** ***Joseph Scaliger*** (Oxford, 1983–1993, 2 vols.) on paradigm-Reformation-humanistic chronology.

¹¹⁰ᵖ **The paradigm ***"Read Moses + Ps 104 + 136 + Basil's Hexaemeron"*** closing recommendation** — Selnecker's paradigm-***self-conscious-deferral*** to paradigm-classical patristic-and-biblical creation-treatments. **Basil the Great** (c. 330–379), Cappadocian Father whose ***Homiliae in Hexaemeron*** (nine homilies, PG 29:3–208; SC 26) is the paradigm ***patristic-golden-standard*** on creation-doctrine, cited paradigmatically throughout Renaissance-Reformation creation-treatments. Standard modern treatment: **Frances Young,** ***Biblical Exegesis and the Formation of Christian Culture*** (Cambridge, 1997), pp. 213–248 on paradigm-Cappadocian *Hexaemeron*-tradition; **Andrew Louth,** ***The Origins of the Christian Mystical Tradition*** (Oxford, 1981), pp. 88–113.

¹¹¹ **The paradigm ***ex-nihilo-anti-Aristotelian-anti-Manichaean*** creation-framework** — the paradigm ***confessional-Christian*** creation-doctrine against paradigm-***Aristotelian*** ***materia-aeterna*** and paradigm-***Manichaean*** ***two-eternal-principles*** frameworks. Paradigm ***Heb. 11:3*** (*τὰ βλεπόμενα ἐκ μὴ φαινομένων*) is the paradigm-*sedes-doctrinae* for *creatio ex nihilo.* — The paradigm ***concursus divinus*** framework — that God ***upholds*** all things continuously — was developed by paradigm ***mature-Lutheran-Orthodoxy*** as a paradigm ***three-fold concurrence*** (Gerhard, Quenstedt): ***concursus generalis, concursus specialis, concursus liberi arbitrii.*** Standard modern treatment: **Robert D. Preus,** ***The Theology of Post-Reformation Lutheranism*** Vol. II (see n. 95 above), pp. 165–226.

¹¹² **Locus IV De Angelis** — the paradigm ***Angelological-Locus.*** Selnecker's paradigm-treatment is refreshingly ***sober*** relative to paradigm-scholastic-Angelological tradition: he explicitly rejects paradigm-***curiosity-questions*** ("how many, when created, of what substance") that paradigm-medieval scholastic-treatments elaborated at length (paradigm-Aquinas ***Summa Theologiae*** I.qq. 50–64), and confines himself to paradigm-scripturally-warranted claims. Standard modern treatment: **Peter Kreeft,** ***Angels and Demons: What Do We Really Know About Them?*** (San Francisco, 1995) — for the paradigm-Catholic mature-Thomistic angelology; **Susan Garrett,** ***No Ordinary Angel*** (New Haven, 2008) for paradigm-modern-critical historical treatments.

¹¹³ **The pseudo-Dionysian Celestial Hierarchy** — the paradigm ***nine-orders angelic-hierarchy*** framework: Seraphim, Cherubim, Thronoi, Kyriotētes, Dynameis, Exousiai, Archai, Archangeloi, Angeloi — set forth in ***De Coelesti Hierarchia*** (attributed to Dionysius the Areopagite; probably a paradigm-late-5th-c. Syrian Neoplatonist, PG 3:120–370). The paradigm-*pseudo-Dionysian corpus* was highly-influential throughout the medieval-scholastic tradition (Aquinas, Bonaventura), but paradigm-critical-humanistic scholarship since Erasmus (1504) and Lorenzo Valla (1439) had established the paradigm-non-apostolic-non-Dionysian authorship. Selnecker's paradigm-Reformation-humanistic dismissal (paradigm ***"olim monachi... obtrudere simplicioribus voluerunt"***) reflects paradigm-mature-Wittenberg pseudo-Dionysian criticism. Standard modern treatment: **Paul Rorem,** ***Pseudo-Dionysius: A Commentary*** (Oxford, 1993). — The paradigm ***three-estates × three-angels*** medieval-scholastic pastoral-adaptation reflects paradigm-Aquinas ***Summa Theologiae*** I.q.113 application-theology.

¹¹³ᵃ **The paradigm ***Michael-Raphael-Gabriel-Uriel*** four-Archangel naming framework** — the paradigm-***biblical + apocryphal*** enumeration of the four named Angels: **Michael** (Dan. 10, 12; Rev. 12; Jude 9) — paradigm-classical Christological identification with the ***Angel of the Covenant*** (Malachi 3), a paradigm ***Christophany-in-OT*** reading; **Raphael** (Tobit 3, 5, 12) — paradigm-Deuterocanonical; **Gabriel** (Dan. 8, 9; Luke 1) — paradigm-canonical; **Uriel** (4 Esdras 4:1) — paradigm-Pseudepigraphal-appropriated.
